### 1.5 Core (`exchangeserver.core`)

| Type | Shape | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `Request<R>` (sealed) | `Submit`, `Cancel`, `CancelAll` (become commands); `Snapshot`, `Attach`, `Resend` (reads) | what a caller hands in; R is the result type |
| `Pending<R>` | request, its own `CompletableFuture`, the stamped command, `refused` | one per request, never shared |
| `PrivateEvent` (sealed) | `OrderReply(participant, oseq, side, quantity, response)`, `CancelReply(participant, oseq, result, byCancelAll)`, `MakerFill(participant, oseq, execution)` | one numbered message of a participant's private stream (D73) |
| `ResendResult` | events, firstAvailable, lastOseq | the answer to RESEND |
| `Ledger` | engine + `OutputDigest` + `OutputLog` | rebuilt as a unit (replay, poison rebuild) |
| `Snapshot` | lastSequence, lastOrderId, lastTimestamp, lastTradePrice, digest, orders, lastOseqs | the state after `lastSequence` (D71) |
| `Exchange.Recovery` | base, lastSequence, replayed, truncatedBytes, quarantined, adoptedRulesFrom | what startup did (L1) |

**[Verified]** `src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Request.java:21-56`, `src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/PrivateEvent.java:15-31`, `src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Snapshot.java:41-42`, `src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Exchange.java:78-80`.

### 1.6 Gateway (`exchangeserver.gateway`)

- `ClientMessage` (sealed): `Login(participant, secret, cancelOnDisconnect)` (its `toString` hides the secret), `NewOrder`, `Cancel`, `Resend`, `Subscribe`, `Unsubscribe`, `Ping`, `Quit`. Syntax only.
- `Credentials`: participant → (iterations, salt, PBKDF2 hash).
- `TcpGateway.Settings`: bind address, port, credentials, TLS context, session limits, timeouts, flow control.

**[Verified]** `src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/ClientMessage.java:9-40`, `src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/Credentials.java:34-48`.

### 1.7 Class diagram (the main relations)

```mermaid
classDiagram
    class OrderRequest
    class Order
    class Command {
      <<sealed>>
      sequenceNumber
      timestamp
    }
    class NewOrderCommand
    class CancelOrderCommand
    class CancelAllCommand
    class SkipCommand
    class CheckCommand
    Command <|-- NewOrderCommand
    Command <|-- CancelOrderCommand
    Command <|-- CancelAllCommand
    Command <|-- SkipCommand
    Command <|-- CheckCommand
    NewOrderCommand --> OrderRequest
    Order ..> OrderRequest : built from
    class OrderBook
    class PriceLevel
    class OrderNode
    OrderBook "1" --> "*" PriceLevel : bids, asks
    PriceLevel "1" --> "*" OrderNode
    OrderNode --> Order
    class MatchingEngine
    MatchingEngine --> OrderBook
    class EngineOutput {
      <<sealed>>
    }
    class Ledger
    class OutputDigest
    class OutputLog
    Ledger --> MatchingEngine
    Ledger --> OutputDigest
    Ledger --> OutputLog
    OutputLog --> PrivateEvent : numbers
    class Exchange
    Exchange --> Ledger
    Exchange --> CommandJournal
    Exchange --> Sequencer
    class Snapshot
    Ledger ..> Snapshot : from / to
```

---

## 2. The journal file (format v3, D69)

```
HEADER,3,<rulesVersion>,<baseSequence>,<crc>
NEW,<seq>,<timestamp>,<orderId>,<participant>,<clientOrderId>,<side>,<type>,<quantity>,<price or ->,<crc>
CANCEL,<seq>,<timestamp>,<participant>,<targetOrderId>,<crc>
CANCEL_ALL,<seq>,<timestamp>,<participant>,<crc>
SKIP,<seq>,<timestamp>,<skippedSeq>,<crc>
CHECK,<seq>,<timestamp>,<outputDigest>,<crc>
```

**[Verified]** `src/main/java/exchangeserver/journal/JournalCodec.java:18-35`.

- Every record ends with `,<crc>\n`, where `<crc>` is the CRC-32C of the text before that comma, as 8 lower-case hex digits. A changed character makes the record corrupt; startup stops with the record number (D69). **[Verified]** `JournalCodec.java:166-187`.
- Records are LF-only. A record ending in `\r` (a CRLF conversion by an editor or git) is reported as such; `.gitattributes` keeps `*.journal` and its siblings out of git's line-ending conversion. **[Verified]** `JournalCodec.java:172-175`.
- `baseSequence` names the snapshot the journal continues from (0: none); the first record is `baseSequence + 1` (D71).
- Timestamps are `Instant.toString()`; a MARKET order's price is `-`.

A real journal (2026-10-27): **[Verified]** live run.

```
HEADER,3,2,0,5d7826e0
NEW,1,2026-10-27T14:20:40.373493200Z,1,alice,a1,SELL,LIMIT,10,100,379beca7
NEW,2,2026-10-27T14:20:40.802670Z,2,alice,a2,BUY,LIMIT,5,100,f33577be
NEW,3,2026-10-27T14:20:41.217763100Z,3,bob,b1,BUY,LIMIT,4,100,bed0c339
CANCEL_ALL,4,2026-10-27T14:20:42.045241200Z,alice,2b6bb4a5
```

Neighbours of the journal: `exchange.journal.lock` (empty; holds the OS lock while a process has the journal open, D65), `exchange.journal.snapshot-<seq>` and `exchange.journal.archive-<first>-<last>` (after compactions).

## 3. The snapshot file (D71)

```
SNAPSHOT,1,<lastSequence>,<lastOrderId>,<lastTimestamp or ->,<lastTradePrice>,<digest>,<crc>
ORDER,<orderId>,<participant>,<clientOrderId>,<side>,<type>,<price>,<originalQty>,<filledQty>,<timestamp>,<crc>
OSEQ,<participant>,<lastOseq>,<crc>
END,<orderCount>,<participantCount>,<crc>
```

**[Verified]** `src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Snapshot.java:27-40`, `Snapshot.java:64-77`.

- ORDER lines are in book priority (bids best first, then asks best first, time priority within a level), so loading them in order restores time priority; partial fills are kept (`filledQty`).
- OSEQ lines are sorted by participant, so the file is the same bytes whatever the hash map's iteration order.
- END proves the file is complete; it is written to a temporary file, fsynced and renamed atomically.
- The snapshot depends on no rule: it is state, not commands to re-run. That is what lets a rules change be crossed.

A real snapshot (no resting orders left, two participants): **[Verified]** live run.

```
SNAPSHOT,1,4,3,2026-10-27T14:20:42.045241200Z,100,88a18b6a6365ebe4,d7e6a970
OSEQ,alice,4,d1453b0b
OSEQ,bob,1,3d136969
END,0,2,6a6d3c70
```

## 4. The users file (D74)

```
<participant>:pbkdf2-sha256:<iterations>:<salt base64>:<hash base64>
```

One line per participant, written atomically by `--add-user` (which asks for the secret, or reads one line from standard input, dropping a UTF-8 byte-order mark that Windows PowerShell adds). The secret itself is never stored: PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 with a random 16-byte salt and 210,000 iterations. Lines starting with `#` are comments. **[Verified]** `src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/Credentials.java:18-31`, `Credentials.java:72-80`, `src/main/java/exchangeserver/Main.java:212-232`; seen live: `alice:pbkdf2-sha256:210000:gJDXXBPuB1OYmFalrdCSx...`.

A secret is 8 to 64 printable ASCII characters without spaces (the protocol separates fields with spaces). **[Verified]** `Credentials.java:38`.

---

## 5. The wire protocol

ASCII lines ending in `\n` (`\r\n` accepted), at most 256 bytes, tokens separated by single spaces (D53). **[Verified]** `src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/Protocol.java:18-46`.

### Client → server

| Line | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `LOGIN <participant> <secret> [COD]` | authenticate; `COD` = cancel my orders when my last session closes |
| `NEW <clOrdId> <BUY\|SELL> <LIMIT\|IOC\|FOK\|POST_ONLY> <qty> <price>` | priced order |
| `NEW <clOrdId> <BUY\|SELL> MARKET <qty>` | market order |
| `CANCEL <orderId>` | cancel own order |
| `RESEND <oseq>` | repeat retained private events from oseq on |
| `SUBSCRIBE`, `UNSUBSCRIBE`, `PING`, `QUIT` | market data, keep-alive, leave |

### Server → client

| Line | Stream |
|---|---|
| `FILL oseq= orderId= clOrdId= side= price= qty= leaves= liquidity=TAKER\|MAKER` | private, numbered |
| `ORDER oseq= orderId= clOrdId= status= filled= remaining= [reason=]` | private, numbered |
| `CANCELLED oseq= orderId= qty= [reason=CANCEL_ALL]`, `CANCEL_REJECT oseq= orderId= reason=UNKNOWN_ORDER` | private, numbered |
| `… resend=1` | a private line repeated by RESEND |
| `WELCOME participant= oseq=`, `RESENT last=`, `ERROR RESEND_GAP first=` | session |
| `BOOK seq= last= bids=<p>x<q>,… asks=…` | market data snapshot (top 100 levels) |
| `TRADE seq= price= qty= aggressor=`, `LEVEL seq= side= price= qty= orders=` | market data (qty=0: level gone) |
| `SUBSCRIBED`, `UNSUBSCRIBED`, `PONG`, `BYE`, `ERROR <text>` | session |

The private stream is sent to every session of the participant (D73). `oseq` counts one participant's events; `seq` counts journal records for the whole exchange, so market data has gaps (commands without public effects).

---

## 6. Where state lives and who may touch it

| State | Where | Owner thread | Persisted? |
|---|---|---|---|
| Book (levels, nodes, id map, open counts) | `OrderBook` in `MatchingEngine` in `Ledger` | exchange-writer | rebuilt by replay; in snapshots |
| Client-order-id map | `MatchingEngine.orderIdByClientOrderId` | exchange-writer | rebuilt (since the last snapshot) |
| Last order id, last seq, last trade price | `MatchingEngine` | exchange-writer | rebuilt; in snapshots |
| Output digest | `OutputDigest` in `Ledger` | exchange-writer | checked by CHECK records; in snapshots |
| Private streams (lastOseq + last 1,000 events per participant) | `OutputLog` in `Ledger` | exchange-writer | numbers rebuilt and in snapshots; events rebuilt only since the last snapshot |
| Sequencer counters, last timestamp | `Sequencer` | exchange-writer | rebuilt from the journal |
| Skip set, commands since the last CHECK | `Exchange` | exchange-writer | skip set rebuilt; CHECK counter not |
| Request queue, state, halt cause | `ExchangeService` | any thread, under `lock` | no |
| Journal channel, fail-stop flag | `CommandJournal` | exchange-writer (opened by the starting thread) | the file is the persistence |
| Lock | `JournalLock` (`HELD` in-JVM set + OS lock on `<journal>.lock`) | owning journal / compaction | no |
| Sessions, participants → sessions, subscribers | `TcpGateway` (concurrent sets and map) | reader threads, writer thread | no |
| Per-session: participant, COD flag, in-flight count, reader-done, outbound queue, subscription flags, rate tokens | `ClientSession` | reader (tokens), writer and reader (rest, via volatile/atomic/lock) | no |
| Credentials | `Credentials` (immutable) | any | the users file |

**[Verified]** fields: `src/main/java/exchangeserver/engine/MatchingEngine.java:56-61`, `src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Exchange.java:86-94`, `src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/TcpGateway.java:104-111`, `src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/ClientSession.java:60-82`, `src/main/java/exchangeserver/journal/JournalLock.java:27-30`.

---

## 7. How state changes over time

### 7.1 An order's lifecycle

```mermaid
stateDiagram-v2
    [*] --> Rejected: duplicate id, band, caps, post-only cross
    [*] --> Matching: checks pass
    Matching --> Filled: nothing left
    Matching --> Cancelled: IOC/MARKET remainder, FOK not fillable, self-trade prevented
    Matching --> Resting: LIMIT/POST_ONLY remainder
    Resting --> PartlyFilled: an incoming order fills part
    PartlyFilled --> Resting
    Resting --> Gone: fully filled, CANCEL, CANCEL_ALL
    Filled --> [*]
    Cancelled --> [*]
    Rejected --> [*]
    Gone --> [*]
```

**[Verified]** `src/main/java/exchangeserver/engine/MatchingEngine.java:178-263`, `MatchingEngine.java:127-155`.

### 7.2 The service

`RUNNING` → `STOPPING` (after `stop()`, everything accepted is still processed) → `STOPPED`; or `RUNNING`/`STOPPING` → `HALTED` (journal failure, `Error`): everything waiting is refused, exit 2. **[Verified]** `src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/ExchangeService.java:245-257`, `ExchangeService.java:342-359`.

### 7.3 A connection (session)

```mermaid
stateDiagram-v2
    [*] --> Connected: accepted (TLS handshake on first read)
    Connected --> Starting: exchange not attached yet
    Starting --> Closed: "exchange is starting"
    Connected --> LoggedIn: LOGIN with the right secret (Attach read, WELCOME oseq)
    Connected --> Draining: login failed or login timeout
    LoggedIn --> LoggedIn: requests (in-flight count up and down)
    LoggedIn --> Draining: QUIT, end of stream, overlong line, idle timeout
    Draining --> Closed: in-flight count reaches 0 (BYE for QUIT)
    LoggedIn --> Closed: I/O error, slow consumer, gateway close
    Closed --> [*]: if COD and last session of the participant, CANCEL_ALL
```

**[Verified]** `src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/ClientSession.java:152-182`, `ClientSession.java:293-327`, `ClientSession.java:421-442`, `src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/TcpGateway.java:239-263`.

### 7.4 The journal and its snapshots over time

```mermaid
stateDiagram-v2
    [*] --> Fresh: open creates HEADER,3,rules,0
    Fresh --> Growing: commands appended (CHECK every 10,000)
    Growing --> Growing: restart (torn tail cut, replay)
    Growing --> Compacted: --compact (snapshot-N, archive, HEADER,3,rules,N)
    Compacted --> Growing: commands N+1, N+2, ...
    Compacted --> Adopted: a build with new rules opens the empty journal
    Adopted --> Growing
```

**[Verified]** `src/main/java/exchangeserver/journal/CommandJournal.java:98-132`, `src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Exchange.java:174-194`.

---

## 8. Validation layers

| Layer | Where | Checks | On failure |
|---|---|---|---|
| Line framing | `ClientSession.readLine` | ≤ 256 bytes | ERROR, disconnect after in-flight |
| Syntax | `Protocol.parse` | message type, arity, numbers, enums | ERROR, session continues |
| Authentication | `ClientSession.login`, `Credentials.verify` | name pattern, secret (PBKDF2, constant time) | `ERROR login failed`, disconnect |
| Session limits | `ClientSession.admit`, timeouts | in-flight, rate, login/idle time | ERROR (`SESSION_BUSY`, `RATE_LIMITED`, timeout) |
| Fields | `OrderRequest` | participant, clOrdId, side, type, quantity, price | ERROR, never queued |
| Hand-in | `ExchangeService.enqueue`, `Request` constructors | state, queue capacity, depth > 0, oseq > 0 | BUSY / STOPPED / HALTED |
| Stamping | `Sequencer` + command constructors | valid command | refused, never journalled |
| State-dependent | `MatchingEngine.process` | duplicate id, band, caps, post-only, self-trade | REJECTED or CANCELLED with a reason, journalled |
| Invariants | records' constructors, `OrderBook`, `PriceLevel` | contradictions, bugs | exception (poison-pill path) |
| Storage | `JournalCodec`, `Snapshot.read`, `Replay` | checksums, header, gaps, timestamps, CHECK digests | startup fails, file untouched |

**[Verified]** the cited classes; see guide 05a for the request path through them.

---

## 9. What persists, what is rebuilt, what is lost

| On disk | Rebuilt at startup | Lost on restart |
|---|---|---|
| journal (inputs), snapshot-N (state at N), archives (old journals), users file, lock file (empty) | book, counters, client-order-id map (since the snapshot), digest, oseq numbers, retained private events (since the snapshot), skip set | sessions, subscriptions, queued lines, requests in flight that were not journalled, rate tokens |

A restart rebuilds the identical book and the identical private-stream numbering; a client recovers what it missed with RESEND after logging in again. **[Verified]** tests `ExchangeTest.Restart.*`, `TcpGatewayTest.Resend.theStreamSurvivesARestart`.

---

## 10. "Migrations": versions instead of schema changes

There is no schema to migrate. Three version numbers decide what can be read:

| Version | Where | Changes when | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| `JournalCodec.FORMAT_VERSION` (3) | journal header | the record format changes | a journal of another format is refused (v2 journals from before D69 cannot be opened) |
| `Rules.VERSION` (2) | journal header | any rule replay depends on | a journal of other rules with records is refused; an empty one adopts the new rules |
| `Snapshot.FORMAT_VERSION` (1) | snapshot header | the snapshot format changes | a snapshot of another format is refused |

**[Verified]** `src/main/java/exchangeserver/journal/JournalCodec.java:41`, `src/main/java/exchangeserver/domain/Rules.java:21`, `src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Snapshot.java:44`.

The way across a rules change is a compaction with the old build, then the new build (guide 05b, flow 4c). A code change that alters behaviour without a rules bump is caught by the next CHECK record (`DIVERGED`, D70) and needs the same procedure.

---

## Key takeaways

- Only inputs persist: a checksummed journal of stamped commands, plus a snapshot of the state at the journal's base sequence; everything else is derived.
- Five command records: NEW, CANCEL, CANCEL_ALL (journalled like any input), SKIP (quarantine), CHECK (an output digest assertion).
- The Ledger (engine + output digest + output log) is the writer's derived state and is always rebuilt as one unit.
- Private events are numbered per participant (oseq) and retained (1,000) for RESEND; the numbering survives restarts and compactions.
- All exchange state belongs to the writer thread; sessions, the gateway's maps and the service's queue are the only state touched by other threads.
- Validation happens in layers, from line framing to CHECK digests; nothing the gateway refuses is journalled.
- Three version numbers (journal format, rules, snapshot format) replace migrations; compaction is the bridge across a rules change.

## Common confusions

- **"The snapshot replaces the journal."** It replaces the journal's *past*: the journal after compaction holds only a header naming the snapshot, then new commands.
- **"CHECK records are inputs."** They are assertions about outputs; replay compares them and never feeds them to the engine.
- **"oseq is stored in the journal."** It is derived from the commands by `OutputLog`; only snapshots store each participant's last number.
- **"The lock file must be deleted after a crash."** The OS lock dies with the process; the empty file is reused.
- **"The users file stores secrets."** It stores salted PBKDF2 hashes only.

## Questions to test yourself

1. Which of these are on disk: the book, the client-order-id map, the oseq numbering, the journal, the users file?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>The journal and the users file (plus snapshots). The book and the oseq numbering are also inside a snapshot after a compaction; the client-order-id map is never stored (after a snapshot only resting orders' ids are rebuilt from the ORDER lines).</details>

2. Why are OSEQ lines sorted in the snapshot?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>The output log keeps them in a hash map; sorting makes the file the same bytes regardless of iteration order (determinism, and stable checksums).</details>

3. A record `NEW,…,LIMIT,10,100,379beca7` has its quantity edited to 11. What happens at startup?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>The CRC-32C no longer matches: decoding fails with "Corrupt journal record <n>", startup stops, and the file is not changed.</details>

4. Which thread may read `OutputLog`, and how does a session get data from it?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>Only the exchange-writer. A session queues an Attach or Resend read; its callback runs on the writer and offers lines to the session's outbound queue.</details>


---

<a id="guide-07-modules-domain-and-commands"></a>

<!-- source: docs/understanding/07-modules/domain-and-commands.md -->

# 07 — Module: domain and command

> **Project:** ExchangeServer2 | Single-instrument limit order book: price-time matching engine, write-ahead journal with replay, single writer thread, TCP/TLS gateway with authenticated participants | Stack: Java 17, Maven wrapper, JUnit 5 (+ JMH, PIT)
> **This guide:** the value types every other package is built on (`domain`: `Side`, `OrderType`, `Rules`, `OrderRequest`, `Order`) and the stamped inputs plus their stamper (`command`: `Command`, `NewOrderCommand`, `CancelOrderCommand`, `CancelAllCommand`, `SkipCommand`, `CheckCommand`, `Sequencer`) | **Prerequisites:** 01, 02 | **Related:** 06, 07-modules/engine, 07-modules/journal, 07-modules/exchange-and-replay, 09, 12
> **Repo layout summary:** `pom.xml` + Maven wrapper (`mvnw`, `mvnw.cmd`) at the root; production code in `src/main/java/exchangeserver/` (packages `domain`, `book`, `engine`, `command`, `journal`, `core`, `gateway`, `client`, plus `Main`); tests mirror it in `src/test/java/exchangeserver/` (plus `bench/` JMH benchmarks and `testing/MutableClock`); design docs in `docs/` (`ExchangeServer2_Vision.md` = purpose/roadmap, `ExchangeServer2_Spec.md` = decisions D1–D77); these guides in `docs/understanding/`.

## Overview

These two small packages (about 490 lines together) define *what an order is* and *what an input to the
exchange is*. Almost every rule that can make a request invalid lives here, and the only component allowed to
read the clock or count lives here too.

- **`domain`**: plain business values.
  - `Side` is `BUY` or `SELL`.
  - `OrderType` is one of the five order types.
  - `Rules` holds every limit and constant that replay depends on.
  - `OrderRequest` is a client's order **before** the exchange accepts it. It is the only place fields are
    validated.
  - `Order` is an accepted order: a request plus an id and a timestamp. It is the one mutable object, because it
    tracks how much has been filled.
- **`command`**: the **commands**. A command is a stamped, journalled input, and commands are the system's
  source of truth.
  - There are five kinds. Three are engine inputs: `NewOrderCommand`, `CancelOrderCommand` and
    `CancelAllCommand` (cancel every resting order of one participant, used for cancel-on-disconnect, Spec D72).
    Two are bookkeeping that replay reads but never feeds to the engine: `SkipCommand` (quarantine a poison pill,
    D51) and `CheckCommand` (a digest of every output so far, D70).
  - `Sequencer` stamps them with a sequence number, a timestamp and (for new orders) an order id.

Two project terms appear throughout:

- **Replay.** On restart, the exchange rebuilds its state by feeding every journalled command through the same
  matching engine again (see guide 07-modules/exchange-and-replay). Anything replay depends on must therefore be
  deterministic and must not change silently. That requirement is why `Rules` exists and why only the
  `Sequencer` reads the clock.
- **Stateless vs state-dependent validation.** A *stateless* check needs only the request itself (is the
  quantity positive?). A *state-dependent* check needs the book (is this client order id already used?). The
  stateless checks live in `OrderRequest`; the state-dependent ones live in the engine (guide
  07-modules/engine). **[Verified]** Spec D47, Vision §4 principle 6.

```mermaid
classDiagram
    direction LR
    class Side { <<enum>> BUY; SELL; opposite() }
    class OrderType { <<enum>> LIMIT; IOC; FOK; MARKET; POST_ONLY; canRest() }
    class Rules { <<constants>> VERSION; MAX_ORDER_QUANTITY; MAX_PRICE; PARTICIPANT; CLIENT_ORDER_ID; PRICE_BAND_PERCENT; MAX_OPEN_ORDERS_PER_PARTICIPANT; MAX_RESTING_ORDERS; withinBand() }
    class OrderRequest { <<immutable>> participant; clientOrderId; side; orderType; quantity; price }
    class Order { <<mutable: filledQuantity>> orderId; timestamp; fill(q) }
    class Command { <<sealed interface>> sequenceNumber(); timestamp() }
    class NewOrderCommand { <<record>> orderId; request }
    class CancelOrderCommand { <<record>> participant; orderId (target) }
    class CancelAllCommand { <<record>> participant }
    class SkipCommand { <<record>> skippedSequenceNumber }
    class CheckCommand { <<record>> digest (16 hex) }
    class Sequencer { clock; nextSequenceNumber; nextOrderId; previousTimestamp }
    OrderRequest --> Rules : validated by
    Order --> OrderRequest : built only from
    NewOrderCommand --> OrderRequest
    Command <|.. NewOrderCommand
    Command <|.. CancelOrderCommand
    Command <|.. CancelAllCommand
    Command <|.. SkipCommand
    Command <|.. CheckCommand
    Sequencer --> Command : stamps
```

## Responsibility

| Type | Responsibility | Mutable? |
|---|---|---|
| `Side` | Which side of the book; `opposite()` | no |
| `OrderType` | The five order types; `canRest()` says which may sit on the book | no |
| `Rules` | One home for every limit replay depends on, plus the price-band arithmetic | no |
| `OrderRequest` | The only field-validation layer (stateless) | no |
| `Order` | An accepted order inside the engine and book; tracks `filledQuantity` | **yes** (only `fill`) |
| `Command` + 5 records | Stamped, journalled inputs; validated in their constructors | no |
| `Sequencer` | The only clock reader and the only counter | yes (writer thread only) |

## Public interface and who calls it

| Member | Visibility | Called by |
|---|---|---|
| `Side.opposite()` | public | `MatchingEngine.process` (`restingSide`), `MatchingEngine.matchLimit` (the touch, D66), `MatchingEngine.fillability` |
| `OrderType.canRest()` | public | `OrderBook.add` (guard), `MatchingEngine.process` (the resting caps, D68) |
| `Rules.*` constants | public | `OrderRequest`, `CancelOrderCommand`, `CancelAllCommand`, `ClientSession.login` and `Credentials` (participant pattern), `MatchingEngine` (band, caps via `Limits.RULES`, collar), `Exchange` (`Rules.VERSION` to open, compact and quarantine the journal) |
| `Rules.withinBand(price, ref)` | public | `MatchingEngine.process` (band check) |
| `OrderRequest.of(..., OptionalLong)` | public | `ClientSession.newOrder` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/ClientSession.java:332`), `JournalCodec.decode` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/journal/JournalCodec.java:138`), `MatchingEngine.restore` (rebuilding resting orders from a snapshot, `src/main/java/exchangeserver/engine/MatchingEngine.java:89`) |
| `OrderRequest.limit/ioc/fok/postOnly/market` | public | tests, benchmarks |
| `OrderRequest.rawPrice()` | package-private | `Order` constructor only |
| `new Order(id, request, ts)` | public | `MatchingEngine.onNewOrder`, `MatchingEngine.restore` |
| `Order.fill(q)` | public | `PriceLevel.fillHead` (resting side), `MatchingEngine.process` (incoming side), `MatchingEngine.restore` (re-applying a snapshot's filled quantity) |
| `Sequencer.stampNewOrder/stampCancel/stampCancelAll` | public | `Exchange.process` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Exchange.java:207`, `:209`, `:211`) |
| `Sequencer.stampCheck` | public | `Exchange.process`, at the start of a batch when a CHECK is due (`Exchange.java:201`) |
| `Sequencer.stampSkip` | public | `Exchange.quarantine` (both the static operator tool, `Exchange.java:164`, and the live poison-pill path, `Exchange.java:280`) |
| `Sequencer.resume(...)` | public static | `Exchange.open` (after replay, `Exchange.java:122`), `Exchange.quarantine` (static tool, `Exchange.java:163`) |

All of the above is **[Verified]** by reading the call sites.

## Internal walkthrough: `domain`

### `Side` and `OrderType`

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/domain/Side.java:3-10
public enum Side {
    BUY,
    SELL;

    public Side opposite() {
        return this == BUY ? SELL : BUY;
    }
}
```

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/domain/OrderType.java:3-23 (trimmed)
/*
LIMIT      match what crosses now, rest the remainder.
IOC        match what crosses now, cancel the remainder.
FOK        fill completely now or do nothing.
MARKET     no price; match at any price inside the market collar (Rules), cancel the remainder.
POST_ONLY  rest without matching; rejected if it would cross on arrival.
 */
public enum OrderType {
    LIMIT, IOC, FOK, MARKET, POST_ONLY;

    // Only these two ever rest on the book.
    public boolean canRest() {
        return this == LIMIT || this == POST_ONLY;
    }
}
```

**[Verified]** The comment block is the clearest one-line definition of each type. IOC means "immediate or
cancel" and FOK means "fill or kill". `canRest()` drives two distinct things:
1. `OrderBook.add` refuses any other type, which is a bug guard.
2. Only `LIMIT` and `POST_ONLY` are subject to the resting caps (per participant and for the whole book), and
   then only when they would actually rest (Spec D68; guide 07-modules/engine). That is a business rule.

### `Rules`: everything replay depends on

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/domain/Rules.java:17-47 (trimmed)
public final class Rules {

    // 2: self-trade prevention (D67), caps only for orders that would rest + BOOK_FULL (D68), market collar before
    //    the first trade anchored on the opposite touch (D66), CANCEL_ALL (D72).
    public static final int VERSION = 2;

    // Stateless (checked by OrderRequest, i.e. at the gateway).
    public static final long MAX_ORDER_QUANTITY = 1_000_000_000L;
    // Notional (price x quantity) can never overflow a long.
    public static final long MAX_PRICE = Long.MAX_VALUE / MAX_ORDER_QUANTITY;
    // No ',' or whitespace: they separate fields in the journal and in the wire protocol.
    public static final Pattern PARTICIPANT = Pattern.compile("[A-Za-z0-9_]{1,16}");
    public static final Pattern CLIENT_ORDER_ID = Pattern.compile("[A-Za-z0-9_.-]{1,32}");

    // State-dependent (checked by the engine).
    public static final int PRICE_BAND_PERCENT = 10;
    public static final int MAX_OPEN_ORDERS_PER_PARTICIPANT = 10_000;
    // All participants together (D68): bounds the book's memory however many participants exist.
    public static final int MAX_RESTING_ORDERS = 1_000_000;

    // Exact integer comparison, no rounding: price * 100 vs reference * (100 +- band).
    // Cannot overflow: both prices are <= MAX_PRICE (~9.2e9), times at most 110.
    public static boolean withinBand(long price, long referencePrice) {
        return price * 100 <= referencePrice * (100 + PRICE_BAND_PERCENT)
                && price * 100 >= referencePrice * (100 - PRICE_BAND_PERCENT);
    }
}
```

Line by line:

- **`VERSION` (line 21) is 2.** **[Verified]** It is written into the journal header, and `CommandJournal.open`
  refuses a journal *with records* whose header names another version (Spec D46). Replay feeds *old* commands
  through *today's* code. If, say, `MAX_ORDER_QUANTITY` shrank, an old valid order would fail decoding or be
  rejected differently, and the rebuilt book would differ from what clients saw. The class comment
  (`Rules.java:5-16`) says exactly this.
  - **Why 2 [Verified]** (`Rules.java:19-21`): version 2 changed what the engine does with the same commands:
    self-trade prevention (D67), the caps applying only to orders that would rest plus the new `BOOK_FULL` cap
    (D68), the market collar before the first trade (D66) and the new `CANCEL_ALL` command (D72). A version-1
    journal replayed under version 2 could build a different book, so it is refused.
  - **How a rules change is crossed [Verified]** (Spec D71): compact the journal with the *old* build (a
    snapshot plus an empty journal); an empty journal adopts the new build's version when it is opened (guide
    07-modules/journal).
  - **Caveat [Verified]** (Spec D70, §8): the version catches *deliberate* rule changes. A code change that alters
    outputs without touching `Rules` is now caught at the next `CHECK` record, whose output digest replay must
    reproduce; it is no longer silent, but it is detected only at a CHECK and only if an output differs.
- **`MAX_RESTING_ORDERS` (line 37) = 1,000,000.** **[Verified]** A cap on resting orders across *all*
  participants (Spec D68). The per-participant cap alone did not bound memory, because participant names used to
  be free (now they need credentials, D74). The engine rejects a resting order at this cap with `BOOK_FULL`.
- **`MAX_PRICE` (line 26) = `Long.MAX_VALUE / 1_000_000_000` = 9,223,372,036.** **[Verified]**
  `RulesTest.maxPriceTimesMaxQuantityFitsInALong` checks it. So `price * quantity` can never overflow a `long`,
  whatever the inputs. A consequence **[Inferred]**: prices are integer "ticks" (no decimals anywhere), with a
  ceiling of about 9.2 billion ticks.
- **The two patterns (lines 28–29).** They exclude `,` (the journal's field separator) and spaces (the wire
  protocol's token separator). So a participant or client order id can never corrupt either format. **[Verified]**
  by the comment, by `JournalCodec` splitting on `,` and by `Protocol.parse` splitting on a single space.
- **`withinBand` (lines 44–47).** It compares `price × 100` with `reference × 110` and `reference × 90` in
  integers, so there's no floating point and no rounding. Both edges are inclusive.
  - **Worked example [Verified by arithmetic]:** with last trade 105, the band is `[94.5, 115.5]` in real
    numbers. For integer prices 95 is the lowest allowed (`9500 >= 9450`) and 115 the highest
    (`11500 <= 11550`). 94 is out (`9400 < 9450`) and 116 is out (`11600 > 11550`).
  - **Overflow note [Verified]:** `MAX_PRICE × 110 ≈ 1.01e12`, far below `Long.MAX_VALUE`.
    `RulesTest.bandDoesNotOverflowAtTheMaximumPrice` covers it.

### `OrderRequest`: the only field-validation layer

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/domain/OrderRequest.java:24-74 (trimmed)
private OrderRequest(String participant, String clientOrderId, Side side, OrderType orderType, long quantity, long price) {
    if (participant == null || !Rules.PARTICIPANT.matcher(participant).matches()) {
        throw new IllegalArgumentException("Invalid participant: " + participant);
    }
    if (clientOrderId == null || !Rules.CLIENT_ORDER_ID.matcher(clientOrderId).matches()) { ... }
    if (side == null) { ... }
    if (orderType == null) { ... }
    if (quantity <= 0) { ... }
    if (quantity > Rules.MAX_ORDER_QUANTITY) { ... }
    if (orderType != OrderType.MARKET) {
        if (price <= 0) { ... }
        if (price > Rules.MAX_PRICE) { ... }
    }
    ...
}

// The general factory: the price is present for every type except MARKET.
public static OrderRequest of(String participant, String clientOrderId, Side side, OrderType orderType,
                              long quantity, OptionalLong price) {
    Objects.requireNonNull(price, "price");
    if (orderType == OrderType.MARKET) {
        if (price.isPresent()) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("A market order has no price");
        }
        return new OrderRequest(participant, clientOrderId, side, orderType, quantity, Order.NO_PRICE);
    }
    if (price.isEmpty()) {
        throw new IllegalArgumentException("A " + orderType + " order needs a price");
    }
    return new OrderRequest(participant, clientOrderId, side, orderType, quantity, price.getAsLong());
}
```

Key design points:

1. **The constructor is private.** Every route in goes through a factory, so every instance has passed every
   check. "An invalid `OrderRequest` cannot exist" is enforced by the type, not by convention. **[Verified]**
   `OrderRequest.java:24`.
2. **One rule set, three users.** **[Verified]**
   - The gateway builds requests with `of(...)`: `ClientSession.java:332`.
   - The journal decoder builds them with `of(...)` too (guide 07-modules/journal).
   - `MatchingEngine.restore` rebuilds each resting order of a snapshot through `of(...)` (`MatchingEngine.java:89`),
     so even a snapshot's orders pass the stateless rules.

   So a journalled record is re-validated on replay by the *same* rules as the gateway applied. The class comment
   calls this "reuse, not a third copy of the rules" (`OrderRequest.java:9`).
3. **A market order has no price in the API.** `getPrice()` returns `OptionalLong`, empty for `MARKET`
   (`OrderRequest.java:116-118`). Internally the field holds the sentinel `Order.NO_PRICE = -1`
   (`Order.java:18`), but only `Order`'s constructor can see it, through the package-private `rawPrice()`
   (`OrderRequest.java:121-123`). **[Verified]** Spec D25/D26 record this choice.
4. **`equals`/`hashCode` cover every field** (`OrderRequest.java:126-140`). **[Verified]**
   `OrderRequestTest.equalityCoversEveryField`. **[Inferred]** These matter for tests that compare decoded
   journal records with the originals, and for `NewOrderCommand` (a record), whose `equals` delegates to the
   request's.

`IllegalArgumentException` means "bad input from outside". **[Verified]** Spec D27:
`IllegalArgumentException` = bad input; `IllegalStateException` = our bug or a corrupt journal. The gateway
catches the `IllegalArgumentException` and answers `ERROR clOrdId=… <message>`, so the request never reaches the
queue (guide 07-modules/gateway).

### `Order`: an accepted order

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/domain/Order.java:30-103 (trimmed)
public Order(long orderId, OrderRequest request, Instant timestamp) {
    Objects.requireNonNull(request, "request");
    Objects.requireNonNull(timestamp, "timestamp");
    if (orderId <= 0) {
        throw new IllegalArgumentException("Order id must be positive");
    }
    this.orderId = orderId;
    this.participant = request.getParticipant();
    ...
    this.price = request.rawPrice();
    this.originalQuantity = request.getQuantity();
    this.timestamp = timestamp;
}

public boolean hasPrice() { return orderType != OrderType.MARKET; }

// A market order has no price; asking for one is a bug.
public long getPrice() {
    if (!hasPrice()) {
        throw new IllegalStateException("Order " + orderId + " is a market order and has no price");
    }
    return price;
}

public long getRemainingQuantity() { return originalQuantity - filledQuantity; }

public void fill(long quantity) {
    if (quantity <= 0) { throw new IllegalArgumentException("Fill quantity must be positive"); }
    if (quantity > getRemainingQuantity()) { throw new IllegalArgumentException("Cannot overfill order " + orderId); }
    filledQuantity += quantity;
}
```

- **The only constructor takes a request** (Spec D47). So `Order` re-checks only what a request cannot know:
  the id and the timestamp. **[Verified]**
- **Its single mutable field is `filledQuantity`** (`Order.java:28`); "remaining" is derived from it. **[Verified]**
- **An `Order` holds no references to the book, level or node.** "An Order does not know where it is stored"
  (`Order.java:13`). The book wraps it in an `OrderNode` instead (guide 07-modules/book). **[Verified]**
- **`getPrice()` throws for `MARKET`.** Code that forgets the "market has no price" case fails loudly instead of
  matching at price −1. **[Verified]**
- **Who calls `fill`.** It is called on the resting order (inside `PriceLevel.fillHead`) and on the incoming
  order (in `MatchingEngine.process`). Both pass the same quantity per trade. **[Verified]** by reading both.

## Internal walkthrough: `command`

### The sealed `Command` interface and its five records

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/command/Command.java:11-16
public sealed interface Command permits NewOrderCommand, CancelOrderCommand, CancelAllCommand, SkipCommand, CheckCommand {
    long sequenceNumber();
    Instant timestamp();
}
```

A **sealed interface** (Java 17) lists every allowed implementation, so the compiler knows the full set. A
**record** is an immutable data class; a *compact constructor* (`public NewOrderCommand { ... }`) runs
validation before the fields are assigned. Commands are immutable, so "the object that is logged and the object
that is processed cannot differ" (`Command.java:7-8`). **[Verified]**

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/command/NewOrderCommand.java:8-21 (trimmed)
public record NewOrderCommand(long sequenceNumber, Instant timestamp, long orderId, OrderRequest request)
        implements Command {
    public NewOrderCommand {
        Objects.requireNonNull(timestamp, "timestamp");
        Objects.requireNonNull(request, "request");
        if (sequenceNumber <= 0) { throw new IllegalArgumentException("Sequence number must be positive"); }
        if (orderId <= 0) { throw new IllegalArgumentException("Order id must be positive"); }
    }
}
```

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/command/CancelOrderCommand.java:8-23 (trimmed)
// orderId is the target; a cancel is not issued an id of its own (D32).
public record CancelOrderCommand(long sequenceNumber, Instant timestamp, String participant, long orderId)
        implements Command {
    public CancelOrderCommand {
        Objects.requireNonNull(timestamp, "timestamp");
        if (sequenceNumber <= 0) { ... }
        if (participant == null || !Rules.PARTICIPANT.matcher(participant).matches()) { ... }
        if (orderId <= 0) { ... }
    }
}
```

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/command/SkipCommand.java:14-24 (trimmed)
public record SkipCommand(long sequenceNumber, Instant timestamp, long skippedSequenceNumber) implements Command {
    public SkipCommand {
        Objects.requireNonNull(timestamp, "timestamp");
        if (sequenceNumber <= 0) { ... }
        if (skippedSequenceNumber <= 0 || skippedSequenceNumber >= sequenceNumber) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Can only skip an earlier command, not " + skippedSequenceNumber);
        }
    }
}
```

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/command/CancelAllCommand.java:8-21 (trimmed)
// Cancels every resting order of one participant (cancel-on-disconnect, D72). An input like any other: journalled
// before it is processed, replayed on restart.
public record CancelAllCommand(long sequenceNumber, Instant timestamp, String participant) implements Command {
    public CancelAllCommand {
        Objects.requireNonNull(timestamp, "timestamp");
        if (sequenceNumber <= 0) { ... }
        if (participant == null || !Rules.PARTICIPANT.matcher(participant).matches()) { ... }
    }
}
```

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/command/CheckCommand.java:16-29 (trimmed)
public record CheckCommand(long sequenceNumber, Instant timestamp, String digest) implements Command {

    private static final Pattern HEX16 = Pattern.compile("[0-9a-f]{16}");

    public CheckCommand {
        Objects.requireNonNull(timestamp, "timestamp");
        if (sequenceNumber <= 0) { ... }
        if (digest == null || !HEX16.matcher(digest).matches()) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Digest must be 16 lower-case hex digits: " + digest);
        }
    }
}
```

- **`NewOrderCommand`** carries the issued `orderId` and the validated request.
- **`CancelOrderCommand`** carries the participant, needed for the ownership check (Spec D45, D48), and the
  *target* order id. A cancel does not consume an order id. **[Verified]**
- **`CancelAllCommand`** carries only the participant whose resting orders are all cancelled (Spec D72). The
  gateway issues it when the last session of a participant that logged in with `COD` closes. It is an input like
  any other: journalled first, replayed on restart, and it consumes a sequence number but no order id.
  **[Verified]** `CancelAllCommand.java:8-9`.
- **`SkipCommand`** is the quarantine marker of the poison-pill policy (Spec D51). It names an *earlier*
  sequence number that replay must not feed to the engine. The skipped record stays in the file: "history is
  never edited, only annotated" (`SkipCommand.java:11`). **[Verified]** The constructor's "only earlier"
  check makes a self-skip or a forward skip impossible. **[Verified]**
- **`CheckCommand`** is a checkpoint (Spec D70): "the digest of every engine output for the commands before this
  one is `digest`" (`CheckCommand.java:9-10`). The live exchange writes one every `checkInterval` commands; replay
  recomputes the digest and stops if it differs. `HEX16` (`CheckCommand.java:18`) requires exactly 16 *lower-case*
  hex digits: the digest is a 64-bit value printed with `%016x`, so any other spelling cannot be one. It is never
  fed to the engine. **[Verified]**

Only the first three are engine inputs. `SkipCommand` and `CheckCommand` are bookkeeping that replay reads and
acts on (skip a command; compare a digest) but never feeds to the engine. **[Verified]** `Replay.feed`
(`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Replay.java`, guide 07-modules/exchange-and-replay).

There is no command for reads (snapshots, attach, resend). Reads are never stamped or journalled (guide
07-modules/exchange-and-replay). **[Verified]** `ExchangeTest.interceptorSeesEveryCommandLiveInOrder` asserts
that only the two orders and the cancel got sequence numbers.

### `Sequencer`: the only clock reader and counter

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/command/Sequencer.java:20-94 (trimmed)
public final class Sequencer {
    private final Clock clock;
    private long nextSequenceNumber;
    private long nextOrderId;
    private Instant previousTimestamp;

    public Sequencer(Clock clock) { this(clock, 1, 1, Instant.MIN); }

    public static Sequencer resume(Clock clock, long lastSequenceNumber, long lastOrderId, Instant lastTimestamp) {
        if (lastSequenceNumber < 0 || lastOrderId < 0) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Counters cannot be negative");
        }
        return new Sequencer(clock, lastSequenceNumber + 1, lastOrderId + 1,
                lastTimestamp == null ? Instant.MIN : lastTimestamp);
    }

    public NewOrderCommand stampNewOrder(OrderRequest request) {
        Objects.requireNonNull(request, "request");     // before stamping: a null must not use up numbers
        Instant timestamp = nextTimestamp();
        return new NewOrderCommand(nextSequenceNumber++, timestamp, nextOrderId++, request);
    }

    public CancelOrderCommand stampCancel(String participant, long orderId) {
        CancelOrderCommand command = new CancelOrderCommand(nextSequenceNumber, nextTimestamp(), participant, orderId);
        nextSequenceNumber++;                           // only once the command is valid
        return command;
    }

    public CancelAllCommand stampCancelAll(String participant) {
        CancelAllCommand command = new CancelAllCommand(nextSequenceNumber, nextTimestamp(), participant);
        nextSequenceNumber++;
        return command;
    }

    public CheckCommand stampCheck(String digest) {
        CheckCommand command = new CheckCommand(nextSequenceNumber, nextTimestamp(), digest);
        nextSequenceNumber++;
        return command;
    }

    public SkipCommand stampSkip(long skippedSequenceNumber) {
        SkipCommand command = new SkipCommand(nextSequenceNumber, nextTimestamp(), skippedSequenceNumber);
        nextSequenceNumber++;
        return command;
    }

    private Instant nextTimestamp() {
        Instant now = clock.instant();
        if (now.isBefore(previousTimestamp)) {
            now = previousTimestamp;
        }
        previousTimestamp = now;
        return now;
    }
}
```

The tricky parts, line by line:

1. **Two counters, both starting at 1** (`Sequencer.java:27-29`). Every command gets the next sequence number;
   only new orders get the next order id. **[Verified]**
   - Example: NEW, CANCEL, NEW is stamped seq 1/2/3 with order ids 1 and 2.
   - `SequencerTest.onlyNewOrdersUseUpOrderIds` checks this.
   - CANCEL_ALL, CHECK and SKIP also take a sequence number and no order id.
     `SequencerTest.CommandValidation.cancelAllAndCheckUseSequenceNumbersButNoOrderIds` checks the first two.
2. **"Only once valid" ordering.** `stampCancel`, `stampCancelAll`, `stampCheck` and `stampSkip` build the
   record first, which runs its validation, and increment `nextSequenceNumber` only afterwards
   (`Sequencer.java:55-56`, `61-62`, `67-68`, `73-74`). An invalid cancel (bad participant, order id ≤ 0) or
   cancel-all (bad participant) therefore throws without using a number, and the journal keeps its sequence
   numbers contiguous. **[Verified]** `SequencerTest.InvalidInputUsesUpNothing`, and
   `cancelAllAndCheckUseSequenceNumbersButNoOrderIds` (an invalid `stampCancelAll` leaves the next number at 3).
   - Why it matters more now: `Exchange.process` stamps a whole batch before journalling it, and a CHECK at the
     start of a batch (D70). A number used up by an invalid request would leave a gap that replay refuses.
   - `stampNewOrder` uses the opposite style: post-increments inside the constructor call. That is safe because
     the only failure its constructor could raise on real input (a null request) is checked *before*
     (`Sequencer.java:49`). **[Verified]** by reading.
   - **[Inferred]** `nextTimestamp()` is still called before an invalid cancel throws, so `previousTimestamp` may
     move forward for a command that is never issued. That is harmless, because timestamps only need to be
     non-decreasing.
3. **The backwards clock (Spec D33).** If the wall clock steps back (an NTP correction, a VM pause),
   `nextTimestamp` reuses the previous timestamp instead of going backwards (`Sequencer.java:86-93`). Equal
   timestamps are allowed. **[Verified]** `SequencerTest.Clock`.
   - Why it matters: replay *verifies* that timestamps never decrease and refuses a journal where they do (guide
     07-modules/exchange-and-replay). So a backwards stamp would make the next restart fail.
4. **`resume` continues after a replayed journal** (Spec D41). The caller passes the last sequence number, the
   last *new-order* id and the last timestamp. **[Verified]** The comment at `Sequencer.java:38-39` explains why
   it must be the last NEW record's id rather than the last record's id field: a cancel's id field is a target,
   not an issued id. `ExchangeTest.nextOrderIdComesFromTheLastNewOrderNotTheLastRecord` covers this. After a
   compaction (D71) the counters come from the snapshot plus whatever the journal added since, so numbering
   continues across the compaction. **[Verified]** `ExchangeTest.Compaction.restartAfterCompactionRebuildsTheSameState`.
5. **The clock is injected.** The `Clock` is a constructor parameter, so tests pass `MutableClock` (a clock tests
   can move, even backwards; `src/test/java/exchangeserver/testing/MutableClock.java`). **[Verified]**
6. **Not thread-safe, by design.** It is called only from the writer thread. Because stamping happens there,
   *sequence order equals queue order equals processing order* without any lock (`Sequencer.java:16-17`; Spec
   D55). **[Verified]**

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant W as Writer thread (Exchange.process)
    participant S as Sequencer
    participant C as Clock
    W->>S: stampNewOrder(request)
    S->>S: requireNonNull(request)
    S->>C: instant()
    C-->>S: now (maybe earlier than previous)
    S->>S: now = max(now, previousTimestamp)
    S-->>W: NewOrderCommand(seq++, now, orderId++, request)
    W->>S: stampCancel("alice", 0)
    S->>C: instant()
    S-->>W: throws IllegalArgumentException (seq NOT incremented)
```

## Dependencies in and out

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    subgraph domain
      Side; OrderType; Rules; OrderRequest; Order
    end
    subgraph command
      Command; NewOrderCommand; CancelOrderCommand; CancelAllCommand; SkipCommand; CheckCommand; Sequencer
    end
    OrderRequest --> Rules
    Order --> OrderRequest
    NewOrderCommand --> OrderRequest
    CancelOrderCommand --> Rules
    CancelAllCommand --> Rules
    Sequencer --> NewOrderCommand & CancelOrderCommand & CancelAllCommand & SkipCommand & CheckCommand
    gateway[gateway.ClientSession / Credentials] --> OrderRequest
    gateway --> Rules
    journal[journal.JournalCodec] --> OrderRequest & NewOrderCommand & CancelOrderCommand & CancelAllCommand & SkipCommand & CheckCommand
    engine[engine.MatchingEngine] --> Order & Rules & OrderType & Side & CancelAllCommand
    book[book.OrderBook / PriceLevel] --> Order & Side
    core[core.Exchange / Replay] --> Sequencer & Command
```

**[Verified]** by imports. `domain` depends on nothing but the JDK. `command` depends only on `domain`. They are
the foundation layer.

## How it's tested

| Test class | `@Nested` groups / notable tests | What it pins down |
|---|---|---|
| `domain/OrderRequestTest` (16 tests) | `Factories` (each typed factory sets its type; `of` matches the typed factories; market with price and priced type without price are refused); `Validation` (parameterised quantity/price must be positive; limits inclusive; patterns); `equalityCoversEveryField` | The stateless rule set |
| `domain/OrderTest` (8) | `Construction`, `Price` (market has no price and says so), `Fill` (partial then full, cannot overfill, must be positive) | The one mutable object |
| `domain/RulesTest` (6) | `bandIsInclusiveAtBothEdges`, `bandComparesExactlyWithoutRounding`, `bandDoesNotOverflowAtTheMaximumPrice`, `maxPriceTimesMaxQuantityFitsInALong`, `oppositeSide`, `onlyLimitAndPostOnlyRest` | Arithmetic edges; also `Side` and `OrderType` |
| `command/SequencerTest` (21) | `Stamping`, `InvalidInputUsesUpNothing`, `Clock` (backwards step reuses the previous timestamp; resumes once it catches up; equal timestamps allowed), `Resume`, `CommandValidation` (the records' constructors: `cancelAllCommand`, `checkCommandNeedsSixteenHexDigits` including upper-case refused, `cancelAllAndCheckUseSequenceNumbersButNoOrderIds`, the skip rule) | Counters, clock policy, record validation |

**[Verified]** Test counts come from counting `@Test`/`@ParameterizedTest` annotations; parameterised tests run
more cases than that. Two deviations from "one test class per production class" **[Verified]**: `Side` and
`OrderType` are tested in `RulesTest`, and the five command records in `SequencerTest.CommandValidation`. Both
packages are included in PIT mutation testing (`pom.xml` `targetClasses`). **[Verified]**

## Key takeaways

- `OrderRequest` is the **only** field-validation layer. Its private constructor makes an invalid request
  impossible, and both the gateway and the journal decoder build requests through it.
- `Order` is the only mutable domain object, and only `filledQuantity` changes. A market order has no price
  (`hasPrice()` is false, `getPrice()` throws).
- `Rules` holds every constant replay depends on. Changing one means bumping `Rules.VERSION` (now 2, for D66–D68
  and D72); an old journal with records is then refused, and the way across is compaction with the old build.
- `MAX_PRICE × MAX_ORDER_QUANTITY` fits in a `long`; the band check is exact integer arithmetic, inclusive at both
  edges.
- Commands are immutable records under a sealed interface: three engine inputs (NEW, CANCEL, CANCEL_ALL) and
  two pieces of bookkeeping replay reads but never feeds (SKIP, CHECK). Reads are not commands.
- `Sequencer` is the single source of sequence numbers, order ids and timestamps. It never moves a timestamp
  backwards and never uses up a number for an invalid input.
- Only new orders consume order ids; cancels carry a *target* id; a SKIP names an earlier sequence number and never
  deletes history.

## Common confusions

- **"Is `Order` validated twice?"** No. `Order` checks only the id and the timestamp; everything else was checked
  when the `OrderRequest` was built (Spec D47).
- **"Does the order id equal the sequence number?"** Only by coincidence. The two counters diverge as soon as a
  cancel or skip is stamped, which uses a sequence number but no order id.
- **"A rejected order doesn't get an order id, right?"** Wrong. The order id is stamped before the engine runs,
  so a state-dependent reject (duplicate client order id, band, cap, post-only cross) still uses up an order id
  and a sequence number, and is journalled. Only *stateless* rejects (refused at the gateway) use nothing.
- **"`Order.NO_PRICE` is −1, so a market order trades at −1?"** No. The sentinel never leaves the domain package
  through a public API; `getPrice()` throws for market orders, and the engine uses a separate "match limit"
  (guide 07-modules/engine).
- **"`Rules.VERSION` protects against any behaviour change."** No, only against deliberate changes to `Rules`.
  Other behaviour changes are caught later and differently: at the next `CHECK` record, if an output differs
  (Spec D70).
- **"A CHECK is an engine command, like a cancel."** No. It takes a sequence number, but replay only compares
  its digest; the engine never sees it.

## Questions to test yourself

1. Why is `OrderRequest`'s constructor private, and which two production call sites build requests?
   <details><summary>Answer</summary>So that every instance has passed every check: factories are the only way in. The gateway (`ClientSession.newOrder` via `OrderRequest.of`) and the journal decoder (`JournalCodec.decode` via `OrderRequest.of`) both build requests, so replay re-applies the same stateless rules as the gateway.</details>
2. Stamp, in order: NEW, CANCEL (participant "bob", order 1), CANCEL (participant "bad name", order 1), NEW. What sequence numbers and order ids result?
   <details><summary>Answer</summary>NEW → seq 1, id 1. CANCEL → seq 2. The invalid cancel throws `IllegalArgumentException` from the record constructor before the increment, so it uses no number. NEW → seq 3, id 2.</details>
3. The clock reads 10:00:05, then 10:00:03, then 10:00:06. Which timestamps are stamped, and why does it matter?
   <details><summary>Answer</summary>10:00:05, 10:00:05 (the previous one is reused), 10:00:06. Replay refuses a journal whose timestamps decrease, so a backwards stamp would make the next restart fail.</details>
4. The last trade was 200. Which integer prices pass `Rules.withinBand`?
   <details><summary>Answer</summary>`price×100 ≤ 200×110 = 22000` and `price×100 ≥ 200×90 = 18000`, so 180 to 220 inclusive.</details>
5. Why must `Sequencer.resume` take the last NEW record's order id rather than the last record's id field?
   <details><summary>Answer</summary>The last record may be a CANCEL, whose id field is the target order, not an issued id. Taking it would reuse or skip ids; the engine refuses a non-increasing id (D35), so startup or the next order would fail.</details>
6. Can a `SkipCommand` skip itself or a later command? Where is that enforced?
   <details><summary>Answer</summary>No. Its compact constructor requires `0 < skippedSequenceNumber < sequenceNumber` (`SkipCommand.java:21-23`).</details>
7. Why do the participant and client-order-id patterns exclude commas and spaces?
   <details><summary>Answer</summary>Commas separate journal fields and spaces separate wire-protocol tokens; an id containing either could corrupt or be mis-parsed by those formats.</details>
8. A batch arrives with a CHECK due, then NEW, CANCEL_ALL ("alice"), NEW; the sequencer is at seq 10, order id 4. What is stamped?
   <details><summary>Answer</summary>CHECK seq 10 (stamped first, so its digest covers everything before it), NEW seq 11 id 4, CANCEL_ALL seq 12 (no order id), NEW seq 13 id 5.</details>
9. Why did `Rules.VERSION` go from 1 to 2, and what must an operator do to move a version-1 journal to this build?
   <details><summary>Answer</summary>Version 2 changes what the engine does with the same commands (self-trade prevention, caps only for orders that would rest plus BOOK_FULL, the market collar before the first trade, CANCEL_ALL), so replaying a version-1 journal could build a different book. In general the way across is to compact with the old build (snapshot + empty journal) and start the new build, whose open adopts the empty journal. (Format v2 journals from before this change also fail the format check, Spec §8.)</details>


---

<a id="guide-07-modules-book"></a>

<!-- source: docs/understanding/07-modules/book.md -->

# 07 — Module: book

> **Project:** ExchangeServer2 | Single-instrument limit order book: price-time matching engine, write-ahead journal with replay, single writer thread, TCP/TLS gateway with authenticated participants | Stack: Java 17, Maven wrapper, JUnit 5 (+ JMH, PIT)
> **This guide:** the order book data structure (`OrderBook`, `PriceLevel`, `OrderNode`) and its small result types (`Fill`, `LevelUpdate`, `CancelResult`, `CancelStatus`, `RestingOrderView`), its invariants and how they are kept, and the read-only walks the engine uses for self-trade prevention, cancel-all and snapshots | **Prerequisites:** 01, 07-modules/domain-and-commands | **Related:** 06, 07-modules/engine, 09, 10
> **Repo layout summary:** `pom.xml` + Maven wrapper (`mvnw`, `mvnw.cmd`) at the root; production code in `src/main/java/exchangeserver/` (packages `domain`, `book`, `engine`, `command`, `journal`, `core`, `gateway`, `client`, plus `Main`); tests mirror it in `src/test/java/exchangeserver/` (plus `bench/` JMH benchmarks and `testing/MutableClock`); design docs in `docs/` (`ExchangeServer2_Vision.md` = purpose/roadmap, `ExchangeServer2_Spec.md` = decisions D1–D77); these guides in `docs/understanding/`.

## Overview

An **order book** is the list of orders waiting to trade (**resting** orders), grouped by side and price:
- the **bids** are buy orders, best = highest price first;
- the **asks** are sell orders, best = lowest price first.

Inside one price, orders queue in arrival order, which is **time priority**. Together that gives
**price-time priority**: better price first, then earlier arrival.

The `book` package is a data structure with a few hard rules (**invariants**) and no business logic:
- It does not decide whether two orders match. That is the engine's job (guide 07-modules/engine).
- It *carries out* what the engine decides, through exactly three mutating entry points: `add`, `fillBest` and
  `cancel`.
- It lets the engine *look* at individual resting orders only through copies: `walk`, `restingOrders` and
  `headParticipant` hand out `RestingOrderView` records or a participant name, never the `Order` objects inside
  the book (added for Spec D67, D71, D72).

**[Verified]** Spec D44 and Vision §4 principles 2 and 9.

The physical shape:

- **Each side** is a `TreeMap<Long, PriceLevel>`, a sorted map keyed by price. The bid map uses
  `Comparator.reverseOrder()`, so `firstEntry()` is the best price on both sides. **[Verified]**
  `src/main/java/exchangeserver/book/OrderBook.java:41-42`.
- **Each `PriceLevel`** is a hand-written **doubly linked list with sentinels**: dummy `head` and `tail` nodes
  that are never removed, so linking and unlinking need no special cases for the ends. **[Verified]** Spec D6, D7.
- **An id map `orderId → OrderNode`** lets a cancel find an order's node in O(1) and unlink it in O(1).
  **[Verified]** `OrderBook.java:43`, Spec D1, D5.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    subgraph OrderBook
      direction TB
      B["bids: TreeMap (reverse order)<br/>101 → level, 100 → level"]
      A["asks: TreeMap (natural order)<br/>102 → level, 105 → level"]
      M["orders: HashMap orderId → OrderNode"]
      P["openOrders: HashMap participant → count"]
      C["changed: LinkedHashSet of LevelKey(side, price)"]
    end
    subgraph "PriceLevel BUY 100"
      direction LR
      H((head sentinel)) <--> N1["node: order 7<br/>rem 5"] <--> N2["node: order 9<br/>rem 3"] <--> T((tail sentinel))
    end
    B --> H
    M -. "7 →" .-> N1
    M -. "9 →" .-> N2
```

## Responsibility

| Type | What it is | Visibility |
|---|---|---|
| `OrderBook` | Owns the lifecycle of every resting order (Spec D1); the only mutator of levels | public class |
| `PriceLevel` | Orders at one price on one side, in time priority. **Public getters only; every mutator is package-private** | public class |
| `OrderNode` | One list node: `order`, `prev`, `next`, `level` | package-private class |
| `Fill` | Immutable record of what `fillBest` did to one resting order | public record |
| `LevelUpdate` | Immutable record: one level's state after a command (level-2 market data); volume 0 = gone | public record |
| `CancelResult` / `CancelStatus` | Outcome of a cancel: `CANCELLED` (with quantity) or `UNKNOWN_ORDER` | public record / enum |
| `RestingOrderView` | Immutable copy of one resting order (id, owner, client order id, side, type, price, original and filled quantity, timestamp) | public record |

"Level-2 market data" (L2) means aggregated volume per price level, as opposed to individual orders.

## Public interface and who calls it

| Member | Visibility | Called by |
|---|---|---|
| `OrderBook.add(Order)` | public | `MatchingEngine.process` (rests a LIMIT/POST_ONLY remainder), `MatchingEngine.restore` (reloads a snapshot's orders) |
| `OrderBook.fillBest(Side, maxQty) → Fill` | public | `MatchingEngine.process` (the match loop) |
| `OrderBook.cancel(participant, orderId) → CancelResult` | public | `MatchingEngine.onCancel`, `MatchingEngine.onCancelAll` (once per collected id) |
| `OrderBook.drainLevelUpdates()` | public | `MatchingEngine.onNewOrder` / `onCancel` / `onCancelAll` (once per command), `restore` (to discard the reload's updates) |
| `OrderBook.bestLevel(Side)` | public | `MatchingEngine.process` (match loop, post-only check), `MatchingEngine.matchLimit` (the touch, D66); `MatchingBenchmark` |
| `OrderBook.levels(Side)` | public | `MatchingEngine.levels` (snapshot) |
| `OrderBook.walk(Side, Predicate<RestingOrderView>)` | public | `MatchingEngine.fillability` (self-trade-aware FOK/cap check, D67/D68), `MatchingEngine.onCancelAll` (D72), `restingOrders` |
| `OrderBook.restingOrders()` | public | `MatchingEngine.exportState` (snapshots, D71) |
| `OrderBook.headParticipant(Side)` | public | `MatchingEngine.process` (self-trade check in the match loop, D67) |
| `OrderBook.openOrders(participant)` | public | `MatchingEngine.process` (participant cap) |
| `OrderBook.restingOrderCount()` | public | `MatchingEngine(OrderBook)` constructor (must be 0), `MatchingEngine.process` (`BOOK_FULL` cap, D68) |
| `volumeAt`, `levelCount`, `isResting` | public | tests only **[Verified]** by grep |
| `OrderBook.ordersAt`, `checkInvariants()` | package-private | tests only (test seams) |
| `PriceLevel.getSide/getPrice/getTotalVolume/getOrderCount/isEmpty` | public | engine (read-only) and `OrderBook` |
| `PriceLevel.addOrder/headNode/nextNode/fillHead/unlink` | package-private | `OrderBook` only |

Because the engine is in another package, the compiler stops it from calling `PriceLevel` mutators. That is the
"ownership enforced by packages" rule from `CLAUDE.md`. **[Verified]** Spec D44.

## The invariants

The class comment lists them, and `checkInvariants()` re-derives them from scratch:

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/book/OrderBook.java:23-28 (trimmed comment)
Every mutation goes through four methods (add, fillBest, cancel, and the private removeNode they share), so
these invariants have exactly one place to hold:
1. An id is in `orders` iff its order is linked in a level.
2. No empty level is ever left in `bids` or `asks` (eager removal, D10).
3. openOrders.get(p) == number of resting orders owned by p (absent when 0).
4. Every level touched since the last drainLevelUpdates() is in `changed`.
```

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/book/PriceLevel.java:20-25 (comment)
1. totalVolume == sum of remaining quantity over the linked nodes.
2. orderCount  == number of linked nodes.
3. node.level == this  <=>  node is linked in this level.
4. Every linked order has remaining > 0, except the head between fillHead() exhausting it and
   OrderBook unlinking it (both inside one OrderBook.fillBest call).
```

Plus, from `checkInvariants` (`OrderBook.java:226-267`): levels are strictly ordered on each side, and the book
is never **crossed**, meaning the best bid is below the best ask. **[Verified]** The "never crossed" invariant
holds because the engine always matches before resting; the book itself does not enforce it on `add`.
**[Inferred]** from reading `add`, which has no cross check.

## Internal walkthrough: `PriceLevel`

### Construction and the sentinels

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/book/PriceLevel.java:28-41 (trimmed)
public final class PriceLevel {
    private final Side side;
    private final long price;
    private final OrderNode head = OrderNode.sentinel();
    private final OrderNode tail = OrderNode.sentinel();
    private long totalVolume;
    private int orderCount;

    PriceLevel(Side side, long price) {
        this.side = side;
        this.price = price;
        head.next = tail;
        tail.prev = head;
    }
```

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/book/OrderNode.java:18-35 (trimmed)
final class OrderNode {
    final Order order;
    OrderNode prev;
    OrderNode next;
    PriceLevel level;

    OrderNode(Order order) {
        this.order = Objects.requireNonNull(order, "A real node must wrap an order");
    }
    private OrderNode() { this.order = null; }
    static OrderNode sentinel() { return new OrderNode(); }
}
```

`order == null` holds **if and only if** the node is a sentinel. That is enforced by construction: the
real-node constructor rejects null, and sentinels can only come from `sentinel()`. **[Verified]**
`OrderNode.java:12-13, 24-34`. Every real linked node therefore has a non-null `prev` and `next`, at worst a
sentinel, which is why link and unlink have no `if (node == head)` branches.

### `addOrder`: append at the tail, checks first

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/book/PriceLevel.java:64-87
OrderNode addOrder(Order order) {
    // All checks before any mutation.
    if (order.getSide() != side || order.getPrice() != price) { throw new IllegalStateException(...); }
    if (order.getRemainingQuantity() <= 0) { throw new IllegalStateException(...); }
    if (order.getRemainingQuantity() > Long.MAX_VALUE - totalVolume) { throw new IllegalStateException(...); }

    OrderNode node = new OrderNode(order);
    OrderNode last = tail.prev;
    node.prev = last;
    node.next = tail;
    last.next = node;
    tail.prev = node;
    node.level = this;

    totalVolume += order.getRemainingQuantity();
    orderCount++;
    return node;
}
```

- It returns the node so `OrderBook` can store it in the id map. **[Verified]**
- It adds the order's *remaining* quantity, not the original. A partially filled incoming order that rests adds
  only what is left (`PriceLevelTest.addsRemainingNotOriginalQuantity`). **[Verified]**
- **Overflow guard.** It makes sure `totalVolume` can never wrap round. **[Verified]** **[Inferred]** It is
  practically unreachable, since at most 10,000 orders per participant × 1e9 each per participant. Spec §7 lists
  the related `<=`/`<` mutant as equivalent.

### `fillHead`: fill, never remove

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/book/PriceLevel.java:104-121
long fillHead(long maxQuantity) {
    if (maxQuantity <= 0) { throw new IllegalArgumentException("Fill quantity must be positive"); }
    OrderNode first = headNode();
    if (first == null) { throw new IllegalStateException("Fill on empty level " + side + " " + price); }
    Order order = first.order;
    if (order.getRemainingQuantity() == 0) { throw new IllegalStateException("Exhausted order ... left at the head ..."); }

    long quantity = Math.min(order.getRemainingQuantity(), maxQuantity);
    order.fill(quantity);
    totalVolume -= quantity;
    return quantity;
}
```

It fills the head order by `min(remaining, maxQuantity)` and subtracts that from `totalVolume`, but **does not
unlink an exhausted head**: "that is OrderBook's job, so the id map and the list change together" (Spec D3).
**[Verified]** This leaves a deliberate, short window where invariant 4 is broken: the head has remaining 0. The
window closes inside the same `OrderBook.fillBest` call (below).

### `unlink`: O(1) removal, shared by fills and cancels

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/book/PriceLevel.java:124-142
void unlink(OrderNode node) {
    if (node.order == null) { throw new IllegalStateException("Cannot unlink a sentinel"); }
    if (node.level != this) { throw new IllegalStateException("Order ... is not linked in level ..."); }

    node.prev.next = node.next;
    node.next.prev = node.prev;

    // 0 for a filled order (fillHead already subtracted it); the live remainder for a cancel.
    totalVolume -= node.order.getRemainingQuantity();
    orderCount--;

    node.prev = null;
    node.next = null;
    node.level = null;
}
```

The key arithmetic is `totalVolume -= remaining`:
- For a **fill-removal**, `fillHead` already subtracted the filled quantity and the remaining is 0, so this
  subtracts 0.
- For a **cancel**, the remaining is still on the books, so this subtracts it.

One line serves both paths (Spec D2). **[Verified]** Nulling the links (Spec D8) makes a second `unlink` of the
same node fail loudly (`node.level != this`), and so does unlinking a node from another level.
`PriceLevelTest.Unlink.refusesASecondUnlink`, `refusesANodeFromAnotherLevel` and `refusesASentinel` cover this.
**[Verified]**

## Internal walkthrough: `OrderBook`

### `add`: all-or-nothing

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/book/OrderBook.java:49-74
public void add(Order order) {
    if (!order.getOrderType().canRest()) { throw new IllegalStateException("A " + ... + " order never rests: " ...); }
    if (order.getRemainingQuantity() <= 0) { throw new IllegalStateException(... "has nothing left to rest"); }
    if (orders.containsKey(order.getOrderId())) { throw new IllegalStateException("Duplicate order id " ...); }  // D15

    TreeMap<Long, PriceLevel> side = sideOf(order.getSide());
    PriceLevel level = side.get(order.getPrice());
    boolean newLevel = level == null;
    if (newLevel) {
        level = new PriceLevel(order.getSide(), order.getPrice());
    }
    OrderNode node = level.addOrder(order);         // throws before linking anything
    if (newLevel) {
        side.put(order.getPrice(), level);          // only now: a failed add must not leave an empty level
    }

    orders.put(order.getOrderId(), node);
    openOrders.merge(order.getParticipant(), 1, Integer::sum);
    changed.add(new LevelKey(order.getSide(), order.getPrice()));
}
```

The **ordering** is the point. A new level is created but put into the tree only *after* `addOrder` succeeded.
If `addOrder` threw (wrong price, overflow), the tree would otherwise keep an empty level and break invariant 2.
After `addOrder` returns, the remaining statements (`put`, `merge`, `add`) cannot fail on valid state.
**[Verified]** `OrderBookTest.Add.duplicateIdThrowsAndChangesNothing`, `refusesTypesThatNeverRest` and
`refusesAnExhaustedOrder`. **[Inferred]** Only an `OutOfMemoryError` could interrupt them, and an `Error` halts
the exchange anyway.

### `fillBest`: fill, remove the exhausted order, remove an emptied level

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/book/OrderBook.java:78-95
public Fill fillBest(Side side, long maxQuantity) {
    Map.Entry<Long, PriceLevel> best = sideOf(side).firstEntry();
    if (best == null) { throw new IllegalStateException("Fill on empty side " + side); }
    PriceLevel level = best.getValue();
    OrderNode head = level.headNode();
    Order order = head.order;

    long quantity = level.fillHead(maxQuantity);
    changed.add(new LevelKey(side, level.getPrice()));
    if (order.getRemainingQuantity() == 0) {
        removeNode(head);
    }

    return new Fill(order.getOrderId(), order.getParticipant(), order.getClientOrderId(), side,
            level.getPrice(), quantity, order.getRemainingQuantity());
}
```

Line by line:
1. `side` is the side **of the book being hit**, the resting side, not the incoming order's side. The comment
   on `bestLevel` says the same (`OrderBook.java:178`). **[Verified]**
2. It remembers `head` and `order` before filling, because the head may be unlinked during the call.
3. `fillHead` fills; then, if the order is exhausted, `removeNode` unlinks it, drops it from the id map,
   decrements the owner's open-order count, and drops the level if it emptied. That closes the invariant-4 window
   inside this one call (Spec D4 revised by D44). **[Verified]**
4. It returns an **immutable copy** (`Fill`), so the engine learns the resting order's id, owner, client order
   id, price, filled quantity and leaves (what is left) without ever holding the `Order` object that sits in the
   book. **[Verified]** `Fill.java:5-9`. `level.getPrice()` is still valid after the level left the tree, because
   the object still exists.

### `cancel`: owner-only, unknown otherwise

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/book/OrderBook.java:98-108
public CancelResult cancel(String participant, long orderId) {
    OrderNode node = orders.get(orderId);
    if (node == null || !node.order.getParticipant().equals(participant)) {
        return CancelResult.unknown(orderId);
    }
    long remaining = node.order.getRemainingQuantity();
    changed.add(new LevelKey(node.order.getSide(), node.order.getPrice()));
    removeNode(node);
    return CancelResult.cancelled(orderId, remaining);
}
```

- Someone else's order looks exactly like a missing one: the result is `UNKNOWN_ORDER`, never a "not owner"
  status. A distinct status would let any participant probe which order ids are live (Spec D48). **[Verified]**
- `UNKNOWN_ORDER` is a normal return value, not an exception, because racing a fill is ordinary: you cancel an
  order that just filled (Spec D17). **[Verified]** `CancelResult.java:5`.
- An unknown cancel touches no level, so no level update is produced
  (`OrderBookTest.LevelUpdates.unknownCancelTouchesNothing`). **[Verified]**

### `removeNode`: the single removal path

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/book/OrderBook.java:111-128
private void removeNode(OrderNode node) {
    Order order = node.order;
    PriceLevel level = node.level;
    if (level == null || orders.get(order.getOrderId()) != node) {
        throw new IllegalStateException("Order " + order.getOrderId() + " is not resting");
    }
    level.unlink(node);
    orders.remove(order.getOrderId());
    openOrders.computeIfPresent(order.getParticipant(), (p, n) -> n == 1 ? null : n - 1);

    if (level.isEmpty()) {
        PriceLevel removed = sideOf(level.getSide()).remove(level.getPrice());
        if (removed != level) {
            throw new IllegalStateException("Removed the wrong level at " + ...);   // D18
        }
    }
}
```

- **One path for fills and cancels** (Spec D2), so invariants 1–3 are maintained in exactly one place.
- `computeIfPresent` returning `null` removes the map entry when the count reaches zero, which keeps invariant 3
  ("absent when 0"). **[Verified]**
- **Eager level removal** (Spec D10, D14). An emptied level leaves the tree immediately. **D18:** it checks that
  `TreeMap.remove` returned *this* level object, as a guard against a corrupted tree. **[Verified]**

### `drainLevelUpdates`: deterministic L2 output

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/book/OrderBook.java:131-144
public List<LevelUpdate> drainLevelUpdates() {
    if (changed.isEmpty()) {
        return List.of();
    }
    List<LevelUpdate> updates = new ArrayList<>(changed.size());
    for (LevelKey key : changed) {
        PriceLevel level = sideOf(key.side()).get(key.price());
        updates.add(level == null
                ? new LevelUpdate(key.side(), key.price(), 0, 0)
                : new LevelUpdate(key.side(), key.price(), level.getTotalVolume(), level.getOrderCount()));
    }
    changed.clear();
    return updates;
}
```

- `changed` is a `LinkedHashSet<LevelKey>`, so each touched level appears **once**, in the order it was **first**
  touched. Iteration order is insertion order, never hash order, which keeps outputs deterministic across runs
  and replays. **[Verified]** `OrderBook.java:45-46`. A plain `HashSet` would iterate in an order that depends on
  hash codes; still repeatable in practice for these keys, but the code avoids relying on that. **[Inferred]**
- The update carries the **absolute** state after the command (not a delta); a vanished level is reported as
  volume 0, orders 0 (`LevelUpdate.java:7`). **[Verified]**
- The engine drains once per command, so every output's level updates belong to that command only. **[Verified]**
  `MatchingEngine.onNewOrder`/`onCancel`.

### Read walks: `walk`, `restingOrders`, `headParticipant`

Self-trade prevention, cancel-all and snapshots need to see *individual* resting orders (who owns the next order
in line, which orders belong to one participant, every order in priority order). The engine must not hold the
