| `Ledger.digest()`, `engine()`, `log()` | package | `Exchange`, `Replay`, tests | writer |
| `OutputDigest.update(EngineOutput)`, `hex()`, `resume(hex)` | package | `Ledger`, `Snapshot.read` | writer |
| `OutputLog.record(Command, EngineOutput)` | package | `Ledger.apply` | writer |
| `OutputLog.lastOseq(p)`, `since(p, from)`, `lastOseqs()`, `resume(...)` | package | `Exchange.read` (Attach/Resend), `Ledger` | writer |
| `PrivateEvent` (sealed: `OrderReply`, `CancelReply`, `MakerFill`) | public | `TcpGateway.onOutput`, `Protocol.lines` | writer |
| `ResendResult(events, firstAvailable, lastOseq)` | public | `ClientSession.resend` callback | writer |
| `Snapshot.write(path)`, `read(path)`, `path(journal, seq)`, `empty()` | package | `Exchange.compact`, `Replay.snapshotFor` | tool / starting thread |

## Ledger: one unit, rebuilt together

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Ledger.java:37-61
static Ledger from(Snapshot snapshot, int retention) {
    MatchingEngine engine = snapshot.lastSequence() == 0 ? new MatchingEngine() : MatchingEngine.restore(snapshot.engineState());
    return new Ledger(engine, OutputDigest.resume(snapshot.digest()), OutputLog.resume(retention, snapshot.lastOseqs()));
}

Applied apply(Command command) {
    EngineOutput output;
    if (command instanceof NewOrderCommand n) {
        output = engine.onNewOrder(n);
    } else if (command instanceof CancelOrderCommand c) {
        output = engine.onCancel(c);
    } else if (command instanceof CancelAllCommand c) {
        output = engine.onCancelAll(c);
    } else {
        throw new IllegalStateException("Not an engine command: " + command);
    }
    digest.update(output);
    return new Applied(output, log.record(command, output));
}

Snapshot snapshot(long lastSequence, long lastOrderId, Instant lastTimestamp) {
    var state = engine.exportState();
    return new Snapshot(lastSequence, Math.max(lastOrderId, state.lastOrderId()), lastTimestamp, state.lastTradePrice(),
            digest.hex(), state.orders(), log.lastOseqs());
}
```

Line by line:
- **`from`** builds a fresh ledger either from nothing (`Snapshot.empty()` has `lastSequence` 0) or from a
  snapshot: `MatchingEngine.restore` re-adds the resting orders in book order (see guide 07 (modules/engine)), the
  digest continues from the snapshot's value, and the output log continues each participant's numbering
  **[Verified]** (`Ledger.java:37-40`).
- **`apply`** is the only way a command changes derived state. The engine runs first; *only if it returns*
  (no exception) does the digest absorb the output and the log number the events. So a poison pill (engine throws)
  leaves the digest and the log untouched, and then `Exchange` throws the whole ledger away anyway
  **[Verified]** (`Ledger.java:42-55`; `Exchange.java:278-287`).
- **`snapshot`** exports the engine state and pairs it with the digest and the per-participant last oseq.
  `Math.max(lastOrderId, state.lastOrderId())`: the replay verifier's `lastOrderId` counts NEW records that were
  *skipped* (their ids were issued even though the engine never saw them), so it can be larger than the engine's
  own counter; the snapshot must keep the larger one so an id is never reused **[Verified]** (`Ledger.java:57-61`,
  `src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Replay.java:103`).

Why one class: a poison-pill rebuild used to replace only the engine. With three derived things, replacing them one
by one would risk an engine at sequence 100 and a digest at sequence 120. Making the ledger the unit of replacement
makes that impossible by construction **[Verified — comment `Ledger.java:15-17`]**.

## OutputDigest (D70)

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/OutputDigest.java:48-83 (trimmed)
void update(EngineOutput output) {
    StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder(128);
    sb.append(output.sequenceNumber()).append('|').append(output.timestamp()).append('|');
    if (output instanceof OrderOutput o) {
        Response r = o.response();
        sb.append("O|").append(r.orderId()).append('|').append(r.clientOrderId()).append('|').append(r.status())
                .append('|').append(r.reason()).append('|').append(r.filledQuantity()).append('|')
                .append(r.remainingQuantity());
        for (Trade t : r.trades()) {
            sb.append("|T").append(t.price()).append(',').append(t.quantity()).append(',').append(t.buyOrderId())
                    .append(',').append(t.sellOrderId()).append(',').append(t.aggressorSide());
        }
        // ... passive fills as "|E...", cancels as "C|..." / "A|..."
    }
    for (LevelUpdate l : output.levelUpdates()) {
        sb.append("|L").append(l.side()).append(',').append(l.price()).append(',').append(l.volume())
                .append(',').append(l.orderCount());
    }
    sb.append('\n');
    for (byte b : sb.toString().getBytes(UTF_8)) {
        state ^= b & 0xff;
        state *= PRIME;
    }
}
```

- **What it is**: FNV-1a 64 (a simple, fast, non-cryptographic hash: XOR each byte in, multiply by a fixed prime)
  run continuously over a canonical text form of every output, in order **[Verified]** (`OutputDigest.java:17-28`).
  The state is the digest; `hex()` prints it as 16 hex digits (the format `CheckCommand` requires).
- **Canonical encoding, field by field**: the comment explains why it does not use `toString()` or `hashCode()`:
  Java does not promise their formats (a record's `hashCode` algorithm is unspecified), and a digest must be the
  same in every JVM and every future build that behaves the same **[Verified — comment `OutputDigest.java:21-22`]**.
- **What it covers**: sequence number, timestamp, the full response (id, client order id, status, reason, filled,
  remaining), every trade, every passive fill, every cancel result, every level update. So *any* behavioural
  difference visible in an output changes it **[Verified]** (`src/test/java/exchangeserver/core/OutputDigestTest.java:54`
  `eachFieldChangesTheDigest`, `:73` `cancelResultsAreInTheDigest`).
- **Order matters**: the same two outputs in the opposite order give a different digest (`OutputDigestTest.java:38`).
- **Not cryptographic**: it detects accidents (a code change), not an attacker who edits the journal and fixes up
  the CHECK records. The per-record CRC (D69) has the same scope **[Verified — comment; Inferred: the threat model]**.
- **Cost**: O(bytes of one output) per command, nothing per book size; compared with hashing the whole book at
  every CHECK, this keeps a CHECK free **[Inferred]**.

### Why digest outputs, not state? (D70)

`Rules.VERSION` catches deliberate rule changes. It does not catch an *accidental* behaviour change in code (a
different check order, a rounding change): replay would silently build a different book (risk M5 in guide 11). The
choices were (a) hash the book at each CHECK, or (b) hash every output. (b) is cheaper (no walk over the book) and
stricter: two runs can end with the same book but different trades in between, and (b) still catches that
**[Verified — Spec D70; Inferred: the "stricter" argument]**. The price: a divergence is detected only at the next
CHECK (every 10,000 commands by default), and only if it changes an output **[Verified — Spec §8]**.

## OutputLog and PrivateEvent (D73)

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/OutputLog.java:58-76
List<PrivateEvent> record(Command command, EngineOutput output) {
    List<PrivateEvent> events = new ArrayList<>();
    if (output instanceof OrderOutput o && command instanceof NewOrderCommand n) {
        String p = n.request().getParticipant();
        events.add(add(new PrivateEvent.OrderReply(p, next(p), n.request().getSide(), n.request().getQuantity(), o.response())));
        for (Execution e : o.passiveFills()) {
            events.add(add(new PrivateEvent.MakerFill(e.participant(), next(e.participant()), e)));
        }
    } else if (output instanceof CancelOutput c && command instanceof CancelOrderCommand cc) {
        events.add(add(new PrivateEvent.CancelReply(cc.participant(), next(cc.participant()), c.result(), false)));
    } else if (output instanceof CancelAllOutput a && command instanceof CancelAllCommand) {
        for (CancelResult r : a.results()) {
            events.add(add(new PrivateEvent.CancelReply(a.participant(), next(a.participant()), r, true)));
        }
    } else {
        throw new IllegalStateException("Output " + output.getClass().getSimpleName() + " does not match " + command);
    }
    return events;
}
```

- **One output, several events, each numbered in its owner's stream.** A NEW gives the submitter an `OrderReply`
  first, then one `MakerFill` per passive fill, each going to the resting order's owner. A CANCEL gives the
  canceller a `CancelReply` (also for `UNKNOWN_ORDER`). A CANCEL_ALL gives one `CancelReply` per cancelled order,
  flagged `byCancelAll` **[Verified]** (`OutputLog.java:56-76`, `PrivateEvent.java:21-31`).
- **Numbering**: `next(p)` is that participant's last oseq + 1; `add` stores it as the new last and appends it to
  the participant's bounded deque, dropping the oldest beyond `retention` **[Verified]** (`OutputLog.java:106-118`).
  Within one output, an event is `add`ed before the next one is numbered, so two events for the same participant
  (a CANCEL_ALL of 3 orders) get consecutive numbers.
- **`OrderReply` carries the request's side and original quantity**: the TAKER `FILL` lines show `side=` and a
  `leaves=` that counts down from the original quantity, and the `Response` alone has neither **[Verified]**
  (`PrivateEvent.java:21-22`; `src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/Protocol.java:148-155`).

Worked example (seen live on 2026-10-27, guide 07 (modules/gateway)): alice rests a1 (oseq 1), her a2 is cancelled
by self-trade prevention (oseq 2), bob's buy fills 4 of a1 (bob's oseq 1; alice's MakerFill oseq 3), alice
disconnects with COD and CANCEL_ALL cancels a1's remaining 6 (oseq 4). After reconnecting, `RESEND 1` returned
those four events and `RESENT last=4` **[Verified — observed]**.

### Why the numbering is deterministic

- The events are computed from `(command, output)` only, and the outputs are deterministic (guide 08).
- The `streams` map is a `HashMap`, but it is only *looked up* by participant, never iterated while producing
  events. The one place that iterates it, `lastOseqs()`, copies into a `TreeMap` (sorted), so a snapshot file has
  the same bytes whatever the hash order **[Verified]** (`OutputLog.java:25-26`, `:99-104`).
- So after a restart, replaying the journal rebuilds the same per-participant numbers and the same retained events.
  `TcpGatewayTest.Resend.theStreamSurvivesARestart` (`src/test/java/exchangeserver/gateway/TcpGatewayTest.java:598`)
  logs in after a restart and gets `WELCOME participant=alice oseq=1` and a resend of that event.

### Retention and RESEND

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/OutputLog.java:84-97
ResendResult since(String participant, long from) {
    Stream s = streams.get(participant);
    if (s == null) {
        return new ResendResult(List.of(), 1, 0);
    }
    long firstAvailable = s.recent.isEmpty() ? s.lastOseq + 1 : s.recent.peekFirst().oseq();
    List<PrivateEvent> events = new ArrayList<>();
    for (PrivateEvent e : s.recent) {
        if (e.oseq() >= from) {
            events.add(e);
        }
    }
    return new ResendResult(events, firstAvailable, s.lastOseq);
}
```

- Only the last `retention` events per participant are kept (`Exchange.Options.outputRetention`, default 1,000;
  `--resend-window` in `Main`) **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Exchange.java:62-74`).
- `firstAvailable` tells the gateway whether the request reaches further back than what is kept; the gateway then
  sends `ERROR RESEND_GAP first=<n>` before the events **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/ClientSession.java:353-355`).
- After a snapshot, `resume` restores each participant's *number* but no events: `firstAvailable` becomes
  `lastOseq + 1`, so RESEND never reaches back past the last compaction **[Verified]** (`OutputLog.java:49-54`;
  `ExchangeTest.Compaction.outputNumberingContinuesAcrossACompaction`, `src/test/java/exchangeserver/core/ExchangeTest.java:459`).
- Memory: retention x number of participants. Participants are bounded by the users file (D74) **[Inferred]**.

## Snapshot (D71)

```
SNAPSHOT,1,<lastSequence>,<lastOrderId>,<lastTimestamp or ->,<lastTradePrice>,<digest>,<crc>
ORDER,<orderId>,<participant>,<clientOrderId>,<side>,<type>,<price>,<originalQty>,<filledQty>,<timestamp>,<crc>
OSEQ,<participant>,<lastOseq>,<crc>
END,<orderCount>,<participantCount>,<crc>
```

A real one, written by `--compact` in the live check (no resting orders; two participants):

```
SNAPSHOT,1,4,3,2026-10-27T14:20:42.045241200Z,100,88a18b6a6365ebe4,d7e6a970
OSEQ,alice,4,d1453b0b
OSEQ,bob,1,3d136969
END,0,2,6a6d3c70
```

**[Verified — observed]**.

- **File name** `<journal>.snapshot-<lastSequence>`: each journal's header names the base it needs, so several
  snapshots can coexist and a half-done compaction never leaves a journal pointing at a missing or wrong snapshot
  **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Snapshot.java:60-62`; Spec D71).
- **ORDER lines in book priority** (bids best first, then asks, time priority within a level): loading them in
  order restores time priority **[Verified]** (`Snapshot.java:36-38`).
- **Every line sealed with the journal's CRC-32C** (`Checksums.crcHex`), **an END line with counts**, and a write
  through `Durable.writeAtomically` (temp file, fsync, atomic rename, directory fsync) **[Verified]**
  (`Snapshot.java:64-77`, `:118-120`).
- **No rules version inside**: a snapshot is *state*, not commands to re-run, so a build with different rules can
  load it. That is how a `Rules.VERSION` change is crossed: compact with the old build, start the new one on the
  empty journal **[Verified — comment `Snapshot.java:37-38`, Spec D71]**.

### Reading and validation

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Snapshot.java:79-116 (trimmed)
static Snapshot read(Path file) throws IOException {
    if (!Files.exists(file)) {
        throw new IllegalStateException("Snapshot " + file + " is missing: the journal continues from it");
    }
    List<String> lines = Files.readAllLines(file, UTF_8);
    int n = 0;
    try {
        String[] h = fields(lines, n++, "SNAPSHOT", 7);
        // ... ORDER lines while they start with "ORDER,", OSEQ lines while they start with "OSEQ,"
        String[] end = fields(lines, n++, "END", 3);
        if (Long.parseLong(end[1]) != orders.size() || Long.parseLong(end[2]) != lastOseqs.size() || n != lines.size()) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("END does not match the records before it");
        }
        return new Snapshot(lastSequence, lastOrderId, lastTimestamp, lastTradePrice, digest, orders, lastOseqs);
    } catch (IllegalArgumentException | DateTimeParseException | IndexOutOfBoundsException e) {
        throw new IllegalStateException("Corrupt snapshot " + file + " at line " + n + ": " + e.getMessage(), e);
    }
}
```

- `fields` checks the CRC, the record type and the field count of each line (`Snapshot.java:122-136`).
- The reported line number is 1-based: `n` was already incremented when the failing `fields` call threw. The test
  first expected "line 1" for a corrupt first ORDER line and was corrected to "line 2" (header = line 1)
  **[Verified]** (`src/test/java/exchangeserver/core/SnapshotTest.java:46`).
- A corrupt snapshot stops startup; there is no fallback (no other state is trustworthy) **[Verified]**
  (`ExchangeTest.Compaction.aCorruptSnapshotStopsStartup`, `ExchangeTest.java:532`).
- `Replay.snapshotFor` also checks the snapshot's own `lastSequence` equals the header's base
  (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Replay.java:59-69`).

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    A["open(journal): header base B"] --> B{"B == 0?"}
    B -- yes --> C["Snapshot.empty(): nothing restored"]
    B -- no --> D["read journal.snapshot-B"]
    D --> E{"exists, every CRC ok, END counts match?"}
    E -- no --> F["IllegalStateException: startup fails"]
    E -- yes --> G{"lastSequence == B?"}
    G -- no --> F
    G -- yes --> H["Ledger.from(snapshot): restore engine, resume digest and numbering"]
    C --> I["replay the journal from B+1"]
    H --> I
```

## How it is tested

| Test class | What it pins down |
|---|---|
| `OutputLogTest` (`src/test/java/exchangeserver/core/OutputLogTest.java:26`) | numbering per participant with the requester first (`:41`), one number per CANCEL_ALL cancel (`:55`), retention and `firstAvailable` (`:64`), resuming after a snapshot (`:80`), a mismatched output is a bug (`:91`) |
| `OutputDigestTest` (`src/test/java/exchangeserver/core/OutputDigestTest.java:20`) | same outputs = same digest, order matters (`:38`); every output kind contributes (`:45`); every field changes it (`:54`); cancel results are in it (`:73`); resuming from hex (`:83`) |
| `SnapshotTest` (`src/test/java/exchangeserver/core/SnapshotTest.java:22`) | round trip and sorted OSEQ (`:37`); a changed character (`:46`); truncation caught by END (`:56`); a disagreeing END (`:65`); a missing file is explained (`:76`) |
| `ReplayTest.Check` (`src/test/java/exchangeserver/core/ReplayTest.java:183`) | a matching CHECK passes, a different one is DIVERGED, a different output changes the digest |
| `ExchangeTest.Compaction` (`ExchangeTest.java:412`) | restart from a snapshot rebuilds the same state, client-order-id scoping, numbering across compaction, chained compactions, crash after the snapshot write, rules crossing, poison rebuild from a snapshot, corrupt snapshot |

PIT (mutation testing) mutates this package. Its survivors here are explained in Spec §7: e.g. boundary mutants in
`Snapshot.fields`/`read` that only turn one `IllegalStateException` into another **[Verified — Spec §7]**.

## Key takeaways

- The ledger = engine + output digest + per-participant private streams; all three are pure functions of the
  commands and are always replaced together.
- `OutputDigest` is FNV-1a 64 over a field-by-field encoding of every output; CHECK records let replay prove it
  reproduced the live run (D70). It catches accidents, not attacks.
- `OutputLog` numbers each participant's events (oseq) deterministically; the requester's reply comes first, maker
  fills follow in match order (D73).
- RESEND reaches back `outputRetention` events and never past the last compaction.
- A snapshot is state (no rules inside), so it crosses a `Rules.VERSION` change; it is named by sequence number,
  sealed line by line, ends with an END record, and is written atomically (D71).
- A snapshot's `lastOrderId` is the larger of the verifier's and the engine's, so skipped NEW ids are never reused.

## Common confusions

- **"The digest is a hash of the book."** It is a hash of the *outputs* in order; two runs with the same final book
  but different trades along the way have different digests.
- **"A snapshot keeps the private events."** It keeps only each participant's last number; the retained events are
  rebuilt by replaying the journal *since* the snapshot.
- **"oseq is global."** It is per participant: alice's 3 and bob's 3 are unrelated.
- **"Market data is in the private stream."** TRADE and LEVEL lines are not numbered by oseq and have no resend; a
  subscriber re-SUBSCRIBEs for a fresh BOOK.
- **"Loading a snapshot re-checks the rules."** It does not; it re-adds resting orders through `OrderBook.add`
  (which checks book invariants) but not through the engine's rule checks.

## Questions to test yourself

1. Why must `Ledger.apply` update the digest only after the engine returns?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>If the engine throws (a poison pill), the live run produced no output for that command and the command is quarantined; replay skips it. Updating the digest first would make the live digest include an output replay never produces, and the next CHECK would report a false divergence. (Exchange also throws the ledger away after a poison pill, so nothing half-updated survives.)</details>

2. alice has oseq 5. A CANCEL_ALL cancels three of her orders. What are their numbers, and what does `RESEND 4` return if retention is 2?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>6, 7, 8. With retention 2 only 7 and 8 are kept, so `firstAvailable` = 7: the gateway sends `ERROR RESEND_GAP first=7`, then the events 7 and 8 marked `resend=1`, then `RESENT last=8`.</details>

3. Why does `lastOseqs()` return a `TreeMap`?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>The snapshot writes one OSEQ line per participant. Iterating the HashMap would make the file's line order depend on hash order; sorting makes the bytes deterministic (and the END count is easy to check).</details>

4. You change the engine so a POST_ONLY at the participant cap gets BOOK_FULL instead of MAX_OPEN_ORDERS, without bumping `Rules.VERSION`. What happens on the next restart with an old journal that contains such a reject?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>The replayed output differs (reason field), so the digest differs; the next CHECK after that command stops replay with `ReplayException(DIVERGED)`, and `Main` prints the compaction hint. Before D70 the book would have been silently identical here (a reject changes no book state) but the outputs differed.</details>

5. Compaction wrote `x.journal.snapshot-40` and crashed before replacing the journal. What does the next start use?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>The old journal is still in place with its old header base (say 0), so replay starts from nothing (or from its own older snapshot) and ignores snapshot-40; the stray file is harmless and is overwritten by the next compaction.</details>


---

<a id="guide-07-modules-exchange-service"></a>

<!-- source: docs/understanding/07-modules/exchange-service.md -->

# 07 — Module: core (ExchangeService, the single-writer thread)

> **Project:** ExchangeServer2 | Single-instrument limit order book: price-time matching engine, write-ahead journal with replay, single writer thread, TCP/TLS gateway with authenticated participants | Stack: Java 17, Maven wrapper, JUnit 5 (+ JMH, PIT)
> **This guide:** `core.ExchangeService`: the writer thread, the hand-in queue and its lock, `Settings`, the four states, batching (smart and linger), halting, the future hand-off (D61), callbacks (logged if they throw, run after the listener), and the stats | **Prerequisites:** 01, 02, 05, 07-modules/exchange-and-replay | **Related:** 05a, 05b, 07-modules/gateway, 08, 10
> **Repo layout summary:** `pom.xml` + Maven wrapper (`mvnw`, `mvnw.cmd`) at the root; production code in `src/main/java/exchangeserver/` (packages `domain`, `book`, `engine`, `command`, `journal`, `core`, `gateway`, `client`, plus `Main`); tests mirror it in `src/test/java/exchangeserver/` (plus `bench/` JMH benchmarks and `testing/MutableClock`); design docs in `docs/` (`ExchangeServer2_Vision.md` = purpose/roadmap, `ExchangeServer2_Spec.md` = decisions D1–D77); these guides in `docs/understanding/`.

## Overview

`ExchangeService` turns the single-threaded `Exchange` (guide 07 (modules/exchange-and-replay)) into something any
number of threads can use. Exactly one thread, `exchange-writer`, owns the `Exchange` (sequencer, journal, ledger,
book); every other thread hands in requests through one queue and gets a result back through a
`CompletableFuture` or a callback **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/ExchangeService.java:22-49`).

Terms:
- **Single writer**: only one thread ever touches the mutable state, so the engine needs no locks.
- **Smart batching** (D52): the writer takes whatever is already queued (up to `maxBatchSize`) and journals it with
  one fsync. **Linger** (group commit with a timeout) is kept only for the benchmark.
- **Backpressure**: refusing with `BUSY` instead of queueing without limit.
- **Happens-before**: the Java memory-model rule that makes one thread's writes visible to another (e.g. a queue's
  put -> take, `Thread.start`, `Thread.join`, a future's complete -> get).

What changed on 2026-10-27: `Settings` carries `Exchange.Options` (CHECK interval, RESEND window); `recovery()`
(L1); new requests `cancelAll`, `attach`, `resend`; callbacks are wrapped so a throw is logged (M9); `stop()` is
refused on the writer thread (L6); the marker is renamed `stopMarker`; callbacks run *after* the listener (D76);
the stats are documented as lagging the replies.

## Dependencies

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    Main --> ExchangeService
    TcpGateway["gateway (ClientSession, TcpGateway)"] --> ExchangeService
    Tests --> ExchangeService
    ExchangeService --> Exchange
    ExchangeService --> Pending
    ExchangeService --> Request
    ExchangeService --> OutputListener
    ExchangeService --> ExchangeException
```

## Interface

| Member | Caller | Thread it runs on |
|---|---|---|
| `start(path, listener, settings)` (public); `start(..., interceptor)` (package, tests) | `Main`, tests, benchmark | caller: opens and replays, then starts the writer |
| `submit`, `cancel`, `cancelAll`, `snapshot`, `resend` -> `CompletableFuture` | tests, programmatic clients | caller enqueues; result completed from the common pool |
| `submit`, `cancel`, `cancelAll`, `snapshot`, `attach`, `resend` with a `BiConsumer` callback | gateway | callback on the writer (or the caller if refused) |
| `stop(timeout)`, `awaitTermination()` | `Main`'s shutdown hook, tests | caller (never the writer) |
| `state()`, `haltCause()`, `recovery()`, `batches()`, `processed()`, `largestBatch()` | `Main`, tests | any |

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/ExchangeService.java:59-71 (trimmed)
public record Settings(int queueCapacity, int maxBatchSize, long lingerNanos, Clock clock, Exchange.Options exchange) {

    public Settings {
        Objects.requireNonNull(clock, "clock");
        Objects.requireNonNull(exchange, "exchange");
        if (queueCapacity <= 0 || maxBatchSize <= 0 || lingerNanos < 0) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("Bad settings");
        }
    }

    public static Settings defaults() {
        return new Settings(100_000, 1_000, 0, Clock.systemUTC(), Exchange.Options.defaults());
    }
```

`Exchange.Options` = `(checkInterval 10,000, outputRetention 1,000)`; `Main` sets them from `--check-interval` and
`--resend-window` with `withExchangeOptions` **[Verified]** (`ExchangeService.java:89-91`;
`src/main/java/exchangeserver/Main.java:141-142`). The old two-argument `start(path, listener)` was removed (no
caller left).

## Starting

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/ExchangeService.java:130-141
static ExchangeService start(Path journalFile, OutputListener listener, Settings settings,
                             CommandInterceptor interceptor) throws IOException {
    Exchange exchange = Exchange.open(journalFile, settings.clock(), interceptor, settings.exchange());
    ExchangeService service = new ExchangeService(exchange, Objects.requireNonNull(listener, "listener"), settings);
    service.writer.start();
    return service;
}

// What recovery did at start (L1). Built on the starting thread before the writer existed; immutable.
public Exchange.Recovery recovery() {
    return recovery;
}
```

- Replay runs on the *calling* thread inside `Exchange.open`; `Thread.start()` is the happens-before edge that
  hands the replayed state to the writer **[Verified]** (`ExchangeService.java:124-125`).
- The constructor copies `exchange.recovery()` into a final field, so any thread can read it later without touching
  the writer's objects **[Verified]** (`ExchangeService.java:114-122`). `Main` prints it.

## Hand-in: one lock for "refused" and "never lost"

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/ExchangeService.java:211-238
private <R> CompletableFuture<R> enqueue(Pending<R> pending, BiConsumer<? super R, ? super Throwable> onDone) {
    if (onDone != null) {
        pending.result.whenComplete((value, failure) -> {
            // whenComplete would capture a throw in a future nobody reads: log it instead (M9).
            try {
                onDone.accept(value, failure);
            } catch (RuntimeException | Error callbackBug) {
                LOG.log(Level.ERROR, "Request callback failed", callbackBug);
            }
        });
    }
    ExchangeException refusal = null;
    synchronized (lock) {
        if (state == State.HALTED) {
            refusal = new ExchangeException(ExchangeException.Reason.HALTED, "exchange halted", haltCause);
        } else if (state != State.RUNNING) {
            refusal = new ExchangeException(ExchangeException.Reason.STOPPED, "exchange is stopping");
        } else if (queue.size() >= settings.queueCapacity()) {
            refusal = new ExchangeException(ExchangeException.Reason.BUSY, "queue full");
        } else {
            queue.add(pending);
        }
    }
    if (refusal != null) {
        pending.fail(refusal);          // outside the lock: callbacks must not run while holding it
    }
    return pending.result;
}
```

- **State check + enqueue under one lock**, and `stop()` flips the state and enqueues the stop marker under the
  same lock, so every accepted request is ahead of the marker: "refused" and "never lost" both hold (D56).
- **The queue is unbounded; capacity is checked under the lock**, so `stop()` can always enqueue its marker even
  when the queue is "full" (D56).
- **M9, new**: a callback is attached with `whenComplete`. `whenComplete` returns a *new* future that captures any
  exception the callback throws; nobody reads that future, so a gateway bug used to vanish (the client just got no
  reply). The wrapper logs it. Tested by capturing the JUL log record (`System.Logger` goes to
  `java.util.logging`) **[Verified]** (`src/test/java/exchangeserver/core/ExchangeServiceTest.java:564`
  `aThrowingCallbackIsLoggedAndTheExchangeCarriesOn`).
- **Where callbacks run**: on the writer, *inside* `Exchange.process` right after that command's listener call
  (D76); for a read, at the read's place in the batch, before any later output (that is how SUBSCRIBE and LOGIN
  join a stream with no gap); on the caller's thread if refused **[Verified]** (`ExchangeService.java:182-185`).
  The order "listener, then callback" is pinned by `listenerRunsOnTheWriterBeforeTheCallback`
  (`ExchangeServiceTest.java:240`); before D76 it was the reverse.

### Future variants and D61

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/ExchangeService.java:170-180
private static <R> CompletableFuture<R> handOff(CompletableFuture<R> internal) {
    CompletableFuture<R> mine = new CompletableFuture<>();
    internal.whenCompleteAsync((value, failure) -> {
        if (failure != null) {
            mine.completeExceptionally(failure);
        } else {
            mine.complete(value);
        }
    });
    return mine;
}
```

The caller never gets the writer's own future: continuations it attaches run on the common pool (they cannot stall
the writer), and cancelling it cannot make the writer skip a journalled command, because the writer decides from
`Pending.refused`, never from a future's state (D61) **[Verified]** (`ExchangeServiceTest.HandIn`, `:196`, `:218`).

## The writer loop

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/ExchangeService.java:286-323
private void run() {
    List<Pending<?>> batch = new ArrayList<>(settings.maxBatchSize());
    Throwable failure = null;
    try {
        boolean stopping = false;
        while (!stopping) {
            batch.clear();
            batch.add(queue.take());
            queue.drainTo(batch, settings.maxBatchSize() - 1);
            if (settings.lingerNanos() > 0) {
                linger(batch);
            }
            stopping = batch.remove(stopMarker);    // always last: nothing is accepted after it
            if (!batch.isEmpty()) {
                exchange.process(batch, listener);
                batches++;
                processed += batch.size();
                largestBatch = Math.max(largestBatch, batch.size());
            }
        }
    } catch (Throwable t) {
        failure = t;
    } finally {
        if (failure != null) {
            halt(failure, batch);
        }
        try {
            exchange.close();
        } catch (IOException e) {
            LOG.log(Level.WARNING, "Closing the journal failed", e);
        }
        if (failure == null) {
            synchronized (lock) {
                state = State.STOPPED;
            }
        }
    }
}
```

- **Smart batching**: block for the first request, then take whatever else is waiting, up to `maxBatchSize`.
- **`stopMarker`** (renamed from the constant-looking instance field `STOP`, a smell in guide 11) is a per-instance
  `Pending` compared by identity; it is always the last element because nothing is accepted after it
  **[Verified]** (`ExchangeService.java:96`, `:298`).
- **Stats lag the replies**: `batches`/`processed`/`largestBatch` are updated *after* `process` returned, i.e.
  after the batch's futures completed. A caller holding a result may see the previous values; after `stop()`
  returns true (the writer's `join`) they are final. That race made `waitingRequestsShareOneBatch` flaky; the tests
  now read stats after `stop()` **[Verified]** (`ExchangeService.java:107-112`; Spec §10).
- **Only the writer closes the journal**, as its last act (D58); the state becomes STOPPED after a clean end.

### Linger (benchmark only)

`linger` keeps polling with a deadline until the batch is full, the deadline passes or the marker arrives
(`ExchangeService.java:325-339`). It exists so `BatchingBenchmark` can compare group commit with smart batching
(Spec §5); production uses `lingerNanos` 0 **[Verified]**.

## States, stop and halt

```mermaid
stateDiagram-v2
    [*] --> RUNNING: start()
    RUNNING --> STOPPING: stop() adds the stop marker under the lock
    STOPPING --> STOPPED: writer reaches the marker, closes the journal
    RUNNING --> HALTED: writer catches a Throwable
    STOPPING --> HALTED: writer catches a Throwable while draining
    STOPPED --> [*]
    HALTED --> [*]
```

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/ExchangeService.java:242-257
// Refuses new requests, lets the writer finish everything already accepted, and waits for it to end.
// Idempotent: later callers just wait. Returns false if the writer did not end within the timeout.
// Not from the writer thread (a listener or callback): it would wait for its own end.
public boolean stop(Duration timeout) throws InterruptedException {
    if (Thread.currentThread() == writer) {
        throw new IllegalStateException("stop() called on the writer thread: it would wait for itself");
    }
    synchronized (lock) {
        if (state == State.RUNNING) {
            state = State.STOPPING;
            queue.add(stopMarker);
        }
    }
    writer.join(Math.max(1, timeout.toMillis()));
    return !writer.isAlive();
}
```

- **L6, new**: called from a listener or callback (both run on the writer), `stop()` used to `join` its own thread
  and wait out the timeout. It now throws at once; the state is untouched **[Verified]**
  (`ExchangeServiceTest.Robustness.stopFromTheWriterThreadIsRefused`, `ExchangeServiceTest.java:597`).
- `Math.max(1, …)` avoids `join(0)`, which means "wait forever" in Java.
- **Halt** (`ExchangeService.java:341-359`): log, set HALTED and the cause under the lock (so `enqueue` refuses
  from then on and the drain is final), fail every request in the batch (no-op for completed ones) and everything
  still queued. `Main` exits 2.
- **During shutdown, sessions still get their replies.** Requests accepted before the marker are processed; their
  callbacks run after their lines went out (D76). The gateway's in-flight count relies on that (guide 07
  (modules/gateway)).

## How it is tested

`ExchangeServiceTest` (`src/test/java/exchangeserver/core/ExchangeServiceTest.java:42`); every wait has a timeout,
and the stats are read only after `stop()` (comment at the top of the class). The `Gate` interceptor holds the
writer inside the engine step to shape batches and races (`:88-109`).

| Group | Covers |
|---|---|
| `HandIn` (`:113`) | many threads each get their own result; reads in queue order; replay at start; null refused on the caller's thread; cancelling the caller's future does not skip a journalled command (D61); continuations run off the writer; listener before callback (D76) |
| `Stop` (`:260`) | drain then refuse; idempotent and releases the journal; `false` on timeout; racing `stop()` never loses a request (the spin-wait now has a deadline, L5) |
| `Backpressure` (`:379`) | BUSY at capacity, nothing journalled |
| `Failures` (`:406`) | poison quarantined; an `Error` halts and everyone waiting is told; queued requests refused on halt |
| `Batching` (`:482`) | waiting requests share a batch; max batch 1; linger collects late arrivals; stop during linger drains |
| `Robustness` (`:560`) | a throwing callback is logged (M9); `stop()` on the writer refused (L6); `cancelAll` and `resend` through the queue; `recovery()` after a restart |

## Key takeaways

- One writer thread owns everything; callers reach state only through the queue (reads included).
- State check + enqueue and `stop()` share one lock: every accepted request is processed or refused, never lost.
- Callers get their own future (D61); gateway callbacks run on the writer, after the request's listener call (D76).
- A throwing callback is now logged (M9); `stop()` from the writer is refused (L6).
- The stats lag the replies; read them after `stop()`.
- `Settings` carries `Exchange.Options` (CHECK interval, RESEND window); `recovery()` reports what start-up replay did.

## Common confusions

- **"The callback runs before the market data."** Since D76 the listener (private lines and market data) runs
  first; the callback follows and only reports refusals and does bookkeeping.
- **"`stop()` cancels queued requests."** It refuses *new* ones; everything already accepted is processed.
- **"A full queue blocks the caller."** It refuses with BUSY; nothing waits.
- **"`attach` is a command."** It is a read (no journal record); its value is the participant's latest oseq.

## Questions to test yourself

1. A gateway callback throws a `NullPointerException`. What does the client see, and what is logged?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>Whatever the callback had already sent; the exception is caught by the wrapper in `enqueue` and logged as "Request callback failed" at ERROR. The exchange carries on; the command itself was already processed and its lines already sent by the listener.</details>

2. Why can `stop()` always enqueue its marker, even when the queue holds `queueCapacity` requests?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>The queue itself is an unbounded `LinkedBlockingQueue`; the capacity is a check `enqueue` makes under the lock. `stop()` does not make that check.</details>

3. A test reads `service.processed()` right after its last future completes and sees a smaller number than expected. Bug?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>No: the writer updates the stats after `process` returns, i.e. after the futures completed. Read them after `stop()` returns true (the join is the happens-before edge).</details>

4. A listener calls `service.stop(Duration.ofSeconds(1))`. What happens now, and what happened before L6?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>Now: `IllegalStateException` at once, state unchanged. Before: the writer thread joined itself and blocked for the full timeout, stalling the whole exchange, then returned false.</details>


---

<a id="guide-07-modules-gateway"></a>

<!-- source: docs/understanding/07-modules/gateway.md -->

# 07 — Module: gateway (TcpGateway, ClientSession, Protocol, Credentials, Tls)

> **Project:** ExchangeServer2 | Single-instrument limit order book: price-time matching engine, write-ahead journal with replay, single writer thread, TCP/TLS gateway with authenticated participants | Stack: Java 17, Maven wrapper, JUnit 5 (+ JMH, PIT)
> **This guide:** `exchangeserver.gateway`: the line protocol (`Protocol`, `ClientMessage`, `ProtocolException`), the acceptor, routing and settings (`TcpGateway`), one connection's two threads with login, limits, timeouts, RESEND and close-after-replies (`ClientSession`), authentication (`Credentials`) and TLS (`Tls`) | **Prerequisites:** 01, 02, 05a, 07-modules/exchange-service | **Related:** 05b, 06, 07-modules/ledger-and-snapshots, 07-modules/client-and-main, 08, 11
> **Repo layout summary:** `pom.xml` + Maven wrapper (`mvnw`, `mvnw.cmd`) at the root; production code in `src/main/java/exchangeserver/` (packages `domain`, `book`, `engine`, `command`, `journal`, `core`, `gateway`, `client`, plus `Main`); tests mirror it in `src/test/java/exchangeserver/` (plus `bench/` JMH benchmarks and `testing/MutableClock`); design docs in `docs/` (`ExchangeServer2_Vision.md` = purpose/roadmap, `ExchangeServer2_Spec.md` = decisions D1–D77); these guides in `docs/understanding/`.

## Overview

The gateway is the only part of the system that talks to the outside world. It accepts TCP (optionally TLS)
connections, gives each one a **reader** thread (parse, check, hand in) and a **sender** thread (bounded queue ->
socket), and implements `OutputListener`, so the exchange's writer thread hands it every command's output to route
**[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/TcpGateway.java:30-50`).

On 2026-10-27 the four deferred security findings and the related risks were fixed here (Spec D72–D77):
- **Authentication and transport (D74):** loopback bind by default; `LOGIN <participant> <secret> [COD]` checked
  against salted PBKDF2 hashes; one attempt per connection; TLS 1.3/1.2 optional.
- **Per-session limits (D75):** login and idle timeouts; at most `maxInFlight` requests in flight; a token-bucket
  rate limit.
- **A numbered private stream per participant (D73):** replies and fills carry `oseq=`, go to *every* session of the
  participant, and can be re-requested with `RESEND`.
- **Cancel-on-disconnect (D72)** when the last `COD` session of a participant closes.
- **Close after the replies, also during shutdown (D76),** replacing the D63 barrier.
- **"Exchange is starting"** for clients that connect during replay (L6), and alarming accept failures.

Terms: **session** = one connection; **participant** = the trading identity a session logs in as; **oseq** = a
participant's private event number; **COD** = cancel-on-disconnect; **in flight** = handed to the exchange and not
yet completed.

## Dependencies and threads

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    Client["TCP / TLS client"] <--> Reader["session-N-reader"]
    Sender["session-N-sender"] --> Client
    Acceptor["gateway-acceptor"] -->|"new ClientSession"| Reader
    Reader -->|"submit / cancel / attach / resend / snapshot"| Service["core.ExchangeService"]
    Writer["exchange-writer"] -->|"onOutput(output, events)"| Gateway["TcpGateway"]
    Writer -->|"callbacks"| Reader
    Gateway -->|"send(): offer only"| Sender
    Reader --> Credentials
    Gateway --> Tls
```

The writer thread only ever *offers* lines to bounded session queues; a slow client is disconnected, never waited
for (D54) **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/ClientSession.java:115-124`).

## Interface

| Member | Caller | Thread |
|---|---|---|
| `new TcpGateway(Settings)` (binds) | `Main`, tests | caller |
| `startAccepting()`, `attach(service)`, `start(service)` | `Main`, tests | caller |
| `onOutput(EngineOutput, List<PrivateEvent>)` | `Exchange.process` | writer |
| `close()` | `Main` shutdown hook, tests | caller |
| `port()` | `Main`, tests | any |
| package: `authenticate`, `loggedIn`, `removed`, `subscribe`, `unsubscribe`, `service()`, `settings()` | `ClientSession` | reader / writer / sender |
| package test hooks: `participantsRegistered()`, `subscriberCount()`, `drainingSessions()` | tests | any |
| `Credentials.load`, `of`, `addUser`, `verify` | `Main`, `TcpGateway`, tests | any (immutable) |
| `Tls.serverContext`, `clientContext`, `passwordFromEnvironment` | `Main`, `ClientMain`, tests | caller |

## TcpGateway

### Settings

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/TcpGateway.java:52-71 (trimmed)
public record Settings(InetAddress bindAddress, int port, Credentials credentials, SSLContext tls, int maxSessions,
                       int outboundCapacity, Duration loginTimeout, Duration idleTimeout, int maxInFlight,
                       double ratePerSecond, int burst) {

    public static Settings defaults(int port, Credentials credentials) {
        return new Settings(InetAddress.getLoopbackAddress(), port, credentials, null, 256, 10_000,
                Duration.ofSeconds(10), Duration.ofMinutes(5), 1_000, 1_000, 2_000);
    }
```

| Setting | Default | `Main` flag | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|
| bind address | 127.0.0.1 | `--bind` | D74 |
| credentials | required | `--users` | D74 |
| TLS context | none | `--tls-keystore` (password in `ES2_TLS_PASSWORD`) | D74 |
| max sessions / outbound lines | 256 / 10,000 | (none) | D54 |
| login / idle timeout | 10 s / 5 min | `--login-timeout`, `--idle-timeout` | D75 |
| max in flight | 1,000 | `--max-in-flight` | D75 |
| rate / burst | 1,000 per s / 2,000 | `--rate`, `--burst` | D75 |

`with...` methods return modified copies; the compact constructor rejects non-positive values and zero or negative
timeouts **[Verified]** (`TcpGateway.java:56-96`).

### Construction: TLS or plain, bound to one address

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/TcpGateway.java:113-129
public TcpGateway(Settings settings) throws IOException {
    this.settings = Objects.requireNonNull(settings, "settings");
    if (settings.tls() == null) {
        this.server = new ServerSocket();
    } else {
        SSLServerSocket tls = (SSLServerSocket) settings.tls().getServerSocketFactory().createServerSocket();
        tls.setEnabledProtocols(Tls.PROTOCOLS);
        this.server = tls;
    }
    try {
        server.setReuseAddress(true);
        server.bind(new InetSocketAddress(settings.bindAddress(), settings.port()));
    } catch (IOException e) {
        server.close();
        throw e;
    }
}
```

- Loopback by default (risk H1 was "binds every interface, anyone on the LAN can log in as anyone"). `Main` warns
  when `--bind` is not loopback and TLS is off **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/Main.java:127-129`).
- With TLS, an `SSLServerSocket` restricted to TLS 1.3 and 1.2. The handshake happens lazily on the session's
  first read or write, so it runs on the session's threads, not the acceptor **[Verified — code; Inferred: JSSE's
  lazy handshake]**.

### Start order: accept before replay (L6)

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/TcpGateway.java:139-158
// Accepts connections from now on; until attach(), sessions are told the exchange is starting (L6).
public synchronized void startAccepting() {
    if (acceptor != null) {
        throw new IllegalStateException("Already accepting");
    }
    acceptor = new Thread(this::acceptLoop, "gateway-acceptor");
    acceptor.setDaemon(true);
    acceptor.start();
}

public void attach(ExchangeService exchangeService) {
    this.service = Objects.requireNonNull(exchangeService, "exchangeService");
}

public synchronized void start(ExchangeService exchangeService) {
    attach(exchangeService);
    if (acceptor == null) {
        startAccepting();
    }
}
```

`Main` binds and starts accepting *before* the (possibly long) replay, then attaches the service. A session that
sends anything but PING/QUIT while `service` is still null gets `ERROR exchange is starting; retry shortly` and is
closed, instead of hanging in the OS backlog **[Verified]** (`ClientSession.java:228-232`;
`src/test/java/exchangeserver/gateway/TcpGatewayTest.java:316` `beforeTheExchangeIsAttachedSessionsAreToldItIsStarting`).

### The acceptor: back off, alarm if it persists

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/TcpGateway.java:164-186 (trimmed)
private void acceptLoop() {
    int failures = 0;
    while (!server.isClosed()) {
        Socket socket;
        try {
            socket = server.accept();
            failures = 0;
        } catch (IOException e) {
            if (server.isClosed()) {
                return;
            }
            failures++;
            LOG.log(failures >= ALARM_AFTER_FAILURES ? Level.ERROR : Level.WARNING,
                    "accept failed " + failures + " time(s) in a row; retrying", e);
            try {
                Thread.sleep(Math.min(MAX_BACKOFF_MILLIS, MIN_BACKOFF_MILLIS << Math.min(failures - 1, 6)));
            } catch (InterruptedException interrupted) {
                return;
            }
            continue;
        }
```

Exponential backoff 100, 200, 400 ... capped at 5 s, and ERROR instead of WARNING from the 10th consecutive failure
(L6: it used to retry every 100 ms forever with a warning each time). Over the session limit, the client gets
`ERROR too many sessions` (with a 2 s socket timeout, so a TLS handshake cannot stall the acceptor) and is closed
**[Verified]** (`TcpGateway.java:187-200`).

### Routing private events and market data

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/TcpGateway.java:285-310 (trimmed)
@Override
public void onOutput(EngineOutput output, List<PrivateEvent> events) {
    for (PrivateEvent e : events) {
        Set<ClientSession> owners = sessionsByParticipant.get(e.participant());
        if (owners != null) {
            List<String> lines = Protocol.lines(e, false);
            for (ClientSession s : owners) {
                s.sendAll(lines);
            }
        }
    }

    if (subscribers.isEmpty() || (output.trades().isEmpty() && output.levelUpdates().isEmpty())) {
        return;
    }
    // ... TRADE and LEVEL lines to every subscriber
}
```

- **Every** session of the participant gets the private stream, replies included. Before D73 the reply went only to
  the requesting session; one numbering per participant only makes sense if every session sees every number
  **[Verified]** (`TcpGatewayTest.Trading.everySessionOfAParticipantSeesItsWholeStream`, `TcpGatewayTest.java:483`).
- The events arrive in oseq order, requester's reply first, then maker fills (guide 07
  (modules/ledger-and-snapshots)). A participant with no open session still gets numbers; the events wait in the
  exchange's output log for RESEND.
- Market data (`TRADE`, `LEVEL`) goes to subscribers after the private lines.

### removed(): unregister, and cancel-on-disconnect (D72)

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/TcpGateway.java:239-263
void removed(ClientSession session) {
    sessions.remove(session);
    subscribers.remove(session);
    String p = session.participant();
    if (p == null) {
        return;
    }
    // Drop the participant's entry with its last session, so ever-new names cannot grow the map.
    boolean[] wasLast = {false};
    sessionsByParticipant.computeIfPresent(p, (k, mine) -> {
        mine.remove(session);
        wasLast[0] = mine.isEmpty();
        return mine.isEmpty() ? null : mine;
    });
    ExchangeService s = service;
    if (wasLast[0] && session.cancelOnDisconnect() && s != null) {
        // D72: the participant's last session is gone and it asked for this. A journalled CANCEL_ALL, so the
        // replies wait in its private stream for RESEND.
        s.cancelAll(p, (results, failure) -> {
            if (failure != null) {
                LOG.log(Level.WARNING, "cancel-on-disconnect for " + p + " failed: " + failure);
            }
        });
    }
}
```

- `computeIfPresent` is atomic per key, so "was this the last session" is decided in the same step that removes
  the entry **[Verified]** (`TcpGateway.java:221-229`, `:246-252`).
- Only the *closing* session's own `COD` flag counts. A clean shutdown closes all sessions before stopping the
  exchange, so COD participants' orders are cancelled then too; a crash does not run it (Spec D72).
- The CANCEL_ALL is an ordinary journalled command; its `CANCELLED ... reason=CANCEL_ALL` replies are numbered and
  retained. Live on 2026-10-27, alice reconnected after a COD disconnect and `RESEND 1` returned
  `CANCELLED oseq=4 orderId=1 qty=6 reason=CANCEL_ALL resend=1` **[Verified — observed]**.

## ClientSession

### Reading, timeouts and the end of reading

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/ClientSession.java:152-182
private void readLoop() {
    // Not try-with-resources: closing a socket's input stream closes the whole socket, and the sender must
    // still flush what is queued (replies, BYE, a final ERROR). The sender closes the socket.
    boolean answerFirst = false;
    try {
        socket.setSoTimeout((int) settings.loginTimeout().toMillis());
        InputStream in = new BufferedInputStream(socket.getInputStream());
        String line;
        while ((line = readLine(in)) != null) {
            if (!handle(line)) {
                break;
            }
        }
        answerFirst = true;                     // QUIT, a failed login, or end of stream
    } catch (SocketTimeoutException e) {
        send(Protocol.error(participant == null ? "login timeout; disconnecting" : "idle timeout; disconnecting"));
        answerFirst = true;
    } catch (ProtocolException e) {
        send(Protocol.error(e.getMessage()));
        answerFirst = true;
    } catch (IOException e) {
        // connection gone: nobody left to answer
    } finally {
        if (answerFirst) {
            readerDone = true;
            finishIfIdle();
        } else {
            close();
        }
    }
}
```

- **Timeouts (D75, M1)** are socket read timeouts: `loginTimeout` until a successful LOGIN, then `idleTimeout`
  (set in `login`). Any line (PING included) resets the idle clock. Before, a silent connection held two threads
  and a session slot forever; 256 of them locked everyone out **[Verified]** (`TcpGatewayTest.Limits`,
  `TcpGatewayTest.java:404`, `:413`).
- `SocketTimeoutException` is caught before `IOException` (it is a subclass).
- **How reading ends decides how the session closes**: QUIT, end of stream, an overlong line, a timeout or a
  failed login -> answer first (`readerDone`, then `finishIfIdle`); a broken connection -> close at once.
- `readLine` is unchanged: bounded to 256 bytes of content (the `\r` of `\r\n` does not count) **[Verified]**
  (`ClientSession.java:184-204`).

### handle(): order of the checks

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/ClientSession.java:220-251 (trimmed)
if (message instanceof ClientMessage.Ping) {
    send("PONG");
    return true;
}
if (message instanceof ClientMessage.Quit) {
    farewell = "BYE";                       // sent after the replies still owed (D76)
    return false;
}
ExchangeService service = gateway.service();
if (service == null) {
    send(Protocol.error("exchange is starting; retry shortly"));        // L6
    return false;
}
if (message instanceof ClientMessage.Login login) {
    return login(service, login);
}
String p = participant;
if (p == null) {
    send(Protocol.error("LOGIN first"));
    return true;
}
if (message instanceof ClientMessage.Unsubscribe) {
    unsubscribe();
    return true;
}

String context = context(message);
String refusal = admit();
if (refusal != null) {
    send(Protocol.error(context + " " + refusal));
    return true;
}
```

PING and QUIT work before LOGIN; everything else needs a participant; NEW, CANCEL, RESEND and SUBSCRIBE also pass
`admit()` **[Verified]** (`ClientSession.java:211-266`).

### Login (D74, D73)

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/ClientSession.java:293-327 (trimmed)
private boolean login(ExchangeService service, ClientMessage.Login login) throws IOException {
    if (participant != null) {
        send(Protocol.error("already logged in as " + participant));
        return true;
    }
    if (loginAttempted) {
        send(Protocol.error("one login attempt per connection"));
        return false;
    }
    loginAttempted = true;
    String requested = login.participant();
    if (!Rules.PARTICIPANT.matcher(requested).matches() || !gateway.authenticate(requested, login.secret())) {
        send(Protocol.error("login failed"));       // the same answer whatever was wrong (D74)
        return false;
    }
    socket.setSoTimeout((int) settings.idleTimeout().toMillis());
    cancelOnDisconnect = login.cancelOnDisconnect();
    participant = requested;
    // On the writer thread, before any later output: register for routing, then report the latest oseq (D73).
    service.attach(requested, tracked((lastOseq, failure) -> {
        // ... on failure: ERROR LOGIN <reason>
        gateway.loggedIn(this);
        // ... undo if the session closed meanwhile (add-then-check)
        send(Protocol.welcome(requested, lastOseq));
    }));
    return true;
}
```

- **One attempt per connection; the same `ERROR login failed` whether the name is invalid, unknown or the secret is
  wrong**, and the connection closes (after flushing the error). This slows down guessing (a new TCP connection
  and a PBKDF2 computation per guess) and does not reveal which names exist. Live: `LOGIN alice guessed-secret` ->
  `ERROR login failed`, connection closed **[Verified — observed; TcpGatewayTest.Security, `:337`, `:345`]**.
- **The secret is checked on the reader thread** (a PBKDF2 hash takes tens to hundreds of ms at 210,000 iterations;
  only this session waits) **[Inferred: the cost]**.
- **Joining the stream without a gap**: `attach` is a read processed on the writer in queue order. Its callback
  registers the session for routing and sends `WELCOME participant=<p> oseq=<latest>`. Any command handed in after
  LOGIN is behind the attach in the queue, so its events find the session registered; any event before it is
  covered by `<latest>` and can be re-requested with RESEND **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Request.java:44-47`).
- `participant` and `cancelOnDisconnect` are `volatile`: written by the reader, read by the writer (routing) and by
  `removed()` on whatever thread closes the session.

### Limits: admit() (D75)

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/ClientSession.java:278-291
// D75: null if the request may go to the exchange, else why not.
private String admit() {
    if (inFlight.get() >= settings.maxInFlight()) {
        return "SESSION_BUSY";
    }
    long now = System.nanoTime();
    tokens = Math.min(settings.burst(), tokens + (now - lastRefillNanos) * settings.ratePerSecond() / 1e9);
    lastRefillNanos = now;
    if (tokens < 1) {
        return "RATE_LIMITED";
    }
    tokens -= 1;
    return null;
}
```

- **In-flight cap**: one session cannot fill the global queue so that everyone gets BUSY (M2).
- **Token bucket**: `tokens` refills at `ratePerSecond` up to `burst`; each request costs one. A flood is refused at
  the gateway, so it never reaches the journal or the client-order-id map (H2) **[Verified]**
  (`TcpGatewayTest.Limits.requestsBeyondTheRateAreRefusedWithoutReachingTheJournal`, `TcpGatewayTest.java:422`).
- Both refusals are stateless `ERROR` lines (`ERROR clOrdId=c3 RATE_LIMITED`): never queued, never journalled, so
  they cannot affect replay. `System.nanoTime` is read here, outside the engine, so determinism is untouched.
- The token fields are only touched by the reader thread, so they need no synchronisation.

### Close after the replies (D76)

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/ClientSession.java:420-442
// D76: counts the request in flight until its callback has run (after the request's own lines went out).
private <T> BiConsumer<T, Throwable> tracked(BiConsumer<T, Throwable> callback) {
    inFlight.incrementAndGet();
    return (value, failure) -> {
        try {
            callback.accept(value, failure);
        } finally {
            inFlight.decrementAndGet();
            finishIfIdle();
        }
    };
}

// Closes after the reader stopped and nothing is in flight; whichever of the two happens last does it.
private void finishIfIdle() {
    if (readerDone && inFlight.get() == 0 && finished.compareAndSet(false, true)) {
        String last = farewell;
        if (last != null) {
            send(last);
        }
        close();
    }
}
```

- Every request the session hands in (attach, NEW, CANCEL, RESEND, SUBSCRIBE) is wrapped by `tracked`. The counter
  goes up before the hand-in and down after the callback ran. Since D76 the callback runs *after* the exchange's
  listener sent the request's lines, so "count is zero" means "every line this session is owed has been offered".
- Two threads can reach `finishIfIdle` (the reader when it stops; the writer when the last callback finishes). Both
  conditions are re-read and `finished.compareAndSet` makes it happen exactly once. Because the reader sets the
  `volatile readerDone` *before* checking the count, and the callback decrements *before* checking `readerDone`,
  at least one of them sees both conditions true **[Inferred — the argument; code at the lines above]**.
- `farewell` is `BYE` only for QUIT; it is written before `readerDone` (volatile), so the finishing thread sees it.

**Why the D63 barrier was replaced.** D63 closed a session after a depth-1 snapshot read queued behind its
requests. Once the exchange is STOPPING, `enqueue` refuses every new request, including that read, and its
callback ran at once, so the session closed immediately. Replies for requests accepted *before* the stop then
found a closed session and were dropped. The in-flight count needs no new request: those accepted requests still
complete (processed before the stop marker), and the last completion closes the session **[Verified]**
(`TcpGatewayTest.Session.quitDuringShutdownStillGetsTheRepliesOfAcceptedRequests`, `TcpGatewayTest.java:214`;
Spec D76).

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant C as client
    participant R as reader
    participant W as exchange-writer
    participant S as sender
    C->>R: NEW a1 ... then QUIT
    R->>W: submit(a1), inFlight = 1
    R->>R: farewell = BYE, readerDone = true, inFlight != 0: wait
    W->>S: listener offers ORDER oseq=... a1
    W->>R: callback (tracked): inFlight = 0, finishIfIdle
    W->>S: BYE, then CLOSE
    S->>C: ORDER, BYE, socket closed
```

### RESEND

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/ClientSession.java:342-361 (trimmed)
private void resend(ExchangeService service, String p, long from) {
    if (from <= 0) {
        send(Protocol.error("oseq must be positive"));
        return;
    }
    // On the writer thread: the events go out before any later live event.
    service.resend(p, from, tracked((ResendResult result, Throwable failure) -> {
        // ... failure -> ERROR RESEND <reason>
        if (from < result.firstAvailable() && from <= result.lastOseq()) {
            send(Protocol.error("RESEND_GAP first=" + result.firstAvailable()));
        }
        for (PrivateEvent e : result.events()) {
            sendAll(Protocol.lines(e, true));
        }
        send("RESENT last=" + result.lastOseq());
    }));
}
```

- The read runs on the writer at its queue position, so resent events and live events cannot interleave out of
  order; a live event may arrive both live and in the resend (the client deduplicates by oseq).
- `RESEND_GAP` when the request reaches further back than the exchange retains (`--resend-window`, or before the
  last compaction) **[Verified]** (`TcpGatewayTest.Resend.eventsTooOldToRetainAreReportedAsAGap`, `:582`).

### Subscriptions and sending

Unchanged in substance: `subscriptionLock` plus numbered subscriptions keep the last SUBSCRIBED/UNSUBSCRIBED word
consistent; the snapshot read and the subscription happen in one writer step (no gap) **[Verified]**
(`ClientSession.java:363-411`). The sender flushes once per burst and closes the socket when it meets the `CLOSE`
sentinel (`:446-466`). A full outbound queue disconnects the session (`:115-124`).

## Protocol and ClientMessage

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/Protocol.java:66-81 (trimmed)
case "LOGIN" -> {
    if (t.length == 4 && t[3].equals(CANCEL_ON_DISCONNECT)) {
        yield new ClientMessage.Login(t[1], t[2], true);
    }
    arity(t, 3);
    yield new ClientMessage.Login(t[1], t[2], false);
}
// ...
case "RESEND" -> {
    arity(t, 2);
    yield new ClientMessage.Resend(number(t[1], "oseq"));
}
```

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/Protocol.java:140-166 (trimmed)
static String welcome(String participant, long lastOseq) {
    return "WELCOME participant=" + participant + " oseq=" + lastOseq;
}

// The lines of one private event; resent ones are marked.
static List<String> lines(PrivateEvent event, boolean resent) {
    List<String> lines = new ArrayList<>(2);
    String o = "oseq=" + event.oseq() + " ";
    if (event instanceof PrivateEvent.OrderReply r) {
        Response response = r.response();
        long leaves = r.quantity();
        for (Trade t : response.trades()) {
            leaves -= t.quantity();
            lines.add(fill(o, response.orderId(), response.clientOrderId(), r.side(), t.price(), t.quantity(), leaves, "TAKER"));
        }
        lines.add(order(o, response));
    }
    // ... CancelReply -> CANCELLED / CANCEL_REJECT, MakerFill -> FILL ... liquidity=MAKER
    if (resent) {
        lines.replaceAll(line -> line + " resend=1");
    }
    return lines;
}
```

- `oseq=` comes right after the message type; a multi-line event (taker FILLs + ORDER) shares one oseq.
- `CANCELLED ... reason=CANCEL_ALL` marks a cancel made by cancel-on-disconnect (`Protocol.java:180-184`).
- `ORDER ... reason=SELF_TRADE_PREVENTED` appears with `status=CANCELLED` (D67; seen live) **[Verified — observed]**.
- `ClientMessage.Login.toString()` leaves the secret out, so a parsed message in a log never shows it
  **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/ClientMessage.java:11-17`).

## Credentials (D74)

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/Credentials.java:82-87
public boolean verify(String participant, String secret) {
    Entry entry = participant == null ? null : entries.get(participant);
    Entry against = entry == null ? dummy : entry;      // same work either way
    byte[] candidate = pbkdf2(secret == null ? "" : secret, against.salt(), against.iterations());
    return MessageDigest.isEqual(candidate, against.hash()) && entry != null;
}
```

- **File format**: one line per participant, `<participant>:pbkdf2-sha256:<iterations>:<salt base64>:<hash base64>`;
  `#` comments and blank lines allowed; a bad line is reported with its number **[Verified]**
  (`Credentials.java:23-25`, `:93-118`). Live: `alice:pbkdf2-sha256:210000:gJDX...` **[Verified — observed]**.
- **PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256**, random 16-byte salt per entry, `DEFAULT_ITERATIONS` 210,000, 256-bit hash: a stolen file
  is expensive to brute-force, and the same secret hashes differently for two users (`CredentialsTest.java:33`).
- **Constant time**: `MessageDigest.isEqual` compares without early exit; an unknown participant is checked
  against a dummy entry so it costs the same as a known one (no user enumeration by timing) **[Verified — code;
  Inferred: the timing argument]**.
- **Secrets**: 8 to 64 printable ASCII characters without spaces (the protocol separates fields by spaces)
  (`Credentials.java:37-38`).
- `addUser` rewrites the whole file atomically through `Durable.writeAtomically` (`:71-80`). Tests build
  `Credentials.of(map, 1_000)` with few iterations because they log in hundreds of times (`TcpGatewayTest.java:52-60`).

## Tls

`Tls.serverContext(keystore, password)` loads a PKCS#12 keystore into a `KeyManagerFactory`;
`clientContext(truststore, password)` trusts that store or, with `null`, the JDK's default certificates. Passwords
come from environment variables, never the command line, where other users could read them in the process list
**[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/Tls.java:14-58`). `TcpGatewayTest.Security.tlsRoundTrip`
(`:369`) creates a keystore with the JDK's `keytool`, logs in over TLS, and checks that a plain-text client does not
get `PONG`.

## How it is tested

`TcpGatewayTest` runs real sockets, a real writer thread and a real journal. A `Hold` listener wraps the gateway
and blocks the writer once after a chosen command's output, so requests stay in flight deterministically
(`TcpGatewayTest.java:69`).

| Group | Covers |
|---|---|
| `Session` (`:145`) | LOGIN first; WELCOME with oseq; QUIT/EOF answer every earlier request first (D63/D76); replies still delivered when QUIT comes during shutdown (D76); malformed and overlong lines; registry cleanup; CRLF accepted; "starting" before attach (L6) |
| `Security` (`:329`) | loopback default; wrong secret and unknown name get the same answer and a close; credentials file; TLS round trip |
| `Limits` (`:401`) | login timeout; idle timeout; RATE_LIMITED without reaching the journal; SESSION_BUSY with a held writer |
| `Trading` (`:454`) | oseq on FILL/ORDER; taker FILLs before ORDER; every session sees the stream; cancel ownership; duplicates; self-trade prevention on the wire; stateless rejects never journalled; refusals after stop |
| `Resend` (`:558`) | a reconnecting client recovers a missed maker fill; RESEND_GAP; the stream survives a restart |
| `CancelOnDisconnect` (`:613`) | the last COD session closing cancels the participant's orders (and the cancels are resendable); without COD orders stay |
| `MarketData` (`:650`) | snapshot then updates, subscribe races, bounded snapshot, non-subscribers get nothing |

Also `ProtocolTest` (parsing and formatting), `ClientSessionTest` (`readLine` bounds) and `CredentialsTest`
(`src/test/java/exchangeserver/gateway/CredentialsTest.java:17`). The gateway is not mutated by PIT (Spec §7). The
whole suite passed on Windows (453 tests) and on Linux (Docker, 446 tests at the time) **[Verified — observed]**.

## Key takeaways

- Loopback bind, secret-checked LOGIN (one attempt, one answer for every failure), optional TLS (D74).
- Per-session limits are refused at the gateway, stateless and unjournalled: timeouts, in-flight cap, token bucket (D75).
- Private events are numbered per participant and go to every session of it; RESEND replays the retained ones (D73).
- A COD participant's orders are cancelled by a journalled CANCEL_ALL when its last session closes (D72).
- A session that stops reading closes when its in-flight count reaches zero, which works during shutdown (D76).
- The writer thread only offers to bounded queues; slow clients are disconnected (D54).

## Common confusions

- **"WELCOME's oseq is 0 for a new session."** It is the participant's latest number; a returning participant sees
  how far its stream already got, and can RESEND from its last seen number + 1.
- **"Rate-limited orders are rejected by the engine."** They never reach the engine or the journal; the reply is an
  ERROR line, not an ORDER line.
- **"COD cancels when any session closes."** Only when the participant's last session closes, and only if that
  session asked for COD.
- **"The idle timeout needs a heartbeat from the server."** It is a read timeout on the client's lines; the client
  keeps the session alive by sending (e.g. PING).
- **"Unknown participants fail faster."** They are checked against a dummy hash on purpose.

## Questions to test yourself

1. A client sends `LOGIN alice wrong-secret`, then `LOGIN alice right-secret` on the same connection. What happens?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>`ERROR login failed`, and the reader stops after the first LOGIN (one attempt per connection), so the second line is never read; the session closes after flushing the error. The client must reconnect.</details>

2. With `maxInFlight` 2 and the writer held after c1, a client sends NEW c1, c2, c3 quickly. What does it get?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>c1 and c2 are handed in (in flight 2); c3 gets `ERROR clOrdId=c3 SESSION_BUSY` and is never journalled. After the writer resumes, ORDER lines for c1 and c2 arrive.</details>

3. Why does `login` register the session in the attach callback, not right after the secret check?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>The callback runs on the writer at the attach read's queue position: after every earlier output and before every later one. So the WELCOME's oseq and the first live event the session receives meet exactly (no gap, no duplicate). Registering from the reader thread could race with an output being routed at that moment.</details>

4. During shutdown a client's NEW was accepted before the stop marker, then the client sends QUIT. What does it receive, and why did D63 fail here?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>Its ORDER line (when the writer processes the NEW), then BYE. D63 queued a snapshot read as a barrier, which a STOPPING exchange refuses, so the session closed at once and the ORDER was dropped. D76 counts in-flight requests instead; the accepted NEW still completes and its callback closes the session.</details>

5. Why are the rate-limit tokens not synchronised?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>Only the session's reader thread calls `admit()`, so `tokens` and `lastRefillNanos` are confined to one thread.</details>


---

<a id="guide-07-modules-client-and-main"></a>

<!-- source: docs/understanding/07-modules/client-and-main.md -->

# 07 — Module: client and Main (ExchangeClient, ClientMain, Main)

> **Project:** ExchangeServer2 | Single-instrument limit order book: price-time matching engine, write-ahead journal with replay, single writer thread, TCP/TLS gateway with authenticated participants | Stack: Java 17, Maven wrapper, JUnit 5 (+ JMH, PIT)
> **This guide:** the entry points: `Main` (server start-up, every flag, the `--add-user`, `--quarantine` and `--compact` tools, recovery summary, failure advice, shutdown and exit codes) and the client side: `ExchangeClient` (the line client used by tests and `ClientMain`) and `ClientMain` (interactive console, TLS optional) | **Prerequisites:** 01, 04, 07-modules/gateway | **Related:** 05b, 07-modules/exchange-and-replay, 07-modules/exchange-service, 10, 14
> **Repo layout summary:** `pom.xml` + Maven wrapper (`mvnw`, `mvnw.cmd`) at the root; production code in `src/main/java/exchangeserver/` (packages `domain`, `book`, `engine`, `command`, `journal`, `core`, `gateway`, `client`, plus `Main`); tests mirror it in `src/test/java/exchangeserver/` (plus `bench/` JMH benchmarks and `testing/MutableClock`); design docs in `docs/` (`ExchangeServer2_Vision.md` = purpose/roadmap, `ExchangeServer2_Spec.md` = decisions D1–D77); these guides in `docs/understanding/`.

## Overview

`Main` is the jar's entry point (`java -jar target/exchange-server2-2.0.0-SNAPSHOT.jar`). It either runs one
operator tool and exits, or starts the server: load credentials, bind the gateway, start accepting, replay the
journal, attach the exchange, print what recovery did, and wait. `ExchangeClient` is a small line client in
`src/main` because both `ClientMain` and the gateway tests use it **[Verified]**
(`src/main/java/exchangeserver/Main.java:23-42`).

What changed on 2026-10-27 (Spec D71–D77):
- new flags: `--bind`, `--users`, `--tls-keystore`, timeouts, flow control, `--check-interval`, `--resend-window`;
- new tools: `--add-user` and `--compact`;
- a recovery summary line (L1) and advice that fits the failure (L2);
- a shutdown hook that ends the process with the documented exit code;
- TLS in the client;
- an `ExchangeClient.linesUntil` fix (L4).

## Dependencies

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    Main --> Credentials["gateway.Credentials"]
    Main --> Tls["gateway.Tls"]
    Main --> TcpGateway["gateway.TcpGateway"]
    Main --> ExchangeService["core.ExchangeService"]
    Main --> Exchange["core.Exchange (quarantine, compact)"]
    Main --> ReplayException["core.ReplayException"]
    ClientMain --> ExchangeClient
    ClientMain --> Tls
    TcpGatewayTest["gateway tests"] --> ExchangeClient
```

## Main

### Options

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/Main.java:48-64
private static final class Options {
    int port = 9000;
    InetAddress bind = InetAddress.getLoopbackAddress();
    Path journal = Path.of("exchange.journal");
    Path users = Path.of("exchange.users");
    Path tlsKeystore;
    String addUser;
    Long quarantine;
    boolean compact;
    int loginTimeout = 10;
    int idleTimeout = 300;
    int maxInFlight = 1_000;
    int rate = 1_000;
    int burst = 2_000;
    int checkInterval = 10_000;
    int resendWindow = 1_000;
}
```

| Flag | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `--port N` | 9000 | gateway port |
| `--bind ADDR` | 127.0.0.1 | listen address; anything non-loopback without TLS prints a warning (D74) |
| `--journal FILE` | `exchange.journal` | the journal; also `FILE.lock`, `FILE.snapshot-N`, `FILE.archive-A-B` next to it |
| `--users FILE` | `exchange.users` | credentials file; required to serve |
| `--tls-keystore FILE` | none | PKCS#12 keystore; password from `ES2_TLS_PASSWORD` |
| `--login-timeout S`, `--idle-timeout S` | 10, 300 | session timeouts (D75) |
| `--max-in-flight N`, `--rate N`, `--burst N` | 1,000, 1,000, 2,000 | session flow control (D75) |
| `--check-interval N` | 10,000 | commands between CHECK records (D70) |
| `--resend-window N` | 1,000 | private events kept per participant for RESEND (D73) |
| `--add-user NAME` | tool | add or replace a participant in `--users` |
| `--quarantine SEQ` | tool | append a SKIP for a poison pill (D51) |
| `--compact` | tool | snapshot + empty journal (D71) |

A bad or unknown option prints `Usage: see the comment at the top of Main.java (...)` and exits 1 **[Verified]**
(`Main.java:66-92`).

### The tools

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/Main.java:94-117 (trimmed)
if (o.addUser != null) {
    tool(() -> {
        Credentials.addUser(o.users, o.addUser, readSecret(o.addUser), Credentials.DEFAULT_ITERATIONS);
        System.out.println("Saved " + o.addUser + " in " + o.users.toAbsolutePath());
    }, "Adding the user");
    return;
}
if (o.quarantine != null) {
    // ... Exchange.quarantine(o.journal, o.quarantine, Clock.systemUTC())
}
if (o.compact) {
    tool(() -> {
        Exchange.Compaction c = Exchange.compact(o.journal);
        System.out.println(c == null ? "Nothing to compact: the journal has no records since its snapshot"
                : "Compacted through sequence " + c.lastSequence() + ": " + c.restingOrders()
                + " resting orders in " + c.snapshot() + "; old journal archived as " + c.archive());
    }, "Compaction");
    return;
}
serve(o);
```

`tool(...)` turns any exception into one line `<what> failed: <message>` and exit 1 (`Main.java:204-210`). Seen live:
`Compaction failed: Journal ... is already open (a running exchange, or a --quarantine or --compact run)` while the
server ran, and `Compacted through sequence 4: 0 resting orders in ...snapshot-4; old journal archived as
...archive-1-4` after it stopped **[Verified — observed]**.

#### --add-user and the PowerShell byte-order mark

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/Main.java:212-232
private static String readSecret(String participant) throws IOException {
    Console console = System.console();
    if (console != null) {
        char[] first = console.readPassword("Secret for %s: ", participant);
        char[] again = console.readPassword("Again: ");
        if (first == null || !Arrays.equals(first, again)) {
            throw new IllegalArgumentException("the secrets differ");
        }
        return new String(first);
    }
    // No terminal (a script): one line from standard input. Windows PowerShell pipes text with a UTF-8
    // byte-order mark in front; it is not part of the secret.
```
