<!-- source: docs/understanding/12-change-recipes.md -->

# 12 — Change Recipes

> **Project:** ExchangeServer2 | Single-instrument limit order book: price-time matching engine, write-ahead journal with replay, single writer thread, TCP/TLS gateway with authenticated participants | Stack: Java 17, Maven wrapper, JUnit 5 (+ JMH, PIT)
> **This guide:** step-by-step recipes for the most likely changes: which files to touch, in which order, which tests to add, and which version numbers, digests and documents must move with them | **Prerequisites:** 01, 03, 05, 06 | **Related:** 07 (all modules), 09, 10, 11
> **Repo layout summary:** `pom.xml` + Maven wrapper (`mvnw`, `mvnw.cmd`) at the root; production code in `src/main/java/exchangeserver/` (packages `domain`, `book`, `engine`, `command`, `journal`, `core`, `gateway`, `client`, plus `Main`); tests mirror it in `src/test/java/exchangeserver/` (plus `bench/` JMH benchmarks and `testing/MutableClock`); design docs in `docs/` (`ExchangeServer2_Vision.md` = purpose/roadmap, `ExchangeServer2_Spec.md` = decisions D1–D77); these guides in `docs/understanding/`.

## Overview

Most changes touch more files than you expect, because one request crosses six layers: the wire protocol and session limits (`gateway`), stateless validation (`domain.OrderRequest`), the stamped and journalled command (`command`, `journal`), the deterministic engine (`engine`, `book`), the ledger that numbers private events and digests outputs (`core.Ledger`), and the writer thread (`core.Exchange`, `core.ExchangeService`).

Three things guard replay. Know which one your change moves:

| Guard | Where | Moves when | If you forget |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Rules.VERSION` (now 2) | `src/main/java/exchangeserver/domain/Rules.java:21` | a rule the engine or `OrderRequest` applies changes meaning (limits, band, caps, patterns, a new reject) | an old journal replays under new rules **[Verified]** (comment `Rules.java:5-16`, Spec D20, D46) |
| `JournalCodec.FORMAT_VERSION` (now 3) | `src/main/java/exchangeserver/journal/JournalCodec.java:41` | the text layout of a record changes | old journals fail to decode or decode into different values **[Inferred]** |
| The output digest in `CHECK` records | `src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/OutputDigest.java:48`, written by `Exchange.process` at `src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Exchange.java:200-203` | *anything* that changes what the engine outputs for the same commands | nothing to forget: replay of an old journal stops with `ReplayException(DIVERGED)` at the next CHECK **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Replay.java:80-88`, Spec D70) |

The header records format, rules version and the base sequence: `HEADER,3,<rules>,<base>,<crc>` **[Verified]** (`JournalCodec.java:58-60`). `CommandJournal.open` refuses a journal with records written under other rules, but lets a journal **with no records** adopt the new version **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/journal/CommandJournal.java:241-261`, Spec D71). That gives the one supported way across a behaviour or rules change:

1. Stop the exchange (old build).
2. `java -jar old.jar --journal exchange.journal --compact` — writes `exchange.journal.snapshot-<N>`, archives the journal, leaves an empty journal with base N.
3. Start the new build on the same journal: it adopts the new rules version and loads the snapshot (a snapshot is state, not commands, so it does not depend on rules).

During development you can also just delete your local `*.journal*` files (they are git-ignored).

**Before you start any change:** ask which collaboration mode applies — the user writes and the LLM reviews, or the LLM writes **[Verified]** (`CLAUDE.md` "Working style"; Vision §3).

**After any behavioural change** (every recipe ends with this):
1. `mvnw.cmd test` — all green, warning-free under `-Xlint:all` **[Verified]** (`pom.xml:62-65`).
2. `mvnw.cmd -Pmutation test` — explain survivors in Spec §7.
3. Next free decision number in Spec §3: **D78** **[Verified]** (Spec §3 ends at D77).
4. Spec §2 class table, invariants, §4 journal format or §6 gateway as relevant.
5. These guides, then `java docs/understanding/build-full-context.java`.
6. Optionally the Linux run (Recipe 13).
7. One Maven build at a time; after restoring files from a backup use `mvnw.cmd clean …` (timestamps) **[Verified]** (`CLAUDE.md` Environment).

---

## Recipe 1 — Add an order type (e.g. STOP, or a good-till-time LIMIT)

`OrderType` is an enum used in an exhaustive `switch`, so the compiler lists most places for you **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/engine/MatchingEngine.java:242-259`).

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/domain/OrderType.java:12-23 (trimmed)
public enum OrderType {
    LIMIT,
    IOC,
    FOK,
    MARKET,
    POST_ONLY;

    public boolean canRest() {
        return this == LIMIT || this == POST_ONLY;
    }
}
```

1. **Decide the semantics with a numeric scenario first** (Vision §3): price or not, rests or not, outcome for full/partial/no fill, which checks apply (band, caps, duplicate id) and how **self-trade prevention** applies — the match loop stops at the participant's own resting order for every type (`MatchingEngine.java:224-227`, D67).
   - **STOP** waits for a trigger and fires inside another command's processing: a new deterministic structure and a new D-number **[Inferred]**.
   - **GTT** needs expiry as a *command* stamped by the `Sequencer` (like `CANCEL_ALL`), never a timer in the engine (determinism) **[Inferred]**.
2. `domain/OrderType.java` — constant and `canRest()`.
3. `domain/OrderRequest.java` — factory and `of(...)` price handling (only MARKET has no price today).
4. `engine/MatchingEngine.java` — the `switch` at `:242-259` won't compile until you add a case. Review the check block `:182-213` (band for priced orders; caps only if `canRest()`; FOK-style pre-checks via `fillability`, `:299-315`, which stops at the own order), `matchLimit` (`:274-291`) and the STP stop (`:224-227`).
5. `engine/Response.java` — if the type can end in a new status/reason combination, extend the invariants (`Response.java:18-56`).
6. `core/OutputDigest.java` — the digest encodes the response field by field (`:48-84`); a new *field* in `Response` must be added there, or two different outputs would digest alike.
7. `gateway/Protocol.java` — `parseNew` (`:102-114`) treats MARKET as "no price"; update the comment block (`:16-45`).
8. `domain/Rules.java` — bump `VERSION` (`:21`) and add the reason to the comment above it.
9. Tests: a `@Nested` group in `MatchingEngineTest` (three outcomes, band/cap/STP interactions), `OrderRequestTest`, `JournalCodecTest.RoundTrip`, `ProtocolTest.Parse`, a `TcpGatewayTest.Trading` case. `OrderBookTest.InvariantsUnderRandomTraffic` draws from `OrderType.values()`, so the type enters the random invariant check automatically **[Verified]** (`src/test/java/exchangeserver/book/OrderBookTest.java`).
10. Docs: Spec §2 order-type table and a D-number.

---

## Recipe 2 — Add a reason or a risk check

Reasons are one enum since D67:

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/engine/Reason.java:12-28 (trimmed)
public enum Reason {
    DUPLICATE_CLIENT_ORDER_ID, PRICE_OUTSIDE_BAND, MAX_OPEN_ORDERS, BOOK_FULL, POST_ONLY_WOULD_CROSS,
    SELF_TRADE_PREVENTED;
    public boolean isReject() {
        return this != SELF_TRADE_PREVENTED;
    }
}
```

`Response` enforces the pairing: a reject reason only with REJECTED, `SELF_TRADE_PREVENTED` only with CANCELLED **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/engine/Response.java:33-38`).

1. **Stateless or stateful?** Request-only checks belong in `OrderRequest` and are answered `ERROR` at the gateway, never journalled (D47). Book- or history-dependent checks run in the engine after journalling and are replayed (D13).
2. `engine/Reason.java` — add the constant. If it is **not** a reject (it can follow trades, like STP), make `isReject()` return false for it and decide which status it goes with in `Response`.
3. `engine/MatchingEngine.java` — the check order in `process` is **[Verified]** (`:182-213`):
   1. duplicate client order id (consumes the id even if a later check rejects, D49);
   2. price band (priced orders, after the first trade);
   3. caps — only for orders that would rest: `MAX_OPEN_ORDERS` or `BOOK_FULL` unless the order fully trades now (D68);
   4. FOK fillability (stops at the own order: `SELF_TRADE_PREVENTED` if that is what blocked it);
   5. POST_ONLY cross.
   Put your check where its meaning fits; it must not mutate anything before rejecting (only the id map). Use `rejected(...)` (`:265-268`).
4. `domain/Rules.java` — constant here, **bump `VERSION`**.
5. Gateway: nothing — `Protocol` prints `reason=<name>` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/Protocol.java:174-178`).
6. The digest already includes `r.reason()` (`OutputDigest.java:53-55`) **[Verified]**.
7. Tests: boundaries in `MatchingEngineTest` (the `Caps` group shows the package-private `Limits` seam for caps too large to fill in a test, `MatchingEngine.java:50-54`); `ResponseTest` for any new pairing; an `ExchangeTest` case proving it is journalled and replays.

---

## Recipe 3 — Change a business-rule constant

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/domain/Rules.java:21-37 (trimmed)
public static final int VERSION = 2;
public static final long MAX_ORDER_QUANTITY = 1_000_000_000L;
public static final long MAX_PRICE = Long.MAX_VALUE / MAX_ORDER_QUANTITY;
public static final int PRICE_BAND_PERCENT = 10;
public static final int MAX_OPEN_ORDERS_PER_PARTICIPANT = 10_000;
public static final int MAX_RESTING_ORDERS = 1_000_000;
```

1. Change the constant and bump `VERSION` in the same edit.
2. Check derived arithmetic: `MAX_PRICE` keeps `price × quantity` inside a `long`; `withinBand` (`Rules.java:44-47`) and `matchLimit` (`MatchingEngine.java:274-291`) must agree exactly (floor for buys, ceil for sells) — `RulesTest` and the `MatchingEngineTest.Market` collar tests guard it. Since D66 the collar's reference before the first trade is the opposite touch; a band change affects that too.
3. Existing journals: compact with the old build, start the new one (Overview).
4. Tests and docs: `RulesTest`, `MatchingEngineTest.Risk`/`Caps`, Spec §2 `Rules` row, guide 13.

---

## Recipe 4 — Add a journal record type or a field

Format v3 seals every record with a CRC-32C **[Verified]**:

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/journal/JournalCodec.java:166-168
static String seal(String text) {
    return text + SEPARATOR + Checksums.crcHex(text) + END_OF_RECORD;
}
```

**A new record type** (e.g. `EXPIRE`):
1. `command/` — a record implementing `Command` with validation in its compact constructor, and add it to `permits` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/command/Command.java`); a `stampExpire` in `Sequencer` (pattern: `stampCancelAll`, `src/main/java/exchangeserver/command/Sequencer.java:60-64`).
2. `journal/JournalCodec.java` — `encode` (`:87-128`) and `decode` (`:130-164`) branches with `requireFieldCount`; update the format comment. A new record word that old builds cannot read is a format change: bump `FORMAT_VERSION` (`:41`) **[Inferred]**.
3. `core/Replay.java` — the verifier counts every record's sequence (`:114-129`); `feed` (`:72-99`) skips SKIP and compares CHECK; an engine command goes to `ledger.apply`.
4. `core/Ledger.java` — a branch in `apply` (`:42-55`) calling the engine; the digest update and `log.record` follow automatically.
5. `core/OutputLog.java` — `record` (`:58-76`) throws on an output it does not recognise: add a branch that numbers the private events your command produces.
6. `core/OutputDigest.java` — encode the new output kind (`:48-84`).
7. `core/Exchange.java` — stamp it in `process` (`:205-213`); add a `Request` type (Recipe 6) if callers hand it in.
8. `engine/` — `onExpire` returning a new `EngineOutput` (add it to `permits`).
9. `Rules.VERSION` bump (engine behaviour).
10. Tests: `JournalCodecTest` (round trip + corrupt cases, extra fields), `SequencerTest`, `ReplayTest`, `OutputLogTest`, `OutputDigestTest`, `ExchangeTest`.

These are `instanceof` chains, not exhaustive switches (Java 17), so the compiler will **not** list them for you: grep for `instanceof CancelAllCommand` to find every place the last record type was handled **[Verified]** (`Ledger.java:44-51`, `OutputLog.java:60-72`, `Exchange.java:205-213`).

**A new field on orders:** validate in `OrderRequest` (the only field-validation layer, D47), copy into `Order`, add to `encode`/`decode` (bump `FORMAT_VERSION`), to `RestingOrderView` and the snapshot format if it must survive compaction (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Snapshot.java:64-116`, bump `Snapshot.FORMAT_VERSION`), to `OutputDigest` if outputs carry it, and to `Protocol` lines. Keep a NEW line ≤ 256 bytes (`Protocol.java:50`).

---

## Recipe 5 — Add a protocol message (e.g. `STATUS <orderId>`)

1. Session-local (like `PING`) or through the exchange? Anything reading state goes through the queue (D57; Recipe 6).
2. `gateway/ClientMessage.java` — a record; if it has a secret, override `toString` like `Login` does.
3. `gateway/Protocol.java` — a `case` in `parse` (`:60-100`) using `arity`/`number`; a formatter; update the comment block (it *is* the protocol documentation).
4. `gateway/ClientSession.java` — handle it in `handle` (`:211-266`). After the `LOGIN first` guard every exchange request:
   - passes `admit()` — the in-flight cap and rate limit (`:279-291`, D75);
   - uses a **callback** variant of `ExchangeService` wrapped in `tracked(...)` (`:421-432`), which counts it in flight so a closing session waits for it (D76);
   - reports refusals with `replyFailure` (`:413-419`).
   Callbacks run on the writer thread **after** the listener (D76) and must not block.
5. Add a `context(...)` label (`:268-276`) so `ERROR` lines say which request was refused.
6. Tests: `ProtocolTest.Parse` (including the malformed list), `ProtocolTest.Format`, a `TcpGatewayTest` case (log in with `login("alice")`, which sends the test secret).

---

## Recipe 6 — Add a read-only query through the queue

Reads are `Request`s that are never stamped or journalled **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Request.java:12-20`; `Exchange.java:231-234`).

1. Book/engine: a read method returning copies (`OrderBook.walk`/`restingOrders` return `RestingOrderView`s, `src/main/java/exchangeserver/book/OrderBook.java:149-165`).
2. `core/Request.java` — a record; validate arguments in its compact constructor, on the caller's thread (like `Snapshot` and `Resend`, `Request.java:35-55`).
3. `core/Exchange.java` — a branch in `read` (`:255-266`).
4. `core/ExchangeService.java` — a future variant via `handOff` (`:170-180`, D61) and a callback variant (`:186-209`).
5. Gateway: Recipe 5. No `Rules.VERSION` bump: reads do not change replay **[Inferred]**.

---

## Recipe 7 — Add a private event type

Private events are numbered per participant (oseq) and are what `RESEND` repeats **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/PrivateEvent.java:12-32`, Spec D73).

1. `core/PrivateEvent.java` — a record in the sealed interface.
2. `core/OutputLog.java` — produce it in `record` (`:58-76`) with `add(new …(participant, next(participant), …))`; order inside one output matters: the requester's reply first, then others.
3. `gateway/Protocol.java` — format it in `lines` (`:145-166`) with the `oseq=` prefix and the `resend` marker.
4. No journal change: events are derived from commands, so replay rebuilds them **[Verified]** (`ReplayTest.Feed.feedsEveryCommandAndReportsTheCounters` checks a rebuilt oseq).
5. Tests: `OutputLogTest`, `ProtocolTest.Format`, a `TcpGatewayTest.Resend` case.

---

## Recipe 8 — Change settings, limits or CLI flags

Two settings records, both validated with `with…` copiers:
- `ExchangeService.Settings(queueCapacity, maxBatchSize, lingerNanos, clock, exchangeOptions)` with `Exchange.Options(checkInterval, outputRetention)` **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/ExchangeService.java:59-92`, `Exchange.java:64-75`).
- `TcpGateway.Settings(bindAddress, port, credentials, tls, maxSessions, outboundCapacity, loginTimeout, idleTimeout, maxInFlight, ratePerSecond, burst)` **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/TcpGateway.java:52-98`).

`Main` maps flags to both **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/Main.java:71-85`, `:130-142`):

```java
// src/main/java/exchangeserver/Main.java:130-142 (trimmed)
gateway = new TcpGateway(TcpGateway.Settings.defaults(o.port, credentials)
        .withBindAddress(o.bind)
        .withTls(tls)
        .withTimeouts(Duration.ofSeconds(o.loginTimeout), Duration.ofSeconds(o.idleTimeout))
        .withFlowControl(o.maxInFlight, o.rate, o.burst));
gateway.startAccepting();
service = ExchangeService.start(o.journal, gateway, ExchangeService.Settings.defaults()
        .withExchangeOptions(new Exchange.Options(o.checkInterval, o.resendWindow)));
```

To add a flag: a field in `Main.Options` (`:49-65`), a `case` in the argument loop, wire it, update the header comment of `Main.java`, README "Run" and `CLAUDE.md` Commands. Batching defaults were chosen by measurement: re-run `BatchingBenchmark` before changing them (Spec §5, D52). Writer stats are read only after `stop()` in tests (Spec §10 #1).

---

## Recipe 9 — Add or rotate a participant's secret

Secrets live as salted PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 hashes in the users file **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/Credentials.java:20-34`, D74).

1. `java -jar target/exchange-server2-2.0.0-SNAPSHOT.jar --users exchange.users --add-user alice` — asks twice on a terminal, or reads one line from stdin (a leading UTF-8 BOM from Windows PowerShell pipes is dropped, `Main.java:212-235`). Adding an existing name replaces its entry (rotation).
2. The running exchange reads the file at startup only: restart to apply **[Verified]** (`Main.java:124`, `Credentials.load`). Existing sessions stay logged in until they disconnect **[Inferred]**.
3. Rules for a secret: 8–64 printable ASCII characters, no spaces (`Credentials.SECRET`, `Credentials.java:38`).
4. In tests use `Credentials.of(map, 1_000)` — few iterations, because tests log in hundreds of times (`src/test/java/exchangeserver/gateway/TcpGatewayTest.java`).

---

## Recipe 10 — Enable TLS

1. Create a PKCS#12 keystore, e.g. `keytool -genkeypair -alias es2 -keyalg EC -groupname secp256r1 -dname CN=localhost -keystore server.p12 -storetype PKCS12` (the test `TcpGatewayTest.Security.tlsRoundTrip` does exactly this).
2. Start with `--tls-keystore server.p12` and the password in `ES2_TLS_PASSWORD` (never on the command line) **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/Tls.java:52-58`, `Main.java:125-126`). Protocols: TLS 1.3 and 1.2 (`Tls.java:22`).
3. Clients: `ClientMain localhost 9000 --tls --truststore server.p12` with `ES2_TRUSTSTORE_PASSWORD`, or the JDK's default trust store for a CA-signed certificate.
4. If you `--bind` a non-loopback address without TLS, `Main` warns that secrets cross the network in clear text **[Verified]** (`Main.java:127-129`).

---

## Recipe 11 — Add a dependency

The production jar has **zero** runtime dependencies (`pom.xml:33-52`) **[Verified]**; TLS and PBKDF2 come from the JDK. A production dependency needs shading or a classpath (no `Class-Path` in the manifest) **[Inferred]**. Keep `-Xlint:all` clean; anything used inside the engine must stay deterministic (no clocks, randomness or hash-order iteration).

---

## Recipe 12 — Add a test

Conventions **[Verified]** (`CLAUDE.md` Tests):
- one test class per production class, `@Nested` per behaviour; every wait has a timeout;
- read engine state only through a future; read writer stats only after `stop()`;
- seams (package-private): `core.CommandInterceptor` (poison pill or `Gate`), `OrderBook.checkInvariants()`/`ordersAt`, `MatchingEngine.book()` and the `Limits` constructor, `Exchange.engine()`/`digest()`, `TcpGateway.participantsRegistered()`/`subscriberCount()`/`drainingSessions()`, `testing.MutableClock`;
- in gateway tests, the `Hold` listener in `TcpGatewayTest` holds the writer once to keep requests in flight;
- journals in `@TempDir`; handle release via `CommandJournal.openJournals()`; write journals with LF (`Files.writeString(…, String.join("\n", …))`) — `Files.write(path, lines)` uses CRLF on Windows and the decoder refuses it (D69);
- cross-process behaviour: spawn a child JVM as `CommandJournalTest.Exclusive.anotherProcessIsRefusedWhileTheJournalIsOpen` does.

---

## Recipe 13 — Run the suite on Linux

Docker, from the repo root **[Verified]** (run during the session; Spec §11):

```
docker run --rm -v "<repo>:/src:ro" eclipse-temurin:17 bash -c \
  "mkdir /w && (cd /src && tar --exclude=./target --exclude=./.git -cf - .) | tar -xf - -C /w \
   && cd /w && sed -i 's/\r$//' mvnw && chmod +x mvnw && ./mvnw -B test"
```

`.gitattributes` keeps `mvnw` LF in fresh checkouts; the `sed` covers working copies made before it existed. To test a signal from a script, enable job control (`set -m`): a non-interactive shell starts background jobs with SIGINT ignored.

---

## Key takeaways

- Three replay guards: `Rules.VERSION` (meaning), `FORMAT_VERSION` (layout), and `CHECK` digests (any output change). Cross a change by compacting with the old build.
- Check order in the engine: duplicate id → band → caps (only if resting) → FOK fillability → post-only cross; STP stops the match loop at the own order.
- A new command or output means `instanceof` branches in `Ledger`, `OutputLog`, `OutputDigest`, `Replay` and `Exchange` that the compiler does not list.
- Gateway requests go through `admit()` and `tracked(...)`; callbacks run after the listener.
- Secrets: `--add-user`, restart to apply; TLS: keystore + environment password.
- Every change ends with tests, `-Pmutation`, a D-number (next D78), docs, and `FULL-CONTEXT.md`.

## Common confusions

- **"A new `Reason` is just an enum constant."** It changes outputs (and the digest) and is a rule change: bump `Rules.VERSION`, and decide its `isReject()`.
- **"My refactor changed no rule, so old journals still replay."** If any output differs, the next CHECK stops replay as DIVERGED. Compact with the old build first.
- **"Compaction works across rules versions by itself."** It must run with the build that *wrote* the journal; the new build then adopts the empty journal.
- **"A new private event needs a journal record."** No: events are derived from commands and rebuilt by replay.
- **"Adding a user takes effect immediately."** The users file is read at startup.

## Questions to test yourself

1. You change `PRICE_BAND_PERCENT` to 15. How do you keep yesterday's book?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>Stop the old build and run <code>--compact</code> with it (snapshot + empty journal), then bump <code>Rules.VERSION</code> in the new build and start it: the empty journal adopts the new version and the book comes from the snapshot (D71).</details>

2. Where does a new "max notional" check go in `MatchingEngine.process`, and what may it change before rejecting?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>In the checks block (<code>MatchingEngine.java:182-213</code>) after the duplicate-id check (which consumes the id) — where its meaning fits relative to band and caps. Nothing but the id map may change before it rejects.</details>

3. You add an `EXPIRE` command. Name four places the compiler will not flag.
<details><summary>Answer</summary><code>Ledger.apply</code>, <code>OutputLog.record</code>, <code>OutputDigest.update</code>, <code>Exchange.process</code>'s stamping, <code>Replay.feed</code>, <code>JournalCodec.encode/decode</code>: all <code>instanceof</code> chains or string switches.</details>

4. Why does a new protocol request use `tracked(...)`?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>It counts the request in flight until its callback ran (after its lines went out), which the session limit uses and which lets a closing session wait for the reply (D75, D76).</details>

5. A refactor of `fillBest` changes nothing visible but swaps the order of two level updates in one output. What happens on restart with an old journal?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>The digest covers level updates in order, so it differs; replay stops at the next CHECK with <code>ReplayException(DIVERGED)</code> and <code>Main</code> advises compacting with the old build.</details>


---

<a id="guide-13-glossary"></a>

<!-- source: docs/understanding/13-glossary.md -->

# 13 — Glossary

> **Project:** ExchangeServer2 | Single-instrument limit order book: price-time matching engine, write-ahead journal with replay, single writer thread, TCP/TLS gateway with authenticated participants | Stack: Java 17, Maven wrapper, JUnit 5 (+ JMH, PIT)
> **This guide:** every domain term, project-specific name and abbreviation, with a short definition and where it lives in the code | **Prerequisites:** none (use it alongside any guide) | **Related:** 01, 06, 07 (all modules), 09
> **Repo layout summary:** `pom.xml` + Maven wrapper (`mvnw`, `mvnw.cmd`) at the root; production code in `src/main/java/exchangeserver/` (packages `domain`, `book`, `engine`, `command`, `journal`, `core`, `gateway`, `client`, plus `Main`); tests mirror it in `src/test/java/exchangeserver/` (plus `bench/` JMH benchmarks and `testing/MutableClock`); design docs in `docs/` (`ExchangeServer2_Vision.md` = purpose/roadmap, `ExchangeServer2_Spec.md` = decisions D1–D77); these guides in `docs/understanding/`.

## Overview

Alphabetical, grouped by first letter. Paths are relative to `src/main/java/exchangeserver/` unless they start with `src/`, `docs/` or `pom.xml`. Every location was read in the current code **[Verified]** unless marked **[Inferred]**.

Abbreviations: **D-n** = design decision n in the Spec; **WAL** = write-ahead log; **L2** = level-2 market data; **IOC/FOK** = immediate-or-cancel / fill-or-kill; **clOrdId** = client order id; **seq** = command sequence number; **oseq** = output sequence number of a participant's private stream; **COD** = cancel-on-disconnect; **STP** = self-trade prevention; **CRC** = cyclic redundancy check; **PBKDF2** = password-based key derivation function 2; **TLS** = transport layer security; **JMH/PIT** = benchmark / mutation tools.

---

## A

- **Acceptor** — the daemon thread `gateway-acceptor` that accepts connections and creates a `ClientSession` each, refusing beyond 256 sessions. It starts *before* replay (sessions answer "starting" until the exchange is attached) and backs off exponentially on `accept()` failures, logging ERROR after 10 in a row (D77). `gateway/TcpGateway.java:164-205`.
- **Adoption (rules adoption)** — a journal with no records yet, written under another `Rules.VERSION`, takes this build's version (its header is rewritten). The way across a rules change, after compaction with the old build (D71). `journal/CommandJournal.java:241-261`, `CommandJournal.adoptedRulesFrom()` at `:216`.
- **Aggressor (taker, incoming order)** — the order being matched now; recorded on every trade as `aggressorSide`. `engine/Trade.java:15`.
- **Append / `appendAll`** — write a batch of sealed records with one write and one fsync; sets the fail-stop flag on error. `journal/CommandJournal.java:143-166`.
- **Archive** — the copy of a journal that compaction keeps, `<journal>.archive-<first>-<last>`; nothing reads it back. `core/Exchange.java:174-196`.
- **Ask** — a sell order resting on the book; the ask side is a `TreeMap` lowest price first. `book/OrderBook.java:42`.
- **Attach (read)** — a queued read that returns a participant's latest oseq; the gateway registers the session for routing in its callback on the writer thread, so `WELCOME … oseq=N` and the first event received leave no gap (D73). `core/Request.java:46-48`, `gateway/ClientSession.java:293-327`.

## B

- **Backpressure / BUSY** — refusing a request when the writer's queue holds `queueCapacity` (100,000) requests. Nothing is journalled. `core/ExchangeService.java:211-240`. Compare **SESSION_BUSY** (per session).
- **Band (price band)** — a priced order must be within ±10% of the last trade price, else REJECTED `PRICE_OUTSIDE_BAND`; no band for priced orders before the first trade. `domain/Rules.java:41-47`, checked at `engine/MatchingEngine.java:188-190`.
- **Base sequence** — the last sequence number of the snapshot a journal continues from, in its header; 0 = none. The first record is base + 1 (D71). `journal/JournalCodec.java:58-60`, `CommandJournal.baseSequence()` at `journal/CommandJournal.java:203`.
- **Batch** — requests the writer takes in one go (≤ `maxBatchSize`, 1,000), stamped, journalled with one fsync, then processed. `core/ExchangeService.java:286-324`, `core/Exchange.java:198-253`.
- **Bid** — a buy order resting on the book; reverse-ordered `TreeMap`. `book/OrderBook.java:41`.
- **BOOK line / `BookSnapshot`** — the snapshot a subscriber gets: `BOOK seq= last= bids= asks=`; `(sequenceNumber, lastTradePrice, bids, asks)`. `gateway/Protocol.java:186-203`, `engine/BookSnapshot.java:14-20`.
- **BOOK_FULL** — reject when the whole book holds `MAX_RESTING_ORDERS` (1,000,000) and the order would rest (D68). `engine/Reason.java:19-20`, `domain/Rules.java:37`.

## C

- **Callback variant** — `ExchangeService.submit/cancel/cancelAll/snapshot/attach/resend(…, BiConsumer)`: runs on the writer thread *after* the command's listener call (D76), or on the caller's thread if refused. A throwing callback is logged (D77). `core/ExchangeService.java:186-240`.
- **Cancel** — removing a resting order by its owner; someone else's order or a gone order yields `UNKNOWN_ORDER` (D48). `book/OrderBook.java:98-108`.
- **CANCEL_ALL / `CancelAllCommand`** — a journalled command that cancels every resting order of one participant, in book priority order; used for cancel-on-disconnect (D72). `command/CancelAllCommand.java:10`, `engine/MatchingEngine.java:136-155`, output `engine/CancelAllOutput.java:11`.
- **Cancel-on-disconnect (COD)** — `LOGIN <p> <secret> COD`; when that participant's last session closes, the gateway submits `CANCEL_ALL`. The cancels land in its private stream (`CANCELLED … reason=CANCEL_ALL`) (D72). `gateway/TcpGateway.java:239-263`.
- **`CancelResult` / `CancelStatus`** — `CANCELLED` (with quantity) or `UNKNOWN_ORDER`. `book/CancelResult.java:6-25`, `book/CancelStatus.java:9-12`.
- **`canRest()`** — true only for `LIMIT` and `POST_ONLY`. `domain/OrderType.java:20-22`.
- **CHECK / `CheckCommand`** — a journal record `CHECK,<seq>,<ts>,<digest>` asserting the output digest of every command before it; written every `checkInterval` (10,000) commands at the start of a batch; replay compares it (D70). `command/CheckCommand.java:16`, written at `core/Exchange.java:200-203`, compared at `core/Replay.java:80-88`.
- **`checkInvariants()`** — package-private test seam re-deriving every book invariant. `book/OrderBook.java:226-267`.
- **Checksum (CRC-32C)** — every journal and snapshot record ends with `,<8 hex digits>`, the CRC-32C of its text; a mismatch is a corrupt record (D69). `journal/Checksums.java:13-17`, `journal/JournalCodec.java:166-187`.
- **clOrdId (client order id)** — the client's id for a new order; unique per participant **within one journal** (after compaction only resting orders keep theirs, D71 revises D49); a repeat is REJECTED `DUPLICATE_CLIENT_ORDER_ID` with the original order id. `domain/Rules.java:29`, map at `engine/MatchingEngine.java:58`.
- **`ClientMessage`** — sealed parsed client lines (Login with secret and COD flag, NewOrder, Cancel, Resend, Subscribe, Unsubscribe, Ping, Quit); `Login.toString` hides the secret. `gateway/ClientMessage.java`.
- **`ClientSession`** — one connection: reader thread, sender thread, bounded outbound queue, login, limits, in-flight count. `gateway/ClientSession.java:53`.
- **Collar (market collar)** — the price range a MARKET order may trade in, fixed at arrival: ±10% around the last trade, or around the opposite **touch** before the first trade (D66); floor/ceil inward. `engine/MatchingEngine.java:274-291`.
- **`Command`** — a stamped, journalled input; sealed: `NewOrderCommand`, `CancelOrderCommand`, `CancelAllCommand`, `SkipCommand`, `CheckCommand` (D12, D31). `command/Command.java`.
- **`CommandInterceptor`** — package-private test seam run just before the engine, live and in replay (where it counts as engine work). `core/CommandInterceptor.java:11-17`.
- **`CommandJournal`** — the write-ahead log: lock, header check, rules adoption, torn-tail cut, sealed append + fsync, streaming read, fail-stop. `journal/CommandJournal.java:46`.
- **Compaction** — `--compact`, run with the exchange stopped: replay, write a snapshot, archive the journal, atomically replace it with an empty one whose base is the snapshot (D71). Bounds recovery; ends clOrdId scope; crosses rules changes. `core/Exchange.java:174-196`.
- **Credentials / users file** — `participant:pbkdf2-sha256:<iterations>:<salt>:<hash>` per line; verified in constant time with a dummy entry for unknown names (D74). `gateway/Credentials.java:34-150`.
- **Crosses** — a buy crosses an ask at or below its limit; a sell a bid at or above it. The single definition (D28): `engine/MatchingEngine.java:293-295`. A crossed book must never exist (`book/OrderBook.java:264`).

## D

- **D-number (D1–D77)** — a numbered design decision with its reason in Spec §3. Next free: D78.
- **Determinism** — engine output depends only on the ordered commands (no clock, counters, randomness, hash-order iteration). `engine/MatchingEngine.java:29-30`.
- **Digest (output digest)** — `OutputDigest`: FNV-1a 64 over a canonical text encoding of every engine output in order; checked by CHECK records (D70). Not cryptographic. `core/OutputDigest.java:25-88`.
- **DIVERGED** — `ReplayException` kind: a CHECK digest differs from what replay produced — this build behaves differently from the one that wrote the journal. `core/ReplayException.java:14-17`.
- **Draining session** — a session whose reader stopped and which waits for its in-flight requests before closing (D76). Test hook `TcpGateway.drainingSessions()`. `gateway/ClientSession.java:111-113`.
- **`drainLevelUpdates()`** — one `LevelUpdate` per level touched since the last call, first-touched order. `book/OrderBook.java:131-145`.
- **`Durable`** — crash-safe file helpers: `writeAtomically` (temp file, fsync, atomic rename, directory fsync), `copyDurably`, `fsyncDirectory` (ignored where a directory cannot be opened, i.e. Windows) (D69, D71). `journal/Durable.java:18-64`.
- **Durable ⇒ visible / visible ⇒ durable** — the second holds; the first does not (a crash after fsync and before the reply). Spec §4.

## E

- **ENGINE_FAILURE** — `ReplayException` kind: the engine threw on a journalled command during replay — a poison pill; `Main` advises `--quarantine <seq>`. `core/ReplayException.java:14-17`, `Main.java:187-198`.
- **`EngineOutput`** — sealed: `OrderOutput`, `CancelOutput`, `CancelAllOutput`. `engine/EngineOutput.java`.
- **`EngineState`** — `(lastSequenceNumber, lastOrderId, lastTradePrice, orders)`: what a snapshot stores for the engine; `MatchingEngine.exportState()`/`restore(...)` (D71). `engine/EngineState.java:14`, `engine/MatchingEngine.java:83-109`.
- **`Exchange`** — the single-threaded live path, poison-pill policy, and the `quarantine`/`compact` tools. `core/Exchange.java:58`.
- **`ExchangeException`** — refusal reasons `BUSY`, `STOPPED`, `HALTED`, `INTERNAL_ERROR`. `core/ExchangeException.java`.
- **`ExchangeService` / `exchange-writer`** — owns the writer thread and the queue. `core/ExchangeService.java:50`, thread created at `:119`.
- **Execution (passive fill)** — a fill on a resting order, addressed to its owner; sent as a `MakerFill` private event, `FILL … liquidity=MAKER`. `engine/Execution.java:11-12`.

## F

- **Fail-stop** — a failed write/fsync refuses every later append; the writer halts; `Main` exits 2. `journal/CommandJournal.java:143-166`.
- **Fill** — (1) `book.Fill`, the book's record of what `fillBest` did; (2) the `FILL` wire line (TAKER or MAKER). Compare **Trade** and **Execution**.
- **`fillBest`** — fill the head of the best level, remove an exhausted order and an emptied level (D44). `book/OrderBook.java:78-95`.
- **Fillability** — the engine's read-only walk of crossing resting orders, stopping at the participant's own order; used by FOK and by the caps (D67, D68). `engine/MatchingEngine.java:299-315`.
- **FOK (fill-or-kill)** — fill completely now or do nothing; CANCELLED with `SELF_TRADE_PREVENTED` if its own order is what blocks it. `engine/MatchingEngine.java:202-208`.
- **Format version** — `JournalCodec.FORMAT_VERSION` = 3 (CRCs, base sequence, new records); v2 journals are refused. `journal/JournalCodec.java:41`. Snapshots have their own (`core/Snapshot.java:44`).
- **fsync / `force(true)`** — force bytes to disk; ~1.5 ms on the dev disk. `journal/CommandJournal.java:143-166`.
- **Future (caller's)** — each future-variant call gets its own `CompletableFuture`, completed on the common pool (`handOff`, D61). `core/ExchangeService.java:170-180`.

## G

- **Gate / Hold** — test helpers that hold the writer: `Gate` (a `CommandInterceptor` in `ExchangeServiceTest`) and `Hold` (an `OutputListener` in `TcpGatewayTest`).
- **Gateway** — TCP/TLS front end: `TcpGateway`, `ClientSession`, `Protocol`, `Credentials`, `Tls`. `gateway/`.
- **Group commit / linger** — wait up to `lingerNanos` for more requests; kept only for benchmark comparison (default off, D52). `core/ExchangeService.java:326-340`.

## H

- **Halt / HALTED** — unrecoverable writer failure; pending requests refused `HALTED`; `Main` exits 2. `core/ExchangeService.java:342-360`.
- **`handOff`** — see Future (caller's).
- **Happens-before** — the Java Memory Model's visibility guarantee (queue put → take, future complete → get, `Thread.start`/`join`). `core/Pending.java:12-13`.
- **Header** — `HEADER,3,<rules>,<base>,<crc>`; checked before anything is changed (D46, D64). `journal/JournalCodec.java:58-85`, `journal/CommandJournal.java:241-261`.
- **`HELD`** — the in-JVM set of held lock files that refuses a second open in the same process before the lock file is touched (D65). `journal/JournalLock.java:27`.

## I

- **Id map** — `orders: orderId → OrderNode`; contains an id iff the order rests. `book/OrderBook.java:43`.
- **Idle timeout** — a logged-in session that sends nothing for 300 s gets `ERROR idle timeout; disconnecting` (socket read timeout, D75). `gateway/ClientSession.java:152-183`.
- **In-flight count / limit (SESSION_BUSY)** — requests a session handed in whose callback has not run; above `maxInFlight` (1,000) new requests get `ERROR <ctx> SESSION_BUSY` (D75); a stopping session closes when it reaches 0 (D76). `gateway/ClientSession.java:279-291`, `:421-443`.
- **Invariant** — a property that must always hold. Spec §2; `book/OrderBook.java:25-28`, `book/PriceLevel.java:20-26`.
- **IOC** — match what crosses now, cancel the remainder. `domain/OrderType.java:6`.

## J

- **JMH** — benchmarks in `src/test/java/exchangeserver/bench/`, `-Pbench`. `pom.xml:93-125`.
- **Journal (WAL)** — the text file of stamped commands, persisted before processing. `journal/JournalCodec.java:16-37`.
- **`JournalCodec`** — encodes/seals and unseals/decodes records; decoding rebuilds requests through `OrderRequest`. `journal/JournalCodec.java:39`.
- **`JournalLock`** — one owner per journal: an exclusive lock on the separate `<journal>.lock` file plus `HELD` (D65). Public so compaction can hold it while replacing the journal. `journal/JournalLock.java:25-84`.

## L

- **L2 market data** — per-price aggregate volume and order count: `LEVEL` lines, `LevelUpdate`. `book/LevelUpdate.java:10`.
- **Last trade price** — band/collar reference; 0 = no trade yet. `engine/MatchingEngine.java:61`.
- **Leaves** — quantity still open after a fill. `book/Fill.java:12`; counted down for taker lines in `gateway/Protocol.java:145-166`.
- **`Ledger`** — what the commands produce, rebuilt together: engine + output digest + output log; replaced as a whole after a poison pill. `core/Ledger.java:22-74`.
- **Level (`PriceLevel`)** — orders at one price on one side, a doubly linked list with sentinels. `book/PriceLevel.java:28`.
- **LIMIT** — match what crosses now, rest the remainder (unless stopped by STP). `domain/OrderType.java:5`.
- **Listener (`OutputListener`)** — `onOutput(output, privateEvents)` on the writer thread, before the request's future completes (D76); the gateway routes private events and market data. Not called in replay. `core/OutputListener.java:19-23`, `gateway/TcpGateway.java:286-312`.
- **Lock file (`<journal>.lock`)** — see `JournalLock`; empty, stays on disk, git-ignored.
- **LOGIN** — `LOGIN <participant> <secret> [COD]`; one attempt per connection; `ERROR login failed` for any wrong part (D74). `gateway/Protocol.java:65-71`, `gateway/ClientSession.java:293-327`.
- **Login timeout** — a connection that does not log in within 10 s gets `ERROR login timeout; disconnecting` (D75).
- **Loopback bind** — the gateway binds 127.0.0.1 unless `--bind` says otherwise (D74). `gateway/TcpGateway.java:68-71`.

## M

- **Maker / passive / resting** — an order on the book; its fills are `MakerFill` events.
- **MARKET** — no price; trades inside the collar; remainder cancelled. `domain/OrderType.java:8`.
- **`MatchingEngine`** — price-time matching, risk checks, STP, outputs; deterministic. `engine/MatchingEngine.java:41`.
- **`MutableClock`** — settable test clock. `src/test/java/exchangeserver/testing/MutableClock.java`.
- **Mutation testing (PIT)** — survivors show untested behaviour. `pom.xml:126-169`; results in Spec §7.

## O

- **Open-order cap** — at most 10,000 resting orders per participant; checked only for an order that would rest (D68). `engine/MatchingEngine.java:193-200`.
- **`Order` / order id** — an accepted order (request + id + timestamp); order ids are issued by the sequencer, strictly increasing. `domain/Order.java:16`, `command/Sequencer.java:48-52`.
- **`OrderRequest`** — the only field-validation layer (D47). `domain/OrderRequest.java:15`.
- **`OrderStatus`** — `FILLED`, `RESTED`, `CANCELLED`, `REJECTED`. `engine/OrderStatus.java`.
- **oseq (output sequence number)** — the number of an event in a participant's private stream: 1, 2, 3, … per participant, derived from the commands so replay rebuilds it and snapshots keep the last one (D73). Printed as `oseq=` on FILL/ORDER/CANCELLED/CANCEL_REJECT and in `WELCOME`. `core/OutputLog.java:31-123`.
- **`OutputLog`** — numbers private events per participant and keeps the last `outputRetention` (1,000) for RESEND. `core/OutputLog.java:31`.

## P

- **Participant** — the authenticated identity owning orders; pattern `[A-Za-z0-9_]{1,16}`; must be in the users file (D74). `domain/Rules.java:28`.
- **PBKDF2** — PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 with a random 16-byte salt and 210,000 iterations by default: makes a stolen users file expensive to brute-force. `gateway/Credentials.java:36`, `:120-136`.
- **`Pending`** — a request in flight with its own future, its command, and the writer-owned `refused` flag. `core/Pending.java:16-21`.
- **Poison pill** — a journalled command that makes the engine throw; quarantined live, operator-quarantined at startup (D51).
- **POST_ONLY** — rests without matching; REJECTED if it would cross (equal price counts), or at a cap. `engine/MatchingEngine.java:209-213`.
- **Price-time priority** — best price first, then earliest arrival. `book/PriceLevel.java:64-88`.
- **Private event / private stream** — `PrivateEvent` (`OrderReply`, `CancelReply`, `MakerFill`): the numbered messages only one participant may see, sent to *every* session of that participant in oseq order (D73; replies used to go only to the requesting session). `core/PrivateEvent.java:12-32`.
- **`Protocol`** — parsing and line formatting; its header comment is the protocol documentation. `gateway/Protocol.java:16-45`.

## Q

- **Quarantine** — appending `SKIP <seq>` so replay passes over a poison pill. Live: `core/Exchange.java:278-289`; operator tool `--quarantine <seq>` (refuses a command already quarantined, D77): `core/Exchange.java:141-171`.
- **Queue** — the single `LinkedBlockingQueue<Pending<?>>` into the writer; capacity enforced under a lock (D56). `core/ExchangeService.java:101`.

## R

- **Rate limit (RATE_LIMITED)** — a token bucket per session: 1,000 requests/s, bursts of 2,000; beyond it `ERROR <ctx> RATE_LIMITED`, nothing queued (D75). `gateway/ClientSession.java:279-291`.
- **`Reason`** — `DUPLICATE_CLIENT_ORDER_ID`, `PRICE_OUTSIDE_BAND`, `MAX_OPEN_ORDERS`, `BOOK_FULL`, `POST_ONLY_WOULD_CROSS` (rejects, `isReject()`), and `SELF_TRADE_PREVENTED` (with CANCELLED). Replaced `RejectReason`. `engine/Reason.java:12-28`.
- **Recovery summary** — the line `Main` prints at start: `Recovered through sequence N [from snapshot B]: replayed R commands, cut T torn bytes[, quarantined …][, journal moved from rules V]` (D77). `Main.java:154-157`, `Exchange.Recovery` at `core/Exchange.java:78-80`.
- **Refused vs rejected** — refused: never processed (ERROR line or `ExchangeException`); rejected: processed and journalled, `status=REJECTED`.
- **Replay** — rebuild the ledger from the snapshot the header names plus the journal: verify, then feed and compare CHECKs (D60, D70, D71). `core/Replay.java:30-131`.
- **`ReplayException`** — replay failure at a known record: `ENGINE_FAILURE` or `DIVERGED`, with the sequence number, so `Main` gives fitting advice (D77). `core/ReplayException.java:12-37`.
- **`Request`** — sealed: `Submit`, `Cancel`, `CancelAll` (journalled) and `Snapshot`, `Attach`, `Resend` (reads). `core/Request.java:20-57`.
- **RESEND / RESENT / RESEND_GAP** — `RESEND <n>` asks for the retained private events from oseq n; they come back with ` resend=1`, then `RESENT last=<latest>`; if n is older than what is retained, `ERROR RESEND_GAP first=<oldest retained>` comes first (D73). `gateway/ClientSession.java:342-371`, `core/ResendResult.java:7`.
- **`Response`** — what a new order's submitter is told; invariants in its constructor (D22). `engine/Response.java:15-56`.
- **Retention** — `Exchange.Options.outputRetention` (1,000 per participant, `--resend-window`). `core/Exchange.java:64-75`.
- **Rules version** — `Rules.VERSION` = 2; in the header; records refuse other versions, an empty journal adopts. `domain/Rules.java:21`.

## S

- **Self-trade prevention (STP)** — the match loop stops when the next resting order to trade with is the incoming order's own participant's; the remainder is CANCELLED with `SELF_TRADE_PREVENTED`, whatever the type; a LIMIT does not rest (D67). `engine/MatchingEngine.java:224-227`, `:240-252`.
- **Sentinel** — dummy head/tail nodes of a level's list. `book/PriceLevel.java:31-32`.
- **Sequence number (seq)** — contiguous number of every journal record (NEW, CANCEL, CANCEL_ALL, SKIP, CHECK), from base + 1. Market-data `seq=` therefore has gaps. `command/Sequencer.java:13-14`.
- **`Sequencer`** — the only clock reader and counter. `command/Sequencer.java:20-94`.
- **SESSION_BUSY** — see In-flight limit.
- **Settings** — `ExchangeService.Settings` (`queueCapacity`, `maxBatchSize`, `lingerNanos`, `clock`, `Exchange.Options`) and `TcpGateway.Settings` (bind address, port, credentials, TLS, sessions, outbound, timeouts, in-flight, rate, burst). `core/ExchangeService.java:59-92`, `gateway/TcpGateway.java:52-98`.
- **Single writer** — exactly one thread touches sequencer, journal, ledger and book.
- **SKIP / `SkipCommand`** — `SKIP,<seq>,<ts>,<skippedSeq>`. `command/SkipCommand.java:14-25`.
- **Slow consumer** — a session whose 10,000-line outbound queue is full; disconnected (D54). `gateway/ClientSession.java:116-124`.
- **Smart batching** — take what is queued, one fsync; the default (D52).
- **Snapshot (two meanings)** — (1) the `Snapshot` *read* returning a `BookSnapshot` for SUBSCRIBE (≤ 100 levels/side, D62); (2) the persistent **snapshot file** `<journal>.snapshot-<seq>` written by compaction: last sequence, last order id, last timestamp, last trade price, digest, resting orders in book priority, each participant's last oseq, an `END` line, a CRC per line (D71). `core/Snapshot.java:21-137`.
- **States** — `RUNNING`, `STOPPING`, `STOPPED`, `HALTED`. `core/ExchangeService.java:52-57`.
- **Stop marker** — the private `Pending` `stopMarker`, enqueued by `stop()` under the lock. `core/ExchangeService.java:96`, `:245-258`.
- **Subscription** — a session receiving TRADE/LEVEL lines; guarded against SUBSCRIBE/UNSUBSCRIBE races. `gateway/ClientSession.java:373-411`.

## T

- **Taker** — see Aggressor; `FILL … liquidity=TAKER`.
- **TLS** — optional transport encryption (TLS 1.3/1.2) with a PKCS#12 keystore; password from `ES2_TLS_PASSWORD` (D74). `gateway/Tls.java:20-67`.
- **Token bucket** — the rate-limit algorithm: tokens refill continuously up to `burst`; each request takes one; none left means refused. `gateway/ClientSession.java:279-291`.
- **Torn tail** — bytes after the last `\n`; cut on open after the header check. `journal/CommandJournal.java:263-297`.
- **Touch** — the best bid and best ask; since D66 also the collar reference before the first trade.
- **Trade** — the public record of one match; price = the resting level's; timestamp = the incoming command's (D34). `engine/Trade.java:15-27`.

## U

- **`UNKNOWN_ORDER`** — cancel outcome for a missing, gone or not-owned order. `book/CancelStatus.java:3-12`.
- **Unlink** — O(1) removal of a node from its level. `book/PriceLevel.java:124-142`.

## W

- **WELCOME** — `WELCOME participant=<p> oseq=<latest>`: sent after the Attach read registered the session. `gateway/Protocol.java:140-142`.
- **`withinBand`** — exact integer band check. `domain/Rules.java:44-47`.
- **Write-ahead** — fsync a command before processing it. `core/Exchange.java:223`.

---

## Key takeaways

- Three "fill" words: **Trade** (public), **Execution** (maker's), **Fill** (book record / wire line).
- Two sequence numbers: **seq** (every journal record, global) and **oseq** (per participant, private stream).
- Two "snapshot"s: the queued read for SUBSCRIBE, and the persistent snapshot file from compaction.
- Three replay guards: rules version, format version, CHECK digests.
- Refusals come in levels: session (`RATE_LIMITED`, `SESSION_BUSY`), exchange (`BUSY`, `STOPPED`, `HALTED`), engine rejects (journalled `REJECTED`).
- "Cancelled" now has three sources: a client cancel, IOC/MARKET/FOK leftovers (sometimes `SELF_TRADE_PREVENTED`), and CANCEL_ALL (`reason=CANCEL_ALL`).

## Common confusions

- **"Passive fill" vs "partial fill"** — passive = the resting side; partial = not the whole quantity.
- **"Reply goes to the requesting session"** — no longer: replies are private events, sent to every session of the participant (D73). Refusals still go only to the requester.
- **"D63 closes after a barrier read"** — replaced by D76's in-flight count.
- **"Stopped" vs "halted"** — STOPPED exits 0 (verified on Linux for SIGINT and SIGTERM); HALTED exits 2.
- **"SELF_TRADE_PREVENTED is a reject"** — no: it goes with CANCELLED, and the order may have traded with others first.
- **"CHECK is a command the engine runs"** — no: replay compares it; the engine never sees it.

## Questions to test yourself

1. What is the difference between `seq` and `oseq`?
<details><summary>Answer</summary><code>seq</code> numbers every journal record exchange-wide (market data carries it, with gaps). <code>oseq</code> numbers one participant's private events 1, 2, 3, …, without gaps, rebuilt by replay; RESEND works on it.</details>

2. A client gets `ERROR clOrdId=c9 RATE_LIMITED`. Was anything journalled?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>No. Session limits are checked on the reader thread before the request reaches the queue (D75).</details>

3. Is a POST_ONLY order at the best ask's price crossing?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>Yes: <code>crosses</code> is inclusive, so it is REJECTED <code>POST_ONLY_WOULD_CROSS</code>.</details>

4. What does `ERROR RESEND_GAP first=3` tell the client?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>Events before oseq 3 are no longer retained (or are older than the last compaction); the lines that follow start at 3.</details>


---

<a id="guide-14-exercises"></a>

<!-- source: docs/understanding/14-exercises.md -->

# 14 — Exercises

> **Project:** ExchangeServer2 | Single-instrument limit order book: price-time matching engine, write-ahead journal with replay, single writer thread, TCP/TLS gateway with authenticated participants | Stack: Java 17, Maven wrapper, JUnit 5 (+ JMH, PIT)
> **This guide:** active learning from easy to hard: self-check questions, predict-then-verify runs, debugger traces, hands-on changes and break-it experiments | **Prerequisites:** 01, 05 (do the rest as each exercise's tags say) | **Related:** all guides, especially 05, 07, 10, 11, 12
> **Repo layout summary:** `pom.xml` + Maven wrapper (`mvnw`, `mvnw.cmd`) at the root; production code in `src/main/java/exchangeserver/` (packages `domain`, `book`, `engine`, `command`, `journal`, `core`, `gateway`, `client`, plus `Main`); tests mirror it in `src/test/java/exchangeserver/` (plus `bench/` JMH benchmarks and `testing/MutableClock`); design docs in `docs/` (`ExchangeServer2_Vision.md` = purpose/roadmap, `ExchangeServer2_Spec.md` = decisions D1–D77); these guides in `docs/understanding/`.

## Overview

Five kinds of exercise, easiest first, each tagged with the guides it reinforces, e.g. `[05, 07-engine]`. Answers are hidden in `<details>`: commit to an answer before you open one.

**Setup used throughout** (Windows; on macOS/Linux use `./mvnw` and `/`):

```
mvnw.cmd -q package -DskipTests
java -jar target/exchange-server2-2.0.0-SNAPSHOT.jar --users study.users --add-user alice     (repeat for bob, carol, dave)
java -jar target/exchange-server2-2.0.0-SNAPSHOT.jar --port 9000 --journal study.journal --users study.users
java -cp target/exchange-server2-2.0.0-SNAPSHOT.jar exchangeserver.client.ClientMain localhost 9000
```

`--add-user` asks for the secret twice on a terminal, or reads one line from standard input **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/Main.java:212-235`). Below, `<secret>` means the secret you chose for that participant. `ClientMain` sends what you type and prints server lines with `< ` **[Verified]** (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/client/ClientMain.java`). Delete `study.journal*` to start fresh (git-ignored). Stop with Ctrl+C: exit code 0 **[Verified on Linux]** (SIGINT and SIGTERM, Spec §11).

For code changes: a throwaway branch (`git switch -c study/<name>`); after swapping files around build with `mvnw.cmd clean …` (incremental compilation goes by timestamp, `CLAUDE.md`). Never run two Maven builds at once.

---

## (a) Self-check questions

1. `[01, 05]` In what order does the writer thread do these for a batch: stamp, fsync, engine, listener, future?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>A CHECK if due, then stamp every request → append all with one fsync → for each: ledger (engine, digest, private events) → OutputListener → complete the future/callback (D76). <code>src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Exchange.java:198-253</code>.</details>

2. `[07-domain-and-commands]` Which single class reads the clock? What happens if the clock steps back?
<details><summary>Answer</summary><code>command.Sequencer</code>; it reuses the previous timestamp, so journal timestamps never decrease (D33). <code>src/main/java/exchangeserver/command/Sequencer.java:86-93</code>.</details>

3. `[07-book]` Why does a `PriceLevel` need sentinel nodes?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>Every real node always has non-null <code>prev</code>/<code>next</code>, so append and unlink need no edge-case branches.</details>

4. `[07-engine, 06]` A BUY LIMIT 4 @ 101 from bob hits alice's ask of 10 @ 100. Price, and who gets which lines?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>At 100 (the resting price). bob's private stream: <code>FILL oseq=… side=BUY price=100 qty=4 leaves=0 liquidity=TAKER</code>, then <code>ORDER oseq=… status=FILLED</code>; alice's stream: <code>FILL oseq=… leaves=6 liquidity=MAKER</code> — to every session of each participant (D73); subscribers: <code>TRADE</code> and <code>LEVEL … qty=6</code>.</details>

5. `[07-engine, 09]` Why is a cancel of someone else's order answered `UNKNOWN_ORDER`?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>D48: a distinct status would let any participant probe which order ids are live.</details>

6. `[07-journal]` What does `CommandJournal.open` do, in order?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>Take the <code>JournalLock</code> (<code>&lt;journal&gt;.lock</code>, D65) → check the header (refuse a foreign file or other rules with records; let an empty journal adopt new rules, D64, D71) → open the journal channel → cut a torn tail → write a header if empty and fsync the directory (D69). <code>src/main/java/exchangeserver/journal/CommandJournal.java:75-128</code>.</details>

7. `[07-exchange-service, 08]` Which refusals mean "this request may be in the journal"?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>BUSY, STOPPED, RATE_LIMITED, SESSION_BUSY: never journalled. HALTED: maybe (resend with the same client order id, or RESEND the private stream after restart). INTERNAL_ERROR: journalled, then quarantined.</details>

8. `[07-exchange-and-replay]` Where does replay start, and what does it compare along the way?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>From the snapshot the header's base names (or empty), then the journal: pass 1 verifies sequence from base + 1 and timestamps, pass 2 feeds the ledger and compares every CHECK digest (D70, D71). <code>src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Replay.java:39-99</code>.</details>

9. `[07-gateway]` Name the three per-session limits and what each answers.
<details><summary>Answer</summary>Login/idle timeouts (<code>ERROR login timeout; disconnecting</code> / <code>idle timeout</code>), in-flight cap (<code>SESSION_BUSY</code>), rate limit (<code>RATE_LIMITED</code>) — D75, <code>src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/ClientSession.java:279-291</code>.</details>

10. `[07-gateway, 09]` Why does a session's request callback run *after* the listener?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>So when the last callback brings the in-flight count to 0 and the session closes, the request's own lines were already offered (D76). The old D63 barrier read was refused during shutdown and lost replies.</details>

---

## (b) Predict, then verify

Write your prediction down, run it, then open the answer.

### b1. The README session `[05, 06, 07-gateway]`
Fresh journal. **alice**: `LOGIN alice <secret>`, `NEW a1 SELL LIMIT 10 100`, `SUBSCRIBE`. **bob**: `LOGIN bob <secret>`, `NEW b1 BUY LIMIT 4 101`. Predict every line.
<details><summary>Answer</summary>

```
alice < WELCOME participant=alice oseq=0
alice < ORDER oseq=1 orderId=1 clOrdId=a1 status=RESTED filled=0 remaining=10
alice < SUBSCRIBED
alice < BOOK seq=1 last=0 bids=- asks=100x10
bob   < WELCOME participant=bob oseq=0
bob   < FILL oseq=1 orderId=2 clOrdId=b1 side=BUY price=100 qty=4 leaves=0 liquidity=TAKER
bob   < ORDER oseq=1 orderId=2 clOrdId=b1 status=FILLED filled=4 remaining=0
alice < FILL oseq=2 orderId=1 clOrdId=a1 side=SELL price=100 qty=4 leaves=6 liquidity=MAKER
alice < TRADE seq=2 price=100 qty=4 aggressor=BUY
alice < LEVEL seq=2 side=SELL price=100 qty=6 orders=1
```
<strong>[Verified]</strong> (<code>TcpGatewayTest.Trading.restThenCrossFillsBothSides</code>, <code>MarketData.snapshotThenIncrementalUpdates</code>; a live run on 2026-10-27 printed the same lines). Each participant's oseq starts at 1.</details>

### b2. Wrong secret `[07-gateway, 09 D74]`
New window: `LOGIN alice guessed-secret`. Then try `PING`.
<details><summary>Answer</summary><code>ERROR login failed</code>, and the connection closes: one attempt per connection, and the same answer whether the name or the secret was wrong <strong>[Verified]</strong> (live run; <code>TcpGatewayTest.Security</code>).</details>

### b3. Duplicate, band, protocol error `[07-engine, 07-gateway]`
Continue as bob: `NEW b1 BUY LIMIT 4 101`, `NEW b2 BUY LIMIT 1 200`, `NEW b3 BUY MARKET 1 100`.
<details><summary>Answer</summary>

```
ORDER oseq=2 orderId=2 clOrdId=b1 status=REJECTED filled=0 remaining=4 reason=DUPLICATE_CLIENT_ORDER_ID
ORDER oseq=3 orderId=4 clOrdId=b2 status=REJECTED filled=0 remaining=1 reason=PRICE_OUTSIDE_BAND
ERROR NEW takes 4 field(s), got 5
```
The duplicate reports the original id 2 but used up id 3. 200 is outside ±10% of 100. A MARKET order has no price field: refused at the gateway, never journalled. <strong>[Inferred from code]</strong> (the same session without oseq was run live before D73).</details>

### b4. Cancels and the journal `[05 flow 2, 07-journal]`
bob: `CANCEL 1`. alice: `CANCEL 1`. Then open `study.journal`.
<details><summary>Answer</summary>

```
bob   < CANCEL_REJECT oseq=4 orderId=1 reason=UNKNOWN_ORDER
alice < CANCELLED oseq=3 orderId=1 qty=6
alice < LEVEL seq=6 side=SELL price=100 qty=0 orders=0
```
Journal: <code>HEADER,3,2,0,&lt;crc&gt;</code>, NEW 1–4 (both rejects too, D13), CANCEL 5 and 6 — every line ending in <code>,&lt;8 hex digits&gt;</code> (D69). bob's rejected cancel consumed his oseq 4.</details>

### b5. Self-trade prevention `[07-engine, 09 D67]`
Fresh journal. x sells 2 @ 100 (`NEW x1 SELL LIMIT 2 100`), alice sells 3 @ 100, y sells 5 @ 100 (use three participants). alice: `NEW a9 BUY LIMIT 9 100`. Then a second case: x sells 2, y sells 3, alice sells 5 (alice last); alice buys 4 @ 100.
<details><summary>Answer</summary>Case 1: alice trades 2 with x, then the next order in time priority is her own: matching stops; <code>ORDER … status=CANCELLED filled=2 remaining=7 reason=SELF_TRADE_PREVENTED</code>; y's order is never reached (STP stops, it does not skip). Case 2: she takes 2 from x and 2 from y and never reaches her own: <code>FILLED</code>. <strong>[Verified]</strong> (<code>MatchingEngineTest.SelfTrade.tradesWithOthersAheadThenStopsAtItsOwnOrder</code>; case 2 by the same code path).</details>

### b6. QUIT right after NEW `[07-gateway, 09 D76]`
In one paste: `LOGIN dave <secret>`, `NEW d1 BUY LIMIT 1 95`, `NEW d2 BUY LIMIT 1 96`, `QUIT`.
<details><summary>Answer</summary>

```
< WELCOME participant=dave oseq=0
< ORDER oseq=1 orderId=… clOrdId=d1 status=RESTED …
< ORDER oseq=2 orderId=… clOrdId=d2 status=RESTED …
< BYE
```
The session counts the two orders in flight and sends BYE when the last callback ran (D76). <strong>[Verified]</strong> (<code>TcpGatewayTest.Session.quitAnswersEveryEarlierRequestBeforeBye</code>; live before oseq existed).</details>

### b7. RESEND after a disconnect `[07-gateway, 09 D73]`
alice (logged in with `COD`): `NEW a1 SELL LIMIT 10 100`, `NEW a2 BUY LIMIT 5 100`; bob buys 4 @ 100; alice `QUIT`. Then a new alice window: `LOGIN alice <secret>`, `RESEND 1`.
<details><summary>Answer</summary>

```
alice2 < WELCOME participant=alice oseq=4
alice2 < ORDER oseq=1 orderId=1 clOrdId=a1 status=RESTED filled=0 remaining=10 resend=1
alice2 < ORDER oseq=2 orderId=2 clOrdId=a2 status=CANCELLED filled=0 remaining=5 reason=SELF_TRADE_PREVENTED resend=1
alice2 < FILL oseq=3 orderId=1 clOrdId=a1 side=SELL price=100 qty=4 leaves=6 liquidity=MAKER resend=1
alice2 < CANCELLED oseq=4 orderId=1 qty=6 reason=CANCEL_ALL resend=1
alice2 < RESENT last=4
```
a2 would have crossed her own ask (STP). On QUIT her last COD session closed, so the gateway submitted CANCEL_ALL (event 4). <strong>[Verified]</strong> (live run, 2026-10-27).</details>

### b8. Crash and replay `[05 flow 4, 07-journal]`
After b4, kill the server hard (`taskkill /F` or close its window). Append half a record: `[IO.File]::AppendAllText("study.journal", "NEW,7,2026-10-27T12:", (New-Object Text.UTF8Encoding($false)))`. Restart. Predict the startup lines; then carol: `SUBSCRIBE`, `NEW c1 BUY LIMIT 5 99`.
<details><summary>Answer</summary>

```
ExchangeServer2 listening on 127.0.0.1:9000, journal …\study.journal
Recovered through sequence 6: replayed 6 commands, cut 20 torn bytes
< BOOK seq=6 last=100 bids=- asks=-
< ORDER oseq=1 orderId=5 clOrdId=c1 status=RESTED filled=0 remaining=5
< LEVEL seq=7 side=BUY price=99 qty=5 orders=1
```
The torn bytes are cut and now reported (D77). <strong>[Inferred]</strong> from code (the same steps were run live before D77 added the summary line).</details>

### b9. Editing the header by hand `[07-journal, 09 D69]`
Copy the journal and change the header's rules version from 2 to 9 in a text editor that keeps LF. Start on the copy.
<details><summary>Answer</summary><code>Startup failed: Bad journal header "HEADER,3,9,0,…": checksum mismatch (expected …, found …)</code> and <code>The journal was not changed.</code>, exit 1. The CRC covers the header too, so a hand edit is caught before the version check. (To really test a version mismatch, use <code>CommandJournal.writeFresh</code> with another version, as <code>CommandJournalTest</code> does.) <strong>[Inferred from code]</strong>, <code>src/main/java/exchangeserver/journal/JournalCodec.java:63-85</code>, <code>:171-187</code>.</details>

### b10. Tools against a running server `[07-exchange-and-replay, 09 D65]`
With the server running: `--journal study.journal --quarantine 2`, then `--compact`.
<details><summary>Answer</summary><code>Quarantine failed: Journal … is already open (a running exchange, or a --quarantine or --compact run)</code> and the same for <code>Compaction failed:</code>, exit 1, nothing written. <strong>[Verified]</strong> live on Windows and (quarantine) on Linux.</details>

### b11. Compaction and restart `[05 flow 4, 09 D71]`
Stop the server, run `--journal study.journal --compact`, list the files, start again.
<details><summary>Answer</summary><code>Compacted through sequence N: K resting orders in study.journal.snapshot-N; old journal archived as study.journal.archive-1-N</code>; files: <code>study.journal</code> (one header line, base N), the archive, the snapshot, the lock file. Restart prints <code>Recovered through sequence N from snapshot N: replayed 0 commands, cut 0 torn bytes</code>. A second <code>--compact</code> says nothing to compact. <strong>[Verified]</strong> (live run with N = 4).</details>

### b12. A JUnit prediction: the collar `[07-engine]`
Add temporarily to `MatchingEngineTest` and predict status, filled and the book:

```java
@Test
void studyCollarBeforeFirstTrade() {
    limit("s", SELL, 100, 1);
    limit("s", SELL, 110, 1);
    limit("s", SELL, 500, 1);
    System.out.println(market("b", BUY, 3));
}
```
Run `mvnw.cmd test -Dtest=MatchingEngineTest#studyCollarBeforeFirstTrade`.
<details><summary>Answer</summary>No trade yet, so the collar is anchored on the touch (100): buys up to 110. It takes 100 and 110, then stops: <code>CANCELLED filled=2 remaining=1</code>; 500 stays. Before D66 it swept all three. <strong>[Verified]</strong> (<code>MatchingEngineTest.Market.beforeTheFirstTradeTheCollarIsAnchoredOnTheTouch</code>).</details>

## (c) Tracing with a debugger

IntelliJ (File › Open › `pom.xml`) or VS Code with the Java extension. Threads you will see: `exchange-writer`, `gateway-acceptor`, `session-N-reader`, `session-N-sender`, `ForkJoinPool.commonPool-worker-*`, `shutdown`.

c1. `[05 flow 1, 08]` **A NEW order end to end.** Breakpoints: `ClientSession.newOrder` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/ClientSession.java:329`), `ExchangeService.enqueue` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/ExchangeService.java:211`), `Exchange.process` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Exchange.java:198`), `CommandJournal.appendAll` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/journal/CommandJournal.java:143`), `MatchingEngine.process` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/engine/MatchingEngine.java:178`), `TcpGateway.onOutput` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/TcpGateway.java:286`). Send one crossing NEW. Which thread hits each? What is in `events` at `onOutput`?
<details><summary>What you should see</summary>The first two on <code>session-N-reader</code>, the rest on <code>exchange-writer</code>. <code>appendAll</code> returns before the engine runs. <code>events</code> holds an <code>OrderReply</code> for the taker and a <code>MakerFill</code> per passive fill, each with its participant's next oseq.</details>

c2. `[07-book]` **Watch a level empty.** Breakpoint in `OrderBook.removeNode` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/book/OrderBook.java:111`). Rest one sell, take it fully; step through `unlink` and the empty-level removal.

c3. `[07-exchange-and-replay]` **A poison pill.** Debug `ExchangeTest$PoisonPill.poisonInTheMiddleOfABatchDoesNotDisturbTheOthers`; breakpoint in the private `Exchange.quarantine` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Exchange.java:278`).
<details><summary>What you should see</summary>Poison seq 2, SKIP stamped as seq 4, <code>skipped = {2}</code>; <code>Replay.rebuild</code> builds a new ledger from the snapshot (none here) plus seq 1; the batch continues with seq 3.</details>

c4. `[07-exchange-service]` **Stop draining.** Debug `ExchangeServiceTest$Stop.drainsEverythingAcceptedAndRefusesTheRest`; breakpoints at `stop` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/ExchangeService.java:245`) and `stopping = batch.remove(stopMarker)` (`:298`).

c5. `[07-gateway, 09 D76]` **A draining session.** Breakpoint in `ClientSession.finishIfIdle` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/gateway/ClientSession.java:434`). Paste `NEW …` and `QUIT` together. Watch `readerDone`, `inFlight` and which thread finally passes the `compareAndSet`.
<details><summary>What you should see</summary>The reader calls it first with <code>inFlight</code> 1 (nothing happens); the writer thread's callback calls it with 0 and closes, after the ORDER line was offered.</details>

c6. `[07-ledger-and-snapshots]` **Replay compares a CHECK.** Debug `ExchangeTest$Checkpoints.aCheckIsWrittenEveryIntervalAndReplayAcceptsIt` with a breakpoint in `Replay.feed` at the CHECK branch (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Replay.java:80-88`). Compare `check.digest()` with `ledger.digest()`.

## (d) Small hands-on changes

Each on its own branch. "Done": new tests pass, `mvnw.cmd test` green, no compiler warnings.

d1. `[12 Recipe 5, 07-gateway]` **Add `TIME` → `TIME seq=<last seq>`** through the queue (snapshot depth 1, callback via `tracked`). Done: `ProtocolTest` parse/format and a `TcpGatewayTest` case.

d2. `[12 Recipe 8, 07-client-and-main]` **Add `--queue-capacity <n>`** to `Main`. Done: bad values print the usage line and exit 1; README and `CLAUDE.md` updated.

d3. `[12 Recipe 3]` **Change the band to 5%.** Bump `Rules.VERSION`, fix the tests that encode 10%. Then carry a journal across: compact with the old jar, start the new one. Done: green, and `Recovered … journal moved from rules 2` is printed.

d4. `[07-ledger-and-snapshots]` **See CHECK records.** Start with `--check-interval 2`, place five orders, read the journal. Done: you can say why each CHECK has the sequence number it has.

d5. `[12 Recipe 2]` **New reject `QUANTITY_ABOVE_BOOK`** for an IOC larger than the whole opposite side. Done: journalled and replayed (`ExchangeTest`), `Rules.VERSION` bumped, PIT shows no survivor in the new branch.

d6. `[12 Recipe 2, 09 D67]` **Change STP to "cancel the resting order"** (cancel own resting orders it meets and keep matching). Done: `MatchingEngineTest.SelfTrade` rewritten for the new policy, a D78 written, `Rules.VERSION` bumped.

d7. `[04, 12 Recipe 13]` **Run the suite on Linux** with the Docker command in guide 12 Recipe 13. Done: 446-plus tests green in the container.

## (e) "Break it" experiments

Predict what breaks and which test catches it; change it on a branch; run `mvnw.cmd clean test`; revert.

e1. `[07-exchange-and-replay, 09 D40]` Move `journal.appendAll(commands)` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Exchange.java:223`) after the processing loop.
<details><summary>Answer</summary>Replies become visible before they are durable; <code>ExchangeTest$LivePath.visibleImpliesDurable</code> is the guard; poison-pill tests break because the SKIP would precede its target.</details>

e2. `[07-exchange-and-replay, 09 D61]` Delete `if (p.refused) continue;` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Exchange.java:228-230`).
<details><summary>Answer</summary>A refused request has <code>p.command == null</code>, is treated as a read, and <code>read(...)</code> throws "Not a read" out of <code>process</code> — the writer halts. <code>ExchangeTest$LivePath.invalidCancelIsRefusedWithoutUsingASequenceNumber</code> fails. <strong>[Inferred]</strong></details>

e3. `[07-engine, 09 D12]` Stamp trades with `Instant.now()` instead of `order.getTimestamp()` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/engine/MatchingEngine.java:234`).
<details><summary>Answer</summary><code>MatchingEngineTest$Outputs.tradeTimestampIsTheIncomingCommandsTimestamp</code> fails; determinism tests usually fail. Trade timestamps are not in the digest, so replay would not stop — another reason the rule matters.</details>

e4. `[07-journal, 08]` Delete `channel.force(true)` in `appendAll` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/journal/CommandJournal.java:160`).
<details><summary>Answer</summary>No test fails: an fsync is not observable without pulling the power (an expected PIT survivor, Spec §7).</details>

e5. `[07-domain-and-commands, 09 D70]` Change `PRICE_BAND_PERCENT` to 5 **without** bumping `VERSION`, and restart on a journal with an order at +8% of the last trade, started with `--check-interval 1`.
<details><summary>Answer</summary>The replayed order is now rejected instead of resting, so the next CHECK's digest differs: startup fails with <code>Replay diverged before CHECK …</code> and the compaction advice. With the default interval (10,000) a small journal has no CHECK yet, and the change goes unnoticed — the digest only catches what a CHECK covers.</details>

e6. `[07-journal, 09 D69]` Convert a journal to CRLF (`(Get-Content j) | Set-Content j2 -Encoding ascii` in Windows PowerShell) and start on it.
<details><summary>Answer</summary>Startup fails with <code>record ends with '\r': the file was converted to CRLF line endings; convert it back to LF (e.g. dos2unix) and restart</code>, exit 1. <strong>[Verified]</strong> (<code>JournalCodecTest.Corrupt.aCarriageReturnIsReportedAsALineEndingConversion</code>; a test that wrote a journal with <code>Files.write</code> hit exactly this on Windows).</details>

e7. `[07-exchange-and-replay, 09 D76]` In `Exchange.process`, swap the listener call and `p.complete(...)` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Exchange.java:246-251`).
<details><summary>Answer</summary><code>ExchangeServiceTest$HandIn.listenerRunsOnTheWriterBeforeTheCallback</code> fails. In the gateway, the last request's callback can close a QUITting session before its ORDER line is offered, so <code>TcpGatewayTest$Session.quitAnswersEveryEarlierRequestBeforeBye</code> and <code>endOfStreamStillGetsTheReplies</code> lose lines. <strong>[Inferred]</strong> for the gateway tests.</details>

e8. `[07-journal, 09 D65]` Remove the `JournalLock.acquire` in `CommandJournal.open`.
<details><summary>Answer</summary><code>CommandJournalTest$Exclusive</code> (both tests, including the child-JVM one) and <code>ExchangeTest$PoisonPill.quarantineToolRefusesWhileTheExchangeIsRunning</code> fail.</details>

e9. `[07-journal, 09 D65]` **The POSIX trap.** Make `JournalLock` lock the journal file itself and run `CommandJournalTest` on Windows and on Linux.
<details><summary>Answer</summary><strong>[Inferred, not run]</strong> Windows: likely green (locks belong to the handle) unless the mandatory lock blocks <code>read()</code>. Linux: <code>anotherProcessIsRefusedWhileTheJournalIsOpen</code> should fail, because closing the handle <code>read()</code> used drops the process's POSIX lock. The current design passes on both (Spec §11).</details>

e10. `[07-engine, 09 D67]` Remove the STP check in the match loop (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/engine/MatchingEngine.java:224-227`).
<details><summary>Answer</summary><code>MatchingEngineTest$SelfTrade</code> tests that expect CANCELLED/<code>SELF_TRADE_PREVENTED</code> after matching fail (the order now trades with itself), and <code>TcpGatewayTest$Trading.selfTradeIsPreventedOverTheWire</code> fails. The FOK tests still pass: <code>fillability</code> stops at the own order on its own. <strong>[Inferred]</strong></details>

e11. `[07-ledger-and-snapshots, 09 D70]` Remove the CHECK comparison in `Replay.feed` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/core/Replay.java:80-88`; keep the `return`).
<details><summary>Answer</summary><code>ReplayTest$Check.aDifferentDigestStopsReplayAsDivergence</code>, <code>ExchangeTest$Checkpoints.aChangedHistoryIsCaughtAtTheNextCheck</code> and <code>MainTest.aReplayDivergenceGetsTheCompactionAdvice</code> fail.</details>

e12. `[07-journal, 08]` Remove every call to `Durable.fsyncDirectory`.
<details><summary>Answer</summary>No test fails. A lost directory entry only shows after a crash on Linux right after a file was created or renamed; on Windows the call is a no-op. PIT lists these as expected survivors (Spec §7).</details>

e13. `[04, 09 D69]` Remove the `*.journal -text` lines from `.gitattributes`, set `core.autocrlf=true`, force-add a journal (`git add -f`, it is git-ignored), commit, delete it and check it out again; start on it.
<details><summary>Answer</summary><strong>[Inferred]</strong> The checkout converts LF to CRLF; startup fails with the CRLF message of e6. With <code>-text</code> git leaves the bytes alone.</details>

e14. `[07-book]` In `OrderBook.add`, put the new level into the TreeMap *before* `level.addOrder(order)` (`src/main/java/exchangeserver/book/OrderBook.java:66-69`).
<details><summary>Answer</summary><strong>[Inferred]</strong> Probably no test fails: <code>addOrder</code> cannot throw on a fresh level today; the ordering is defence in depth.</details>

---

## Key takeaways

- Predict before you run: every answer follows from the paths in guide 05.
- Two sequences to keep apart: `seq` (journal, global) and `oseq` (per participant private stream).
- Some guarantees have no failing test (fsync, directory fsync, data races); some are covered only partly (CHECK every 10,000 commands).
- The live server plus `ClientMain` is the fastest feedback loop; `--check-interval` and `--compact` make the recovery features visible.

## Common confusions

- **"The duplicate's orderId=2 means no id was used."** Id 3 was used.
- **"A protocol ERROR or RATE_LIMITED is a reject."** Neither reaches the journal; a reject does.
- **"My reply went to the session that sent the order only."** It goes to every session of the participant (D73).
- **"If tests pass, concurrency is right."** No: happens-before arguments, not tests.

## Questions to test yourself

1. In b7, why does `WELCOME` say `oseq=4` though alice never saw event 4?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>The CANCEL_ALL ran after her last session closed; its cancel reply was numbered and retained in her stream. WELCOME reports the latest number; RESEND fetches what she missed.</details>

2. Which break-it experiments show a mistake no test catches?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>e4 and e12 (durability), e9 on Windows, e14, and e5 when no CHECK covers the changed command.</details>

3. In c1, why does `appendAll` return before `MatchingEngine.process` is entered?
<details><summary>Answer</summary>Write-ahead: the whole batch is appended and fsynced first.</details>
